# Q1 adversarial review: one planned switch per ministry (Option A)

Phase 30 Imports & Migration Tools. Discovery record, 2026-11-01. Not a spec. No tracker writes.

**Inputs.** Founder answer to Q1 (Option A, plus the request to fully research what must be brought over from SiteStacker, the previous-payment-processor handover with or without Stripe, and edge cases). Research tracks `research/q01-r1` to `q01-r6` (each independently verified), six adversarial reviews `review/q01-v1` to `q01-v6` covering all 22 categories, a skeptic pass on every review, and a synthesis. Evidence baseline: `develop` `629cb71dc`; Phase 29 spec PR #2430 head `1483a349` (open, unmerged).

**Skeptic outcome.** 124 concerns raised: upheld 49, narrowed 68, duplicate 6, rejected 1. Rejected and duplicate concerns do not drive amendments; narrowed concerns are used in their narrowed form.

**Evidence labels.** verified-vendor-doc (URL, accessed 2026-11-01), snippet-only, third-party, repo-ratified, repo-published, repo-integrated, inferred, assumption, unverified.

## 1. Disposition

**Accept with required amendments.**

Option A is the right model for the first release that offers a SiteStacker switch, and it fits governing text. It is the only option with a single writer for supporter data. roadmap.md:4395 forbids promising permanent dual writers, which rules out C. B needs per-family live authority and identities that Asym creates but SiteStacker must reference, and no owner text defines either. No reviewer argued for B or C, so the base model stands (repo-published / repo-ratified, verified on develop 629cb71dc). The decision cannot be recorded as worded, for four reasons. (1) 'Asym becomes the only place for supporters and their history' is false while SiteStacker's scheduler, PayPal or a gateway schedule still charges legacy recurring gifts. P13 PRD :735-741 says 'observation is not control' and forbids silently creating a gap. P16 :1314 keeps the old executor external and read-only until a formal cutover (repo-ratified, verified). (2) Every refinement is too narrow or rests on the wrong source. Same-snapshot replay must keep published convergence (roadmap.md:4363). A gifts-only catch-up sourced from SiteStacker misses refunds, returns, disputes, stop evidence and money that PayPal or a gateway runs, and 'catch-up' is a term Phase 16 avoids (CONTEXT.md:3101, :3111, :3121, :3136). A three-item 'off' attestation would switch off the receipts, reminders and declined-payment path that SiteStacker-charged gifts still need (SiteStacker 151000122015, 151000183830, verified-vendor-doc 2026-11-01), and it cannot reach missionary-owned Mailchimp lists (151000122392). 'Counts and per-currency totals' narrows the published partition of Legal Entity × original currency × source basis (roadmap.md:4369; owner-constraints.md:433-437). (3) The freeze and reconcile steps sit on duties that Phase 13 gives to an unowned 'separate migration workstream' (P13 :743-746, :2192-2199). C3 forbids Phase 30 taking them over by shorthand. (4) The decision says nothing about giving continuity during the switch window, post-go-live reversal, or ministries whose out-of-scope modules (child sponsorship) keep writing. Several amendments change cutover, reversal, financial treatment, consent or source coverage, so they go back to the founder before they count as settled. In two places external practice conflicts with the repo, and the repo governs. First, processors and giving platforms often move recurring donors by token migration with no donor action (q01-r3 §4, third-party and vendor docs), but P16 :1236 and :1314 require a formal cutover with applicable fresh authorization; the founder may ask the Phase 16 owner to consider a narrow amendment, and Phase 30 cannot decide it. Second, vendors talk about a 'freeze', but ADR-0106:94-96 and ADR-0058:22-24 replace an unlockable freeze with an attested pause plus 'No known overlap in the inspected scope' and post-activation overlap monitoring.

## 2. Lead verification notes (checked directly by the session lead)

- **Confirmed in the repo:** P13 :735-741 ("observation is not control"; never silently create a gap, overlap, duplicate charge or fresh mandate) and :743-746 (the separate migration workstream owns processor coordination, credential-vault transfer, reauthorization campaigns, cutover freezes and bulk reconciliation; Asym never handles raw PAN, CVV/CVC or full bank credentials); P13 :659-667 (Stripe Connect direct charges onboarded through Account Links, "Do not use OAuth", a Settlement Account Binding replaces `tenants.stripe_secret_key`); P13 :2190-2200 (cross-processor migration workstream reserved as a seam); the P16 interaction table ("replace/bind method" is **Complete with donor now**) and P16 :1314; CONTEXT.md :3099-3102 and :3134-3137 ("catch-up" under _Avoid_); `packages/api/src/stripe/tenant-client.ts:64-66` (tenant secret key falls back to the platform `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`); `packages/api/src/donate/saga.ts:215-222`, `:300` (checkout reuses a stored `stripe_customer_id`).
- **Confirmed vendor facts (fetched 2026-11-01):** SiteStacker article 151000183512: "Site Stacker does not store payment methods and instead utilizes a token that references a payment method", with the payment method kept in the merchant processor's database. Article 151000122348: "If you are using SiteStacker for donor management, then the income data will populate in Historic Giving automatically", and ledger data "comes from accounting and it can be both negative or positive". Article 151000118930: payment processor gateways are "exclusively connected to a Site"; it does **not** say per currency or payment method, so that part of the synthesis is unverified. Article 151000122015 (titled "Payment Spring: Mastercard Regulation Changes"): a receipt "after every billing" with cancellation instructions, plus an online cancellation method. Stripe self-serve copy (docs.stripe.com/get-started/data-migrations/pan-copy-self-serve): copies cards, US ACH and SEPA payment methods; cannot copy Subscriptions, Invoices, Plans, Bacs or Canadian pre-authorized debits; "doesn't change customer IDs"; for a Connect account without Dashboard access the platform owner must initiate; partial customer selection is unavailable for Connect accounts.
- **Correction to the synthesis:** the synthesis says #2295 does not revoke authenticated INSERT/UPDATE on `crm_record_links`. The issue body indeed does not, but the Samson-approved spec r2 (first comment, 2026-10-24T22:21Z) adds `REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated` on the 12 staff and CRM-identity tables, including `crm_record_links` and `crm_merge_candidates`. It is pending Ready and unintegrated. Decision-log D9 and O7 therefore stay correct in substance; they are clarified to cite spec r2 instead of being replaced.

## 3. Corrected decision (to record after founder confirmation)

Q1 — How a ministry leaves SiteStacker (applies to the first release that offers a SiteStacker switch). DECISION: Option A, one planned switch per ministry (a 'SiteStacker Switch'). Option B (area by area) and Option C (side by side with refresh imports) are not offered. Any partial or side-by-side arrangement needs a new founder decision and owner amendments. 1. SCOPE AND ELIGIBILITY. One Asym tenant is fed by exactly one SiteStacker installation. Shared or multi-organization installations, and legacy merchant accounts serving another organization, are outside v1. Each legacy merchant account and Site is tied to a Legal Entity only by a reviewer-accepted assertion, never inferred from Site. A switch can be scheduled only when all of the following hold. (a) Every Minimum Switch Kit family is committable through its owner's typed import command (owner-constraints.md:389-392; roadmap.md:4367). [Founder to confirm the kit; recommended: Parties and households; contact points with consent and suppression evidence; one-time giving history including reversals; recurring evidence for the Phase 16 classifier.] (b) Every other source family is either committable or recorded as a ministry-accepted visible exclusion with a 'history not yet covered' status. An excluded family may be imported later only from the retained final manifest under the same Switch Boundary, and SiteStacker never stays a writer for it. (c) Every legacy recurring cohort has at least a Phase 16 external/read-only classification target. (d) The readiness census is complete. (e) [Founder to confirm] Any SiteStacker module that will keep writing to migrated people after the boundary (for example child sponsorship) is either a reason the ministry is ineligible, or a declared residual system with no sync, a stated count of overlapping people and the ministry's written acceptance. No exception lets a switch go ahead while a kit family is missing. 2. WHAT ASYM BECOMES. From the Switch Boundary, Asym is the system of record and the only writer for supporter identity, contact data, consent and suppression, relationships, custom data, imported giving history and all staff work. Asym does not control any legacy executor. Each legacy recurring arrangement (SiteStacker's own scheduler, PayPal subscriptions, any gateway-native schedule) stays Externally controlled/read-only under Phase 16 until its own Phase 16 formal cutover or a recorded end. The original processor stays authoritative for settlement, refunds, disputes and returns on charges it executed. A switch is 'live' when supporter authority has moved. It is 'closed' only when every legacy executor is proved stopped, formally cut over or ended, and the Legacy Recurring Tail is closed. Until closure the ministry keeps SiteStacker licensed and keeps its recurring engine and recurring donor messages running. 3. STEPS. (i) Readiness census, taken before the first practice run and again at the final export. It covers retained Asym state and SiteStacker's processors, merchant accounts, executors, automations and outward surfaces. (ii) Practice runs that write nothing to any target owner and cause no outward effect. They write only Phase 29 source custody and Phase 30 session, plan, staging and run records. (iii) Go/no-go with a declared maximum editing-pause length. If go-live criteria are not met by that deadline, the default is to abort before commit and resume SiteStacker. (iv) The recorded Switch Boundary: an exact UTC instant shown in the tenant giving time zone, by default the first moment of a civil date. It governs only Phase 30 classification and never sets or implies a Phase 16, Phase 20 Posting Ownership Cutover or Phase 21 Field Account Operational Cutover boundary. (v) The ministry pauses editing in SiteStacker and sets each legacy surface to its recorded state. (vi) One final Source Snapshot Manifest, which may hold several files, taken after the boundary. (vii) Import through owner commands, with no outward effect. (viii) Reconcile. (ix) Go-live. (x) Legacy Recurring Tail with Legacy Tail Backfills. (xi) Tail closure and a decommission checklist. 4. GIVING CONTINUITY. Donors are never silently left with a dead giving page. [Founder to choose; recommended (a).] (a) A short, declared online-giving pause, its length measured in rehearsal. Parties, contacts and suppression evidence commit and reconcile first. Asym giving and exact redirects then open, and the remaining families commit with no outward effect before the switch is declared live. (b) SiteStacker one-time giving stays on until Asym giving opens. Gifts in that gap arrive through the first Legacy Tail Backfill, and new givers wait in match review until staff create them through the Phase 4 command. Under either option, Asym checkout opens only after a verified Phase 13 Settlement Account Binding exists, and anyone who gives in Asym before the final import completes is resolved in match review with no automatic merge. 5. EXACT REPEAT IMPORT (a founder narrowing of roadmap.md:4387 and :4401). Replaying the same sealed manifest under the same mapping does nothing and recognizes prior work. Any other snapshot of the same source converges on stored source-record bindings and never creates a second identity (roadmap.md:4363). That includes backfills, a forgotten file and a second attempt after an abort. Identity never rests on a file digest or a manifest. In v1, differences from what Asym holds are shown as source-changed conflicts for reviewed correction and are never applied source-wins. A row missing from a later snapshot is reported, never treated as a deletion. 6. AFTER THE SWITCH: LEGACY TAIL BACKFILL (replaces 'gifts-only catch-up'). Facts recorded after the boundary by SiteStacker or another legacy executor enter only through a bounded series of separately reviewed backfills (roadmap.md:4395). They run on a fixed cadence until the recorded tail closure date and are never a standing feed. Each backfill carries append-only money facts: settled charges, and refunds, returns, chargebacks and dispute outcomes linked to the imported original by an exact scoped reference. It also carries schedule-state and stop evidence for Phase 16. [Founder to confirm] It also carries adverse contact facts (unsubscribes, do-not-contact, deceased, erasure), applied only to restrict. A backfill never creates or updates supporter details and never overrides an Asym edit. A fact with no exact source-binding match waits in match review (P13 story 171). Each executor and period has exactly one authoritative gift source: SiteStacker exports for gifts SiteStacker runs, and the ministry's own PayPal or processor report files for executors SiteStacker does not record. Every other source is reconciliation evidence only. Every fact keeps its import origin. Approving a backfill approves data only. SiteStacker's receipt for a tail gift is carried as evidence. No Asym receipt, thank-you, welcome or first-support work follows unless F5 and Phase 7 later allow it. 7. LEGACY SURFACES (replaces the three-item 'off' attestation). Before any Asym sending or automation activates, the ministry records one state for each surface, per Site, Site Channel or list: Off at the boundary; Kept on through the tail (with an owner and an end condition); Not used; or Outside the ministry's control. Off: giving forms and checkout; giving links, QR codes and text-to-give targets; appeal and record-changing Spark and Motion automations; organization Mailchimp sync; SiteStacker imports and rollbacks; staff CRM editing. Kept on until each cohort is handed over or ended: the recurring engine; receipts for legacy charges; recurring confirmation and reminder messages; the declined-payment message with its update link; the donor cancel and update path. Outside control: missionary-owned Mailchimp lists, gateway-sent emails, PayPal notices. Exactly one system is responsible for each donor-facing duty for each cohort, and SiteStacker alone receipts what it charges. Asym shows these states as 'ministry attested', re-confirms them at every backfill, runs an overlap-and-gap check each cycle, and never claims SiteStacker was frozen or switched off. 8. RECURRING AND PROCESSOR HANDOVER. No recurring gift moves on switch day. Moving one is a Phase 16 formal cutover: proof the old executor stopped, plus applicable fresh IDs and collection authorization. Without that proof the arrangement stays external/read-only and nothing runs active-active (P16 :1314). This applies to ministries that already used Stripe too, because SiteStacker is a different application and Asym always binds through its own Settlement Account Binding. Phase 30 never coordinates processors, never transfers vaults, never handles card numbers, bank numbers or credentials, never runs re-authorization and never proves an old executor stopped. Those duties stay with Phase 16 and the Phase 13 'separate migration workstream' until owners are named by editing P13 :743-746 and :2192-2199. Legacy provider identifiers are inert source evidence. 9. RECONCILIATION. Record counts and native-currency totals are reconciled by Legal Entity × original currency × source basis (roadmap.md:4369) at two levels. Level 1: every row of the final manifest has exactly one disposition, and accepted totals tie to export totals. Level 2: export totals tie to controls not derived from the imported files. Those controls are SiteStacker on-screen counts captured at export, its Statements report, and processor reports for the periods the ministry can supply (at least the open fiscal period and the tail). Unavailable periods are declared, never assumed reconciled. Exactly one source basis is declared per gift population and period, with an overlap proof: Historic Giving copies native income, and ledger lines are never gifts. Unexplained variance blocks sign-off, and sign-off is bound to a report version. 10. REVERSAL. Before go-live the ministry may abort. Plans are discarded, committed rows stay invisible and are never deleted, and a later attempt converges. [Founder to confirm] After go-live, system-of-record authority does not return to SiteStacker without a new founder decision. Batch reversal follows roadmap.md:4371 and owner-constraints.md:378-385. Every switch and every run can be halted. 11. KEEPING OPTION B POSSIBLE. Records carry Switch, manifest, run and source-record-binding identity, plus one boundary per source family (all equal in v1). Nothing else is built for B: these records do not preclude it, and they do not make it ready. 12. STATUS OF THE BRING-OVER RESEARCH. The SiteStacker bring-over inventory and processor analysis (q01-r1 to q01-r4) are complete to the limit of public evidence as of 2026-11-01. They stay provisional until a representative real export confirms them.

Adverse-fact forwarding still applies to such a system.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | Child sponsorship is out of program scope, and SiteStacker uses Spark primarily for it. A ministry running sponsorship would keep SiteStacker editing and charging shared supporters, which amounts to Option C for that group and splits consent floors across two live systems.                                                                                                                                                                                                  | openspec/contracts/platform-product-intent.md:315-316 (verified); roadmap.md:4395; C13; q01-r1 K21 (151000115103, 151000191779); v4-02 (narrowed).                                                                                                                    |

Each export profile records a reviewed timestamp-zone evidence fact. Rows within one civil day of the boundary whose zone is unproven go to review. The boundary governs only Phase 30 classification and never sets a Phase 16, 20 or 21 boundary. A legacy charge after the boundary but before its cohort's Phase 16 cutover is a planned tail fact, not a control-loss exception.                                                                                                                                                                                                             | SiteStacker's export time zone is undocumented and DST gaps exist. Phase 20 and 21 boundaries are independently authoritative, and reusing the switch instant as a Phase 16 protected boundary would turn every tail charge into an urgent exception.                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | P16 :112, :1295; owner-constraints.md:410-413 (verified); ADR-0058 :7-12; ADR-0106 :83-104; CONTEXT.md:5035-5043, :6949-6956; SiteStacker 151000115212 (verified-vendor-doc); v2-B4, v2-L4, v1-19.                                                                    |

## 5. Category-by-category review (all 22 categories)

Each concern lists what could go wrong, why it matters, severity and likelihood, evidence, effect on the answer, best fix, exact spec language, bucket, and the skeptic's ruling. Full reviewer prose is in `review/q01-vN-review.md`.

### 5.1 Problem validity, necessity, and alternatives

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v1). **Synthesis verdict:** Option A is the right model and is proportionate. B and C have no governing support (roadmap.md:4395). As worded it overpromises 'only place', treats the first Phase 30 release as the first switch, and has refinements 1 and 2 and the reconciliation wording too narrow. Record A worded correctly, with a legacy tail. This is not a new option.

**What was checked:** Re-read the decision log (C1-C23, D1-D19, O1-O11, F1-F17); roadmap.md:4320-4420; owner-constraints.md:329-455; P16 :1230-1238, :1262-1266, :1287-1296, :1312-1317; P13 :157-166, :729-747, :657-663, :2190-2200. Tracker GETs: #809, #834, #711, #512, #2295 all OPEN; PR #2430 OPEN. r10-gap-g3 shows no real artifact or consenting ministry. Compared A as worded, A-prime (A plus a planned bounded recurring tail), B, C, assisted delivery and raw loads. Re-fetched SiteStacker 151000183830 and 151000191779 (verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01).

#### v1-01: 'Asym becomes the only place' overpromises and invites switching SiteStacker off while it is the recurring executor

- **What could go wrong:** A ministry ends or disables SiteStacker at go-live. Its scheduler stops charging card and ACH recurring gifts (inferred, strong; server code unverifiable), and the declined-payment recovery link (a SiteStacker System Message) disappears. Missionary recurring support silently ends. PayPal Standard subscriptions keep charging, up to an srt cap of 52 cycles, and nothing records them.
- **Why it matters:** Silent loss of missionary recurring support is the worst migration outcome. It breaks P13 'do not silently create a gap'.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** critical / medium
- **Evidence:** P16 :1314, :1264, :1289; P13 :735-737, :740-741 (repo-ratified, verified on develop 629cb71dc). SiteStacker 151000183830 'When a contribution fails for any reason the donor will receive an email ... reprocessed automatically' (verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01). PayPal srt cap from Payment.js ~:1388-1422 (inferred from vendor-served code, verifier-corrected). r5 K1.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Record A-prime. Asym becomes the system of record for supporters, history and staff work at the boundary. SiteStacker stays licensed in a defined tail mode until the recurring tail closes.
- **Spec language:** At the switch boundary Asym becomes the system of record for supporters, their history and all staff work. Legacy recurring gifts are not moved by the switch. They keep running on SiteStacker, its processor or PayPal as named external, read-only arrangements until each cohort is either handed over under Phase 16 formal cutover or deliberately ended with donor notice. The ministry keeps SiteStacker licensed, with its recurring engine and donor-facing recurring messages running, until the switch's recurring tail is closed. Go-live readiness lists every such arrangement by executor.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The core point holds. Calling Asym 'the only place' is wrong while SiteStacker is still charging recurring gifts. P13 :735-741 says observation is not control and forbids silently creating a gap. P16 :1314 makes moving to a different executor a formal cutover, otherwise the old executor stays external and read-only (both checked on develop). The concern is overstated in two ways. (a) The founder's refinements (2) and (4) already assume SiteStacker keeps charging until the Phase 16 handover. So 'A-prime' is a wording correction plus an explicit tail commitment, not a new model. (b) P16 :1289 (planned disconnect) and :1312 (cohort release) are about incidents and Asym's own provider disconnect. They apply only by analogy (inferred), not as direct governance. The PayPal srt cap is inferred from vendor-served code. Severity drops from critical to high because the decision text already signals the tail. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: When recording, replace 'the only place' with 'the system of record for supporters, history and staff work'. Add one sentence: legacy recurring arrangements stay external and read-only (P16 :1264/:1314) until handed over or ended, and SiteStacker stays licensed and running until the tail closes. Do not label this a new option. It is Option A worded correctly.

#### v1-02: First Phase 30 release is not the first ministry switch; A depends on unbuilt owner commands

- **What could go wrong:** The first Phase 30 slice is Party-only. A switch run with Party import alone freezes SiteStacker while Asym holds no giving history. That is an unplanned Option B without B's safeguards.
- **Why it matters:** Staff and missionaries lose history, and C22 blocks first-gift and inactivity conclusions. The rejected model returns by accident.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** roadmap.md:4387, :4367; owner-constraints.md:389-392 (repo-published). #711, #834, #512 OPEN; Party tables #570/#645 open (decision-log.md:150); P29 PR #2430 unmerged.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Add a switch-eligibility precondition. Any fallback is an explicit, dated founder exception, never a default.
- **Spec language:** A ministry switch may be scheduled only when the target commands for every family the ministry needs for day-to-day work are owner-ready. At minimum: Parties and households; contact points with consent and suppression evidence; one-time giving history including reversals; recurring evidence for the Phase 16 classifier. Otherwise the switch is not scheduled, except under a named, dated founder exception that keeps SiteStacker as the read-only reference for the missing family and imports it later as a separate reviewed backfill.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The gap is real. #711, #834, #512, #809 and #570 are OPEN (gh, 2026-11-01) and PR #2430 is unmerged. Mis-cite: #645 is the Phase 11 Custom Fields epic, not a Party-table issue. The fix is partly redundant. owner-constraints.md:389-392 already says adapters 'activate only after the target phase and its typed import command exist', and roadmap.md:4367 says target readiness decides what can be committed. The proposed 'dated founder exception' that lets a switch go ahead while a family is missing is effectively Option B through a side door. The founder rejected B, and the exception would bypass a published owner constraint, so it should not be in the Q1 record. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Clarify that 'first release' means the first release in which a ministry switch is offered. Cite owner-constraints.md:389-392 and roadmap.md:4367 as the eligibility precondition. Drop the founder-exception mechanism. Any partial switch would need an explicit owner amendment and a new founder decision.

#### v1-03: 'Fully research what must be brought over' cannot be completed from desk research

- **What could go wrong:** The inventory and processor picture get recorded as settled. Still unverified: export columns, processor IDs in exports, PayPal payment recording, the gateway list ('STI'), gateway-native schedules. Server-side cron behaviour is unverifiable. The kit then fails on the first real export.
- **Why it matters:** The founder asked for completeness. The honest answer is 'complete to the limit of public evidence'. Verifiers already corrected K22, the srt cap and the OAuth scope.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** r10-gap-g3.md:16, :51 (no artifact, supplier or consenting tenant). r1 and r2 unverified lists. 151000191779 re-verified: recurring checkout without login is possible (verified-vendor-doc).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Record the inventory as provisional. Make F2 the next blocking question. Require a per-ministry processor-and-automation census before the first practice run, retaken at the final export.
- **Spec language:** The SiteStacker bring-over inventory (q01-r1) and processor analysis (q01-r2 to r4) are complete to the limit of public vendor evidence as of 2026-11-01. They are provisional and become kit requirements only once a representative export confirms them. Each ministry switch begins with a processor-and-automation census, retaken at the final export. It covers: every gateway and merchant account per Site and currency; every executor type (SiteStacker scheduler, PayPal subscriptions, gateway-native schedules); recurring arrangements counted with and without a linked SiteStacker login; gateway-sent donor emails; Stripe topology.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Correct. r10-gap-g3 records no artifact, supplier or consenting tenant. Verifiers had already corrected several research claims. The founder's 'fully research' request can only be met to the limit of public evidence. The processor-and-automation census is distinct from the retained-state census in v1-13 and D13, so this is not a duplicate. It is a caveat on the record, not a change to the model. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Record the inventory as provisional, as proposed. Make F2 the next question. Make the per-ministry processor-and-automation census a runbook input, retaken at the final export.

#### v1-04: Refinement (2) 'gifts-only catch-up' is too narrow and assumes the wrong source

- **What could go wrong:** Refunds, ACH returns, chargebacks, failed renewals and donor stops are missed, so totals are overstated and Phase 16 gets no stop evidence. PayPal-run, gateway-native and gateway-dashboard facts may never reach SiteStacker. Gifts with no matching Party have no rule. A repeating catch-up becomes a standing feed.
- **Why it matters:** Money truth and old-stop proof depend on these facts. A standing feed breaches owner-constraints.md:424-430. The ratified seam is a bounded reviewed backfill.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** P13 :1767 (via r5 A17), :539-542 (story 171); P16 :1295 (verified); roadmap.md:4395 'Reviewed backfill is a separate bounded operation'; r4 G1 (verifier-corrected); r5 K2, K3.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Rename to 'reviewed money-and-stop catch-up' and keep both founder protections. Source from SiteStacker re-exports first, with one canonical template for anything SiteStacker does not record. Make it a bounded series with a closure date. Unmatched facts go to match review.
- **Spec language:** After the switch, facts recorded by SiteStacker or a legacy executor enter only through a reviewed money-and-stop catch-up: a bounded series of separately reviewed backfills on a fixed cadence with a recorded closure date, never a standing feed. Each catch-up carries settled gifts; refunds, returns, chargebacks and dispute outcomes linked to the imported original; and schedule stop or change evidence for Phase 16. It never writes supporter fields and never overrides an Asym edit; differences are listed for ordinary correction. A fact with no exact source-binding match waits in match review, with no email, name or fingerprint linking. Every fact is classified against the switch boundary at plan approval and cannot become prospective later.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Refunds, returns, disputes and stop evidence after the boundary are needed for P13 append-only reversals and for P16 old-stop proof. P13 story 171 (:539-543, checked) requires unmatched records to be quarantined, not linked by email or name. roadmap.md:4395 'Reviewed backfill is a separate bounded operation' (checked) is the correct governing cite. Minor overstatement: owner-constraints.md:424-430 routes recurring operational intake to the source domain. It does not forbid a standing feed outright. Some sourcing detail overlaps v1-08. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Widen the catch-up to money facts plus stop evidence and keep both founder protections, as proposed. Each catch-up fact also carries SiteStacker's receipt status as source-bound already_receipted evidence (P13 story 170), so Asym neither re-receipts nor misses a receipt. Sourcing rules belong in v1-08 and should not be repeated here.

#### v1-05: Refinement (1) narrows 'exact repeat import' without restating convergence for later snapshots

- **What could go wrong:** Catch-ups, forgotten files and re-attempts after an abort are different snapshots. If v1 promises only same-snapshot replay, a builder may create duplicates. 'One final export' is really a multi-file manifest, since long exports error out.
- **Why it matters:** It contradicts published 'Reimport converges on the same permitted target identity' and 're-import updates instead of duplicating'.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** roadmap.md:4363; owner-constraints.md:373-374 (repo-published); r10-gap-g3.md:96 (snippet-only, 2.48 export errors); r5 K5, E-40, X4.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Keep the narrow meaning of 'exact repeat', add a convergence floor, and define the final export as a manifest.
- **Spec language:** 'Exact repeat import' means replaying the same source snapshot manifest (all files, digests, filters, exporter role, SiteStacker version and export times). It produces no new writes and recognizes prior work. Any other snapshot of the same source converges on existing source bindings and never creates a second copy. In v1, differences from what Asym holds are shown for review and never applied source-wins. 'One final export' means one final snapshot manifest, taken after the boundary, that may contain several files.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: roadmap.md:4363 'Reimport converges on the same permitted target identity' and owner-constraints.md:373-374 (both checked) bind regardless of how refinement (1) is worded. Defining the final export as a multi-file manifest is cheap and correct. Likelihood is moderate because the published convergence text already governs. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: As proposed. Add one sentence that convergence (roadmap.md:4363) still applies to any different snapshot.

#### v1-06: Reconciliation wording omits Legal Entity, source basis and independent control totals

- **What could go wrong:** Totals re-derived from the same file prove nothing. Historic Giving automatically copies Contributions income, so summing both double-counts. A per-currency total hides a wrong Legal Entity, and SiteStacker has no Legal Entity concept.
- **Why it matters:** A misleading finance sign-off is worse than none.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** roadmap.md:4369; owner-constraints.md:433-437 (repo-published); decision-log D16; r1 K19 citing 151000122348 'income data will populate in Historic Giving automatically' (verifier-checked).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Amend the wording when the decision is recorded.
- **Spec language:** Reconciliation compares Asym's accepted, rejected, quarantined, excluded and unchanged counts, and money totals by Legal Entity x original currency x declared source basis, against control totals SiteStacker itself reports (screen counts or its own reports), never against figures re-derived from the imported file. Exactly one source basis is declared per gift population and period, with an overlap proof. Each variance is categorized; any unexplained variance blocks sign-off.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: roadmap.md:4369 already requires totals 'by Legal Entity, original currency and source basis' (checked). The founder's shorthand cannot override it, so the risk is copy drift, not a change to the decision. The proposed language also has an internal flaw. SiteStacker has no Legal Entity concept (r2), so 'control totals SiteStacker itself reports' cannot be partitioned by Legal Entity. Legal Entity comes from Asym's mapping. The Historic Giving double-count point (151000122348 via r1, verifier-checked) is valid. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Cite roadmap.md:4369 when recording. Compare against SiteStacker control totals by source scope (Site, merchant account, currency, period). Then show how each source-scope total partitions across mapped Legal Entities. Declare one source basis per population, with an overlap proof, as D16 requires.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Problem validity: the founder asked specifically about ministries that already used Stripe, and the review does not handle that case. If SiteStacker charged through the ministry's own Stripe account, binding Asym to that same account is not 'a different account' under P16 :1314. The path is then proof-gated adoption (#711 'adopt-existing-Stripe', #834 classifier, both OPEN), not donor re-authorization. v1-20's assumption that every gift needs a donor step and v1-11's tail length may both be wrong for that cohort. The reverse hazard: once Asym is bound to the same account, Asym's Stripe event ingestion could record SiteStacker-run charges as Asym gifts, and the catch-up would then duplicate them. Whether SiteStacker uses Stripe Billing subscriptions or its own scheduler on stored payment methods is unverified. Severity high; resolve before recording through the per-ministry census.
- Operational burden: receipts and statements for tail gifts are not tied together. SiteStacker receipts what it charges during the tail (v1-15). Unless each catch-up gift carries source-bound already_receipted evidence (P13 story 170) and a Phase 7 import fact (D4, undefined), P19 cumulative coverage (phase-19 :944 'no existing current official coverage') could include those gifts in an Asym year-end statement and duplicate SiteStacker's receipts. Severity high. Owner: P7, P13 and P19 (F13, F5).
- Problem validity: the review's own §5.1 proposed record adds 'any rollback to SiteStacker after go-live' as not in v1 and invariant (8) 'roll-forward only'. That decision was never put to the founder. It should be asked explicitly, not folded into the amended record. Severity medium.
- Problem validity: no owner text defines a Phase 30 'Switch' (r10-gap-g4: 'no owner text sets the CRM switch model'). Recording A creates new Phase 30 scope (switch record, attestations, boundary) beyond roadmap.md:4363-4403. Under C3 that needs a roadmap and owner-constraints amendment at spec time. v1-12 covers only the processor half. Severity medium.
- UX/user friction: the staff CRM blackout is not addressed. Between 'pause editing in SiteStacker' and go-live, staff cannot record contacts, notes or address changes in either system. v1-16 covers giving only. The rehearsals should measure and time-box the pause, with a holding procedure for interactions during it. Severity medium.
- Operational burden: practice runs that change nothing still put a prospect's real donor PII into Asym before Phase 29 custody exists (PR #2430 unmerged) and possibly before any contract. F2 and F4 raise this, but the review does not connect it to Option A's rehearsal step. Severity medium. Already on the frontier as F2/F4.

### 5.2 Brittleness

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v2). **Synthesis verdict:** The wording assumes an atomic single export, self-referential reconciliation, three switches covering every writer, and an unambiguous midnight. Replace these with a multi-file manifest plus a timed rehearsal, two-level reconciliation in the published partition, census-derived surface states, and a boundary as a first instant in the tenant giving zone. The WMTEK dependency of core exports is unproven.

**What was checked:** I checked the founder's Option A wording and refinements 1-4 against roadmap.md:4347-4401, owner-constraints.md:373-374 and :434-437, Phase 16 :112 and :497-499, ADR-0058 and ADR-0106. I compared them with the q01-r1/r5/r6 export, reconciliation and attestation analyses, the SiteStacker vendor articles 151000115212, 151000183830 and 151000119746 (fetched 2026-11-01), and MDN Temporal startOfDay.

#### v2-B1: 'One final export' assumes a point-in-time self-serve snapshot SiteStacker cannot produce

- **What could go wrong:** The final export is in fact about 19 files plus BI datasets. Some can only be built by WMTEK on a billable estimate. Long exports get split into partitions, and an export by a restricted User Group comes out silently partial. The files are taken hours apart while the cron, donor self-service and inbound capture keep writing. The result is an unbounded freeze, or a switch-day dataset that never arrives.
- **Why it matters:** Freeze length is the main operational risk of Option A. Rehearsals as framed practise the plan, not the export procedure.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** q01-r1 §5 lists 19 exports. V39 151000119684 says 'Currently, all reports are built by WMTEK staff' (q01-r1:76, not re-fetched). WMTEK works only on an approved estimate (q01-r1:146). r10-gap-g3.md:96 says 2.48 exports error out (snippet-only). r10-gap-g4.md:78 confirms there is no change feed. q01-r5 E-26 covers the restricted exporter. roadmap.md:4349 binds the plan to exact bytes.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Define the final export as a manifest with a recorded export window that starts after the boundary. Rehearse the exact export procedure, timed, within N days of go/no-go. Each vendor-built dataset must be confirmed in writing before the boundary, taken before the boundary with a declared as-of time, or visibly excluded.
- **Spec language:** The final export is a manifest of named files taken after the recorded boundary. Each file records the exporter, User Group, SiteStacker version, filter or partition, start and finish times and the on-screen control count captured at export time. Go/no-go requires a rehearsal of the same manifest shape completed within {N} days, with a measured duration below the declared maximum freeze length. Any dataset only the vendor can produce is confirmed in writing before the boundary, taken before the boundary with a declared as-of time, or visibly excluded.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The core point holds: 'one final export' is really a set of files (q01-r5 K5) and the plan binds to exact bytes (roadmap.md:4349, checked). The vendor-dependency leg is overstated. The review's own RC6 admits V39 'all reports are built by WMTEK staff' is scoped to Motion 2 workflow reports (q01-r1:76, not re-fetched), so nothing shows the core CRM or gift exports need WMTEK. The partitioning leg is snippet-only (r10-gap-g3.md:96). Likelihood is medium, not high. (corrected severity: high (likelihood medium)) Corrected fix: Record 'one final export set' (a manifest). Require one timed rehearsal of the same manifest shape before go/no-go. Any dataset the census finds is vendor-built must be scheduled in writing or visibly excluded. Drop the claim that the core exports depend on WMTEK until a real census shows it.

#### v2-B2: Reconciling only against the export cannot detect truncated or filtered exports, and the wording drops Legal Entity and source basis

- **What could go wrong:** An export that is truncated, filtered by permissions, missing transactions deleted after a refund, or double counted between Historic Giving and Contributions still reconciles perfectly against itself. 'Per-currency totals' also narrows the published Legal Entity × currency × source basis requirement.
- **Why it matters:** Finance would sign off on fidelity to the file, not to the books or the processor. C3 forbids narrowing a published requirement by shorthand.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** roadmap.md:4369 requires totals 'by Legal Entity, original currency and source basis'. owner-constraints.md:434-437. roadmap.md:4363 asks for per-record lineage against the export. K19 (151000122348 via q01-r1) and K8 (q01-r1:138). q01-r1 H1 (:299) and q01-r6 point 3 call for independent controls. q01-r2 recommends processor reconciliation per merchant account × currency.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Reconcile at two levels, each per Legal Entity × original currency × source basis. Level 1 is import against export. Level 2 is export against independent controls: on-screen counts, the Statements report and processor settlements per merchant account × currency.
- **Spec language:** Reconcile record counts and money totals per Legal Entity × original currency × source basis at two levels: (1) every row of the final export set has exactly one disposition and accepted totals tie to export totals; (2) export totals tie to independent controls (on-screen counts captured at export time, the SiteStacker Statements report, and processor settlement reports per merchant account × currency). Any unexplained variance at either level blocks sign-off.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Checked: roadmap.md:4369 requires totals 'by Legal Entity, original currency and source basis', and owner-constraints.md:434-437 requires Legal-Entity-partitioned per-currency totals. The founder's shorthand drops two of those three dimensions, and C3 forbids narrowing by shorthand. Self-reconciliation cannot detect a truncated or permission-filtered export. Proportionality: settlement-level checks across all history are often impossible, because old processor reports may be gone and q01-r4:113 M1 says Asym cannot read the old account. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Record the published wording (Legal Entity × original currency × source basis). Level 1: every export row gets exactly one disposition, and accepted totals tie to export totals. Level 2: on-screen counts captured at export time and the Statements report for every family. Processor settlement reports per merchant account × currency apply only to the periods the ministry can actually obtain (at least the current and prior year). Unavailable periods are declared, never silently assumed reconciled.

#### v2-B3: Refinement 3's three-switch attestation (Spark, forms, Mailchimp sync) fails in both directions

- **What could go wrong:** Turning Spark off disables goal-reached cancellations of recurring gifts, so the ministry over-collects, and it also stops Mastercard pre-billing reminders. Leaving System Messages on keeps the Contributions Declined rerun link live, and that link charges after the switch. Motion workflows, inbound capture, text-to-give and missionary-owned Mailchimp lists fall outside all three switches. A one-time attestation is never re-checked.
- **Why it matters:** C23 requires zero unintended send or payment effect. A single on/off setting either breaks receipt and card-network duties or lets charges continue.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** 151000119746 (modified 29 Sep 2023, verified-vendor-doc, accessed 2026-11-01): 'you may want to cancel recurring gifts and notify donors when a campaign goal has been reached'. 151000183830 (22 Aug 2024, verified): 'Once the new payment method information is filled out and saved, the gift will be reprocessed automatically'. V64 151000122015 (q01-r1:101, not re-fetched). 151000122392 (verifier). q01-r5 K4/S3. q01-r6 §9.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Generate the checklist from the census, with a state and an owner for each item. Re-confirm it at every catch-up and back it with detection monitors (v2-F5).
- **Spec language:** Before any Asym sending or automation activates for a migrated tenant, every SiteStacker writing or outward surface found by the readiness census is recorded with exactly one state: Off; Kept on for the recurring tail (reason, named owner, planned end date); Not used; or Outside the ministry's control (with follow-up action). Spark outcomes that cancel recurring gifts, and recurring pre-billing reminders, may not be set to Off while any recurring schedule they govern is still charging, unless a replacement action is recorded. The Contributions Declined rerun link is either removed or recorded as a live legacy charging path. The list is re-confirmed at every catch-up run.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Re-fetched 151000119746 (modified 29 Sep 2023). The goal-reached cancellation is an administrator-built Spark example ('For example, you may want to…'), not default behaviour. So 'turning Spark off causes over-collection' only applies where a ministry built that outcome, and likelihood is low-to-medium, not high. The valid residual: a fixed three-item list is incomplete. q01-r1 already lists API keys, integrations, inbound email, text-to-give and missionary Mailchimp keys (q01-r1:173, :204, :258, :362, :389). The Contributions Declined rerun link duplicates v2-E2, and re-confirmation duplicates v2-F5. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Replace the three named switches with 'every writing or outward surface found by the readiness census (q01-r1 1.5) has a recorded state and owner before any Asym sending or automation activates'. Treat the goal-cancellation outcome as one census item, checked only where it is configured. Move the rerun link to E2 and the drift monitoring to F5.

#### v2-B4: Boundary classification depends on undocumented SiteStacker time semantics and a literal 'midnight'

- **What could go wrong:** SiteStacker runs recurring gifts 'in the early morning hours' with no time zone stated, and charges on save. Its export time zone is undocumented, and Sites may be split. q01-r5's '00:00 local' can fall on a time that does not exist. Gift dates shift by a day across zones, so rows near the boundary get misclassified.
- **Why it matters:** Rows that land in the wrong lane (history versus tail, pre-Asym versus not) get the wrong receipt and coverage treatment.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** 151000115212 (26 Apr 2024, verified-vendor-doc). MDN Temporal.ZonedDateTime.startOfDay, America/Sao_Paulo 2015 example (verified-vendor-doc, accessed 2026-11-01). Phase 16 :112 and :497-499 ('never guesses from processor UTC'). q01-r5 §3.1. q01-r6 freeze edge case.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Use the tenant giving timezone, with the boundary at the first instant of the civil date. Record a reviewed profile-level export-zone evidence fact, established by matching sample transactions to processor UTC times and never inferred per row. Hold rows within ±1 civil day of the boundary for review if their zone is unproven.
- **Spec language:** The Switch Boundary is the first instant of the declared civil date in the tenant giving timezone, shown as 'starts at the first moment of {date}, {zone} (UTC{offset})', never 'midnight'. Each export profile records a reviewed timestamp-zone evidence fact. Rows within one civil day of the boundary without proven zone evidence are held for review.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Phase 16 :112 makes the tenant IANA giving timezone canonical and forbids silent server-zone fallback (checked). SiteStacker's export time semantics are undocumented, and the MDN DST point is valid. The fix (first instant of the civil date, a profile-level zone evidence fact, holding near-boundary rows) is proportionate. (corrected severity: medium)

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Brittleness: Legal Entity assignment presupposed. B2's per-Legal-Entity reconciliation assumes every SiteStacker row has a reviewed Legal Entity, but owner-constraints.md:375-377 forbids inferring Legal Entity from Site, provider or a default entity. The switch needs an explicit, reviewed Legal Entity assignment rule per merchant account and Site (see r10-gap-g8) before reconciliation means anything. inferred from repo-published.
- Edge cases: same Stripe account, SiteStacker still holds its keys. The founder asked about ministries already on Stripe, and the review never handles this case. SiteStacker charges saved payment methods off-session with the ministry's keys or Connect access (q01-r4:113). Revoking that access at freeze kills the recurring tail that refinements 2 and 4 rely on; keeping it leaves a live executor on the very account Asym may later bind. Phase 16 :1314 treats a different application as formal cutover even on the same account. Credential revocation therefore has to be sequenced per executor cohort, not at freeze. inferred from repo-ratified + q01-r4.
- Failure modes / edge cases: switch year split across two systems. A switch inside a tax year splits each donor's year between SiteStacker (pre-boundary gifts already receipted, plus SiteStacker self-service year-end statements) and Asym (tail and new gifts). The review does not say which system issues that year's year-end statement, or how duplicate or partial statements are prevented. Phase 19's Late Fact Obligation (phase-19 :976-990, via q01-r5 A28) and Phase 7 already-receipted evidence (phase-13 :1029-1036) govern this, but the switch plan needs an explicit rule. inferred.
- Lifecycle/idempotency: a row that disappears from a later catch-up. A gift present in the final export may vanish from later catch-ups, for example a transaction deleted after a refund (K8). q01-r5:119 handles absence only for rehearsal drift. Catch-ups need the same rule: absence is evidence for finance review, never an automatic reversal and never silently ignored. inferred.
- Lifecycle/concurrency: the same source transaction changes status between catch-ups. ACH can move pending → settled → returned (Phase 16 :1295; Stripe ACH window via q01-r5:199). Without status transitions keyed to the scoped source identity (L2), each catch-up could create a new money fact instead of advancing or reversing the existing one. L5 covers only reversals that staff recorded. inferred.
- Failure modes: processor-level checks depend on unauthorized access. F4 and B2 rely on processor-side data, but q01-r4:113 says the current Phase 13 M1 topology gives Asym no read access to the old account. Without either ministry-supplied reports or a Phase 13 D1 owner amendment (a read-only observation binding), level-2 reconciliation and per-executor catch-up cannot run. This dependency must be named before recording.

### 5.3 Technical debt

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v4). **Synthesis verdict:** Moderate. Bound refinement 4 to identities plus a per-family boundary. Lineage must be append-only and revoked from staff. The backfill must have a closure date so it cannot harden into a manual sync. v4-18 (Phase 28 tickets) was rejected as a duplicate of D12/F17 and does not drive amendments.

**What was checked:** Whether 'designed so B can be added later' is bounded. Where per-record lineage lives and whether it can be trusted. Whether the catch-up can become a standing manual sync. Whether Phase 28 tickets pre-empt shared run and plan identity. Sources: 20260508000413_crm_identity_mapping.sql:73-90, :204-240; 20260818010000_retire_twenty_crm_runtime.sql:21-33; 20261024163000_authz_definer_execute_lockdown.sql:39-41; #2295 body (gh 2026-11-01); decision-log D9, D12, O7; owner-constraints :408-413, :424-426; roadmap :4363, :4395; #2347 and #2087 (OPEN, Ready).

#### v4-15: 'Designed so Option B can be added later' has no bound

- **What could go wrong:** Either speculative Option B machinery is built in v1 (per-family scheduling, partial live writing, a generic orchestrator), or one global boundary is baked in and later needs a data migration to split.
- **Why it matters:** The minimum that keeps B possible already exists in governing text: one boundary per source family (the P20/P21 pattern) plus the run, batch and source identity and lineage the roadmap requires. Anything more is overengineering.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** repo-published: owner-constraints.md:408-413; roadmap.md:4363. repo-ratified: ADR-0106 :83-90. q01-r5 K6.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Persist a Switch grouping record with one Source-Family Boundary per in-scope family (all equal in v1), plus run, manifest and source-binding identities, and nothing else.
- **Spec language:** v1 persists a Switch record grouping one Source-Family Boundary per in-scope family, all equal in v1, together with run, Source Snapshot Manifest and source-binding identities. v1 builds no other Option B capability (per-family scheduling, partial live writing, refresh import). Adding Option B requires its own founder decision. Acceptance: the schema can represent two families with different boundary instants without migration, and v1 validation rejects unequal instants.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Sound and already minimal. It bounds the founder's 'designed so B can be added later' to identities that roadmap.md:4363 already requires. Low risk. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: As proposed.

#### v4-16: The lineage carrier stays writable by staff, and #2295 does not revoke it (correction to decision-log D9/O7)

- **What could go wrong:** crm_record_links, the ratified binding carrier, is still Twenty-shaped (twenty_object_name and twenty_record_id NOT NULL, free-text entity ID). Any staff member of the tenant can INSERT and UPDATE it. #2295 only rewrites the policies to the pure-tenant predicate and drops is_super_admin; it tests cross-tenant denial only, so same-tenant writes remain and might widen. Staff could forge or redirect bindings, and a catch-up would then attach money to the wrong Party with apparent provenance. Lineage is also scattered across external_ref columns, P16 provenance, staging and crm_record_links.
- **Why it matters:** Provenance that anyone can edit is not provenance. Refinement (4) and v4-12 depend on trustworthy bindings.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** repo-integrated: 20260508000413_crm_identity_mapping.sql:73-90, :204 (GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE TO authenticated), :219-240 (staff-membership INSERT/UPDATE policies); 20261024163000:40-41 (retargeted, not removed). repo-published: #2295 body (pure-tenant rewrite; acceptance tests cross-tenant denial only; OQ4 default). decision-log D9 and O7 state #2295 'revokes direct access', which is inaccurate.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** The Phase 30 spec names one append-only source-binding index, written only by the commit path or an owner command with actor and run recorded. Owner tables keep source-qualified references. A dedicated ticket, separate from #2295, revokes authenticated INSERT/UPDATE on any lineage table before bindings are trusted. Correct D9/O7.
- **Spec language:** Source bindings used for lineage, replay or catch-up resolution are append-only, written only by the Phase 30 commit path or an owner command with actor and run recorded, and cannot be inserted or updated by any authenticated role. Acceptance (real database): an authenticated staff session in the same tenant cannot INSERT or UPDATE a binding row; a binding correction appends a superseding row with actor and reason.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified. 20260508000413_crm_identity_mapping.sql:204 has GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE TO authenticated, and :219-240 adds staff-membership INSERT/UPDATE policies. 20261024163000:40-42 only retargets them with ALTER POLICY. The #2295 body (gh, 2026-11-01) describes a pure-tenant RLS rewrite with cross-tenant denial tests and no revoke. So decision-log D9 (:69) and O7 (:93) are wrong to say #2295 revokes direct access. Note also that the Twenty-only CHECK was dropped in 20260818010000:21-22. Severity is medium because nothing trusts these bindings yet. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Correct D9/O7. Before any binding counts as trusted provenance, a separate ticket revokes authenticated INSERT/UPDATE on the lineage table, and writes go only through the commit path or an audited owner command. Leave the single-index design to the spec.

#### v4-17: The catch-up can harden into a standing, manually run sync

- **What could go wrong:** The tail can last years: PayPal subscriptions run up to 52 cycles (about 4.3 years at monthly), SiteStacker's scheduler runs until each donor reauthorizes, and dispute and return windows add months. Monthly manual export, review and commit cycles then continue indefinitely: an unowned money feed run on tribal knowledge.
- **Why it matters:** The roadmap forbids permanent dual writers. owner-constraints :424-426 assigns recurring intake to the source domain, not to Phase 30. The q01-r4 verifier confirms the conflict arises once the catch-up repeats.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** repo-published: roadmap.md:4395; owner-constraints.md:424-426. PayPal srt cap: inferred from vendor-served code (r1/r2 verifiers). ACH about 60 days and cards about 120 days: q01-r5 S9 (Stripe), not re-verified here.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Set a tail closure date at go-live. Each catch-up is a new reviewed plan. After closure, any remaining executor is stopped or moved to a Phase 13/16 owner-domain evidence path, and Phase 30 runs no more catch-ups.
- **Spec language:** Each Switch records a tail closure date at go-live. Every catch-up is a separate reviewed bounded plan. After closure, Phase 30 runs no further catch-up for that Switch; any legacy executor still charging moves to a Phase 13/16 owner-domain evidence path or is stopped. Acceptance: a catch-up plan for a Switch past its closure date cannot be committed.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Valid. roadmap.md:4395 rules out permanent dual writers, and owner-constraints.md:424-426 gives standing intake to the owning source domain. Without an end condition the catch-up becomes a manual sync. Partly overlaps v4-04 and v4-08, but the tail closure date is a separate point. The ACH/card dispute windows were not re-verified. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: As proposed: set a tail closure condition or date at go-live, make each catch-up a new reviewed plan, and run no Phase 30 catch-ups after closure.

#### v4-18: Phase 28 machinery tickets could fix run and plan identity before Phase 30 does

- **What could go wrong:** #2347 (Ready) puts plan storage in Phase 28 tables with no per-profile bounds, and #2087 (Ready, blocked only by #2347) adds a package. Option A's switch-level run, manifest and binding identities would then fork into a second engine or need unwinding.
- **Why it matters:** Refinement (4) makes these shared identities the backbone of the switch lifecycle, which raises the stakes of D12.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** gh issue view 2347/2087 (2026-11-01: OPEN, labels include Ready); decision-log D12 and §6 (from r10-gap-g1).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** The founder acts on F17 (hold, or amend to shared Phase 30 records with per-profile bounds) before #2347 merges.
- **Spec language:** Run, Source Snapshot Manifest, plan and source-binding identities are shared Phase 30 records used by every import profile, including Phase 28 D17. No profile-specific table owns plan or run identity.
- **Bucket:** monitor (signal: A pull request linked to #2347 or #2087 enters review, or either issue closes.; threshold: Any such event before the Phase 30 spec settles run and plan ownership.; owner: The founder, or the Phase 30 product owner acting under founder authority.; response: Apply the F17 decision (hold, or amend to shared Phase 30 records with per-profile bounds) before merge.)
- **Skeptic ruling:** rejected: Facts are accurate (#2347 and #2087 OPEN, labeled Ready), but this is already recorded as decision-log D12 (:72), F17 (:123) and §6 (:129). It has nothing to do with the Q1 switch model, so raising it here repeats existing work. (corrected severity: low)

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Source of truth / data integrity (high): the gifts-only catch-up drops adverse facts that SiteStacker or Mailchimp receive after the boundary, such as unsubscribes, do-not-contact flags, deceased marks, address changes and erasure requests. A gifts-only rule means Asym ignores them, or a person who opted out is treated as contactable. Basis: decision-log F12 (:118) and g4 (:140), which say Mailchimp drift needs a fresh export per import, and P13 :745-747 on erasure. Fix: a narrow reviewed catch-up for adverse consent and identity facts, applied only to restrict, never to widen.
- Source of truth (medium): year-end statements for the switch year. Gifts before the boundary (imported, already_receipted), gifts after it charged by SiteStacker, and gifts after it charged by Asym all fall in one tax year. No rule says which system issues the annual statement for each cohort, or how to avoid duplicate or partial statements (Phase 19; P13 :1030-1034 receipt boundary). v4-04 covers receipts per gift only.
- Data integrity (medium): the Switch Boundary has no defined form. It needs to be an instant with a time zone and a tie rule, matched by source ID across overlapping export windows. Otherwise gifts dated on boundary day are missed or doubled. P16 uses donor-anchored civil-date schedules (P13 :733), so a date-only boundary conflicts. Several reviewer fixes say 'measured from the Switch Boundary' without defining it.
- Hidden coupling (medium): no go/no-go or abort path after the ministry-declared freeze. If the final reconciliation fails, the plan must say whether the freeze is extended, SiteStacker resumes as system of record, or a partial commit is rolled back. Reversal must not delete Parties or rewind financial history (roadmap.md:4371). Option A as recorded only assumes success.
- Data integrity / technical debt (low-medium): practice runs that 'change nothing' still put full supporter PII and payment metadata into Asym staging. Rehearsal snapshot retention, disposal and tenant isolation belong under P29 custody rules (PR #2430, unmerged) and are not stated. Repeated practice runs multiply stored copies.
- Hidden coupling (medium): donor self-service for legacy-executor cohorts after go-live. If supporters move to Asym while SiteStacker still charges their recurring gifts, the Asym portal cannot cancel or update those gifts (P16 :1264, external/read-only), and the SiteStacker donor portal may be off. Each cohort needs a stated cancellation and update channel. v4-08 cites q01-r4 G3 but does not make this a requirement.

### 5.4 Edge cases

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v2). **Synthesis verdict:** Giving continuity between the pause and go-live must be settled before recording. Legacy reruns and charge-on-save are owner requirements on Phase 16 stop evidence. Donor self-service edits in SiteStacker become a reviewed worklist. D17 ordering is hygiene only.

**What was checked:** I read the research catalogs (q01-r1 X1-X20, q01-r2 §7, q01-r3 PX-1..16, q01-r5 E-01..52, q01-r6 §10) so as not to duplicate them. I then checked current checkout matching and constraints (donor-matching.ts, the donors email unique index, the tenant-client platform-key fallback), the Phase 28 D17 REQ28-R19..R22 requirements, and the SiteStacker rerun, charge-on-save and goal-trigger articles.

#### v2-E1: Giving continuity between freeze and go-live is undefined

- **What could go wrong:** Forms go off at the boundary, but Asym pages open only after reconciliation, so the ministry has a giving gap. Opening Asym checkout early creates native donors who later collide with imported Parties on the email unique key. It also misfires first-gift or welcome treatment for long-time supporters and, without a binding, falls back to the platform Stripe key. Leaving SiteStacker forms on creates post-boundary people and gifts.
- **Why it matters:** Every ministry has continuous online giving. Each option affects donors, money routing or identity.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** repo-integrated: packages/api/src/donate/guest/donor-matching.ts:7-10 (attach by normalized email); 20261024140000_identity_email_normalizer_and_donor_keys.sql:162-164 (unique tenant+email); packages/api/src/stripe/tenant-client.ts:64-66 (platform-key fallback). q01-r5 S3 item 1, S8 item 2 and K3. C22.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Proposed, founder choice. Keep SiteStacker giving on until Asym checkout opens. Open checkout only after a proved Phase 13 binding exists, the Parties cohort is live, and coverage is declared incomplete. Gifts taken after the boundary go through the reviewed catch-up under K3. The alternative is a declared, bounded giving gap with a holding page.
- **Spec language:** The switch plan states the giving-continuity rule. Asym online giving for a migrating tenant opens only after a proved Settlement Account Binding exists and the Parties cohort is live; SiteStacker giving stays available until that moment unless the ministry accepts a declared giving gap of at most {H} hours. Gifts SiteStacker takes after the boundary are catch-up facts and never create a supporter without the Phase 4 owner command.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: The founder's sequence (freeze → export → import → reconcile → go-live) does not say what happens to online giving in between. The repo-integrated citations check out: donor-matching.ts:7-10 attaches by normalized email; the unique index (tenant_id, email_normalized) is at migration :162-164; tenant-client.ts:64-66 falls back to the platform Stripe key. Opening checkout early therefore risks identity collisions and money landing in the wrong account. This has to be settled before Q1 is recorded. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Ask the founder a single question: keep SiteStacker giving on until Asym checkout opens, or accept a declared, bounded giving gap. Either way, Asym checkout for a migrating tenant opens only after a proved Phase 13 Settlement Account Binding exists and the Parties cohort is live. The 'coverage declared incomplete' condition belongs to C22 and does not need restating.

#### v2-E2: The legacy executor charges outside its schedule: failed-gift reruns, charge-on-save, disabled goal cancellations

- **What could go wrong:** A donor whose gift failed before the switch can click the rerun link weeks later, after Phase 16 has activated the Asym replacement. SiteStacker then charges the missed period while Asym charges the current one, so the donor is charged twice in a month and the old system has effectively back-charged. Staff edits on a gift's run date also charge immediately.
- **Why it matters:** Stop proof is framed as cancelling future schedules, which misses these paths. #809 is Stripe-adapter focused.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** 151000183830 (verified-vendor-doc): reprocessed automatically, no time limit documented. 151000115212 (verified): 'the charge will happen on save'. 151000119746 (verified). Phase 16 :1314-1316 ('no … blind catch-up, backcharge'). Issue #809 (open). q01-r4 G20.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** The census and every Phase 16 cohort cutover list open failed gifts that can still be rerun. Stop proof for SiteStacker cohorts neutralises the rerun path. The catch-up classifies each rerun with both its original due date and its payment date.
- **Spec language:** Old-executor stop evidence for a SiteStacker-executed cohort covers future schedules, failed gifts that can still be rerun, and staff-edit charge paths. Phase 30 census and catch-up runs report open rerunnable failed gifts per cohort and classify each rerun with its original due date and its payment date.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Both quotes are vendor-cited: 151000183830 (reprocessed automatically) and 151000115212 (charge on save). The goal-cancellation leg is an admin-configured example (see B3). The rerun path is a real way the legacy executor charges outside its schedule. It needs a donor action after a failure, so likelihood is medium-low. This overlaps v2-F3; both are owner requirements on Phase 16 stop proof. (corrected severity: high (likelihood medium-low)) Corrected fix: Owner requirement on Phase 16: stop evidence for a SiteStacker-executed cohort covers rerunnable failed gifts and staff-edit charge paths. Phase 30's census and catch-ups list rerunnable failed gifts per cohort and record both the due date and the payment date. Merge with F3 into one owner requirement.

#### v2-E3: Donor-entered changes in SiteStacker self-service during the tail are silently dropped

- **What could go wrong:** The research recommends keeping the SiteStacker donor dashboard reachable during the tail. Refinement 2 never writes supporter details, so address or email changes donors make there never reach Asym, and receipts and statements go to stale details.
- **Why it matters:** The donor believes the update succeeded while the two systems silently diverge. E-21 covers staff edits only.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** q01-r2 open question 1. q01-r4 question 4. sitestacker.com/online-giving self-service (marketing claim, via q01-r5 S3). r10-gap-g4.md:118-130.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Each catch-up emits a reviewed worklist of SiteStacker-side supporter changes made after the boundary. It has an owner and a response time and is never auto-applied. Alternatively, disable profile editing in self-service while keeping cancellation.
- **Spec language:** Each catch-up run lists supporter changes recorded in SiteStacker after the boundary as a reviewed worklist with a named owner; it never applies them automatically and never silently discards them.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The self-service capability rests only on a marketing claim (sitestacker.com/online-giving, via q01-r5:137). Whether profile editing can be disabled while keeping cancellation is unverified. q01-r5:137 item 5 already raises the self-service decision. Still a fair gap against refinement 2's 'never overwrites'. (corrected severity: medium-low) Corrected fix: Each catch-up reports post-boundary SiteStacker-side supporter changes as a reviewed worklist that is never auto-applied. Present 'disable profile editing' only as a possibility to verify, not as an option.

#### v2-E4: Phase 28 D17 missionary self-import into the same tenant before or during the switch

- **What could go wrong:** Restricted preparation that exists before the staff switch, combined with hidden-match non-disclosure, produces duplicate Parties. D17 commits during the frozen window change target revisions bound to the final plan and cause stale-plan conflicts.
- **Why it matters:** Both profiles reuse one Phase 30 engine, and their ordering within a tenant is undefined.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / low
- **Evidence:** C4. relationships.md:355-371 (REQ28-R19). :392-410 (REQ28-R21, hidden matches). roadmap.md:4328-4333 and :4349.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Pause D17 commits for the tenant between boundary and go-live. Count restricted preparation in the census. Phase 4/9 rules how the two sets of records reconcile.
- **Spec language:** While a tenant's Switch is between boundary and go-live, restricted self-import commits for that tenant are paused; the readiness census reports existing restricted preparation and its reconciliation follows the Phase 4/9 ruling.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: D17 is not yet active, as the review concedes. Existing rules already make the concurrency safe. roadmap.md:4349 turns a changed target into a conflict, not an overwrite. REQ28-R21 (relationships.md:396-410, checked) plus Phase 4 merge review handle duplicates without disclosing hidden matches. The concern is ordering hygiene, not a defect in Option A. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: The readiness census counts existing restricted preparation. Optionally pause D17 commits for the tenant between boundary and go-live. No new Phase 4/9 ruling is needed until D17 ships.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Brittleness: Legal Entity assignment presupposed. B2's per-Legal-Entity reconciliation assumes every SiteStacker row has a reviewed Legal Entity, but owner-constraints.md:375-377 forbids inferring Legal Entity from Site, provider or a default entity. The switch needs an explicit, reviewed Legal Entity assignment rule per merchant account and Site (see r10-gap-g8) before reconciliation means anything. inferred from repo-published.
- Edge cases: same Stripe account, SiteStacker still holds its keys. The founder asked about ministries already on Stripe, and the review never handles this case. SiteStacker charges saved payment methods off-session with the ministry's keys or Connect access (q01-r4:113). Revoking that access at freeze kills the recurring tail that refinements 2 and 4 rely on; keeping it leaves a live executor on the very account Asym may later bind. Phase 16 :1314 treats a different application as formal cutover even on the same account. Credential revocation therefore has to be sequenced per executor cohort, not at freeze. inferred from repo-ratified + q01-r4.
- Failure modes / edge cases: switch year split across two systems. A switch inside a tax year splits each donor's year between SiteStacker (pre-boundary gifts already receipted, plus SiteStacker self-service year-end statements) and Asym (tail and new gifts). The review does not say which system issues that year's year-end statement, or how duplicate or partial statements are prevented. Phase 19's Late Fact Obligation (phase-19 :976-990, via q01-r5 A28) and Phase 7 already-receipted evidence (phase-13 :1029-1036) govern this, but the switch plan needs an explicit rule. inferred.
- Lifecycle/idempotency: a row that disappears from a later catch-up. A gift present in the final export may vanish from later catch-ups, for example a transaction deleted after a refund (K8). q01-r5:119 handles absence only for rehearsal drift. Catch-ups need the same rule: absence is evidence for finance review, never an automatic reversal and never silently ignored. inferred.
- Lifecycle/concurrency: the same source transaction changes status between catch-ups. ACH can move pending → settled → returned (Phase 16 :1295; Stripe ACH window via q01-r5:199). Without status transitions keyed to the scoped source identity (L2), each catch-up could create a new money fact instead of advancing or reversing the existing one. L5 covers only reversals that staff recorded. inferred.
- Failure modes: processor-level checks depend on unauthorized access. F4 and B2 rely on processor-side data, but q01-r4:113 says the current Phase 13 M1 topology gives Asym no read access to the old account. Without either ministry-supplied reports or a Phase 13 D1 owner amendment (a read-only observation binding), level-2 reconciliation and per-executor catch-up cannot run. This dependency must be named before recording.

### 5.5 Footguns
