**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v6). **Synthesis verdict:** Several easy actions cause serious harm: mapping a legacy cus_ into a live column, the platform-key fallback, column defaults, a 'reviewed' backfill that unlocks sends, one charge under two IDs, a literal 'turn it off' checklist, and uploads carrying credentials or PANs. Most are cheap to prevent with safeguards. Batch undo stays as published, and only re-enabling SiteStacker writers needs a founder exception.

**What was checked:** Integrated code a migration would touch:

- stripe/tenant-client.ts:55-72 (platform-key fallback);
- donor-portal/billing.ts:17-42 (whole-account Billing Portal);
- stripe/webhooks.ts:422-445 and recurring.ts:104-112 (metadata routing; tenant filter applied only when a tenant is known);
- column defaults in init_schema.sql:76-82 and foundation_1_schema.sql:333-338;
- missionary-portal/donors.ts:174-192 (unknown status becomes Active);
- contribution-operations resend-receipt.ts:17-55 (live receipt send with no provenance check).

Ratified and published text: Phase 7 :339 (E3 imported gifts can be receipted), Phase 13 D.6 :1023-1039, workflow-studio runtime :26, roadmap.md:4395, owner-constraints.md:378-385, and P13 stories 171-172. Research: SiteStacker attestation hazards in q01-r1, q01-r2 and q01-r5.

#### v6-16: Imported processor IDs placed in live columns hand Asym control of an external executor

- **What could go wrong:** Mapping 'Stripe Customer ID' to donors.stripe_customer_id lets a migrated donor open a whole-account Billing Portal and change or cancel methods that SiteStacker's cron still uses. Stripe's copy keeps the same cus_ IDs on the new account, so the ID resolves on both. Any sub_ written to donor_pledges would be updated by webhooks routed by metadata, through a lookup that filters by tenant only when a tenant is known.
- **Why it matters:** This is adoption by metadata, which Phase 16 forbids, and it violates C14 ('a provider/source ID is a link, not an identity').
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** critical / medium
- **Evidence:** - donor-portal/billing.ts:37-40; stripe/recurring.ts:104-112; stripe/webhooks.ts (all repo-integrated);
- q01-r4 section 2 H1;
- docs.stripe.com/get-started/data-migrations/pan-copy-self-serve 'Copied customers have the same customer ID' (verified-vendor-doc via q01-r4 verifier, accessed 2026-11-01);
- C14.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Hold provider IDs only as scoped provenance, and guard structurally so the import path cannot write live provider columns.
- **Spec language:** No Phase 30 profile maps a processor identifier into a column read by a live payment, portal or webhook path (today donors.stripe_customer_id, donor_pledges.stripe_subscription_id, donor_pledges.stripe_payment_method_id). Provider identifiers are held only as provenance scoped by tenant, provider, account, mode and object type. A real-database test proves the import path cannot write those columns. A unit test proves no mapping preset targets them.
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Verified. billing.ts:38-41 opens a Stripe billing portal for donor.stripe_customer_id. recurring.ts:104-112 matches pledges by stripe_subscription_id. In webhooks.ts:386-410, charge.refunded, subscription and invoice events mutate any row whose live provider column matches. An imported ID in those columns would give donors portal control of an external executor and let provider events rewrite history. The mechanism is real. But C14, P16:2581-2583 (external read-only by default) and P16:1314 already forbid this, so critical is inflated. This is an implementation safeguard. (corrected severity: high)

#### v6-17: Platform-key fallback can send a migrated tenant's donations into Asym's own Stripe account at go-live

- **What could go wrong:** resolveTenantStripe falls back to the platform STRIPE_SECRET_KEY when the tenant has no key. Go-live publishes Asym giving pages. A rehearsal or migrated tenant with no tenant key or binding would therefore settle donations on Asym's platform account.
- **Why it matters:** Money lands in the wrong legal account, contradicting Phase 13 D1 ('Asym never in the flow of funds'). It is the hardest kind of error to unwind.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** critical / low
- **Evidence:** - packages/api/src/stripe/tenant-client.ts:64-66 (repo-integrated);
- q01-r4 section 2.2 and H3;
- q01-r5 S8.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Fail closed: go-live requires a verified binding, and the Phase 13 owner removes the fallback for tenant-scoped charges.
- **Spec language:** Go-live of a switch is blocked unless the tenant has a verified settlement binding for every Legal Entity x currency whose giving pages will be published. A tenant-scoped charge path never falls back to platform credentials (test: a tenant with no key or binding gets stripe_unconfigured and no charge).
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Verified that tenant-client.ts:64-66 falls back to the platform env key when the tenant has no key. It predates Phase 30 and affects every tenant, not only migrated ones. Phase 13 owns it. Likelihood is low. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Gate the Switch go-live on a verified tenant processor binding. Route removing the fallback to the Phase 13 owner. It is not a Phase 30 requirement.

#### v6-18: Column defaults and status normalization manufacture false facts on import

- **What could go wrong:** An import writer that omits a column gets a default:
- joined_date becomes today;
- status becomes 'active';
- gift_date becomes today;
- currency becomes 'usd';
- source becomes 'direct'.

The missionary portal also maps any unknown status to 'Active'. Imported partners would look new and healthy, and a CAD gift with a dropped currency becomes USD; if reconciliation reads Asym-side currency, the error reconciles against itself.

- **Why it matters:** These are silent, plausible-looking corruptions that reach missionaries and finance totals, contrary to P16 'Unknown never defaults to healthy'.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** - init_schema.sql:76 and :82; foundation_1_schema.sql:334-338; missionary-portal/donors.ts:191 (repo-integrated);
- P16 PRD :1068 (repo-ratified);
- gh #570 OPEN (Party spine not built), read 2026-11-01.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Import commands write every fact explicitly or record it as unknown, and a test proves no default is consumed.
- **Spec language:** Import commands never rely on column defaults. Every written fact is explicit or recorded as unknown. A real-database test commits an import row with optional source fields absent and asserts that no defaulted joined_date, gift_date, currency, status or source value was written. Imported records with unknown status never display as Active, New, Lapsed or At Risk.
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified. init_schema.sql:76 and :82 default status 'active' and joined_date CURRENT_DATE. foundation_1_schema.sql:334-338 defaults gift_date CURRENT_DATE and currency 'usd'. missionary-portal/donors.ts:191 falls back to 'Active'. P16:1068 says unknown never defaults. A defaulted 'usd' is a false money fact. Caveat: C15 allows defaults at create for Phase 11 catalog fields. The spec should say that those defaults are not allowed for imported source facts. (corrected severity: high)

#### v6-19: The 'reviewed' catch-up can unlock outward effects, and suppression switched off at go-live would expose it

- **What could go wrong:** Several texts combine into a trap:
- The workflow runtime keeps historical events dark 'unless a bounded backfill request was reviewed'.
- Refinement 2 calls the catch-up 'reviewed'.
- Refinement 3 gates darkness at tenant activation, but catch-ups run after it.
- Catch-up gifts are post-boundary, so a rule keyed on 'pre-Asym' or 'historical' may not reach them.
- Phase 7 E3 says imported or historical gifts can be receipted, and the live resend_receipt action has no provenance check.

If darkness is a tenant 'migration mode', the first catch-up could fire thank-yous, first-gift or new-partner tasks, or a second receipt.

- **Why it matters:** Duplicate or wrong donor communications break C23 and donor trust. Whether late-tail gifts may ever earn Asym acknowledgments is still open founder question F5.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** - docs/prds/workflow-studio/03-runtime-and-recovery.md:26 (repo-published);
- roadmap.md:4395;
- P13 D.6 :1031-1035 (repo-ratified);
- P7 PRD :339 (E3);
- decision-log D4 and F5 (:111);
- admin/contribution-operations/action-handlers/resend-receipt.ts:17-55 (repo-integrated).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Bind every catch-up fact to the import-origin outward-effect class and suppress per fact, never per tenant mode. Any outward effect for late-tail gifts becomes a separate, explicitly bounded request decided under F5 and Phase 7.
- **Spec language:** Every catch-up fact carries the import-origin outward-effect class (historical=true for workflow subscriptions; import-origin for Phase 14 holds), whatever its post-boundary occurrence time, together with the source's receipt evidence. Approving a catch-up plan approves data only. Any outward effect for late-tail gifts needs a separate, explicitly bounded backfill request under the F5 decision and Phase 7 rules. Suppression is keyed to the fact's origin and never to a tenant-level migration mode. Live send actions (receipt resend, acknowledgments) refuse import-origin and catch-up facts unless the governing owner rule allows them.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The core point holds. workflow-studio 03:26 keeps historical events dark unless a bounded backfill is reviewed, and P13:1031-1035 sets the receipt boundary. Late-tail gifts are also receipted by SiteStacker itself, so issuing another receipt risks a duplicate. resend-receipt.ts:17-55 shows a manual staff resend path with no import-origin check, which is real but secondary evidence. It overlaps v6-12. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Each catch-up fact carries the import-origin class and is suppressed per fact, including manual resend paths. Any outward effect for late-tail gifts is decided under F5, D4 and Phase 7, not by Phase 30.

#### v6-20: Catch-up can import one charge twice under two IDs, or duplicate a check keyed in both systems

- **What could go wrong:** The base import converges on SiteStacker transaction IDs, while catch-up from PayPal or processor reports (needed for PayPal-run subscriptions) carries gateway IDs. Whether SiteStacker exports include gateway IDs is unverified, and story 171 forbids linking by name, email or fingerprint. So a charge near the boundary either arrives twice or every processor row quarantines. Checks keyed in both Asym Phase 15 batches and SiteStacker during the freeze have no shared ID at all.
- **Why it matters:** Duplicate giving history corrupts totals, statements and first-gift facts.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** - q01-r1 R10 (unverified);
- P13 PRD :539-542 story 171 (repo-ratified);
- q01-r3 PX-2;
- q01-r1 verifier note on PayPal;
- q01-r5 E-20 (no detection).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Declare one gift source per executor x period. Processor reports are reconciliation evidence only unless a real export proves a join key. Post-boundary manual or offline tender rows go to review.
- **Spec language:** Each catch-up population declares exactly one gift source per executor x period (for example, SiteStacker Contributions for cron-executed cards; the PayPal report for PayPal subscriptions). Rows from any other source are reconciliation evidence only, unless a proven shared identifier links them. Post-boundary SiteStacker rows with manual or offline tender go to review as possible dual entry and are never auto-created.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: P13:539-542 (story 171) requires quarantine instead of fuzzy linking. With two possible sources for one charge (a SiteStacker export and a processor report), duplicates are plausible. Declaring one gift source per executor and period is a small, proportionate fix. (corrected severity: high)

#### v6-21: A blunt 'turn it off' checklist breaks card-network duties and misses lists missionaries own

- **What could go wrong:** Following the checklist literally causes harm or misses the target:
- Turning Spark off kills the Mastercard pre-billing reminder while SiteStacker still charges annual gifts.
- Turning off Contribution Receipt and Declined messages leaves tail charges unreceipted.
- Pausing the cron risks an unknown burst of charges on restart.
- 'Mailchimp sync off' does nothing to lists already exported to missionary-owned accounts.
- **Why it matters:** It creates compliance and donor harm through exact obedience to the checklist.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** - SiteStacker 151000122015 (verified-vendor-doc via q01-r4 section 4 and q01-r2: receipts 'after every billing', online cancellation);
- 151000122392 manual one-way missionary export (verified-vendor-doc via q01-r1 verifier);
- q01-r5 K4;
- q01-r2 section 7 (cron restart unverified).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Use a three-state, item-level checklist with a defined set of items kept on through the tail.
- **Spec language:** The attestation checklist has named items, each confirmed_off | kept_on_with_reason | not_used. These are kept on through the tail unless re-homed by an owner ruling: SiteStacker recurring cron; Contribution Receipt; Contributions Declined; the recurring reminder; the donor cancel path. These are turned off: giving forms and checkout; text-to-give replies; non-transactional Spark events and Motion workflows; org Mailchimp sync; SiteStacker imports and rollbacks. Lists exported to missionary-owned Mailchimp accounts are a separate per-missionary item: disconnect, suppression export requested, or recorded out of scope.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The item-level point holds. SiteStacker sends receipts after each billing and supports online cancellation (verified-vendor-doc via the research, which I did not re-fetch). The missionary Mailchimp list is a manual export. 'Card-network duties' cites no card-network rule and is unverified. The supportable claim is that donors on the tail still need receipts and a cancellation path. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Use an item-level checklist (off / kept on through the tail / not applicable), with recurring receipts and donor cancellation kept on until each executor's tail closes. Drop the card-network claim unless a network rule is cited.

#### v6-22: A generic 'Undo batch' or emergency fallback after go-live recreates dual writers

- **What could go wrong:** A 'time-boxed batch undo' on a final switch run after go-live can remove or hide records that Asym-native gifts, withdrawals and edits now reference. Under pressure, a ministry may re-enable SiteStacker forms 'temporarily', creating an active-active writer.
- **Why it matters:** A well-meant recovery causes data loss or a dual writer.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / low
- **Evidence:** - owner-constraints.md:378-385;
- roadmap.md:4395 'permanent dual writers';
- q01-r5 X3 and E-48.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** After go-live, offer only owner-successor corrections. Re-enabling any SiteStacker writer is a recorded founder exception.
- **Spec language:** After go-live, a switch run offers no batch Undo. Corrections use owner successor commands through a prepared, reviewed reversal or correction plan. Re-enabling any SiteStacker writer after go-live is recorded on the Switch as a founder-approved exception with scope and end date, and the overlap monitor treats its writes as expected-but-reviewed.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Re-enabling SiteStacker writers after go-live would break roadmap:4395. But owner-constraints.md:378-385 explicitly allows time-boxed batch undo while no immutable downstream coverage exists. The proposed 'only owner-successor corrections after go-live' contradicts that published rule. Undoing an import batch does not create a dual writer. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Re-enabling any SiteStacker writer after go-live is a recorded founder exception. Batch undo follows owner-constraints.md:378-385 unchanged.

#### v6-23: Intake footguns: partial exports, credential-bearing packages, card-number files

- **What could go wrong:** - An export run by a restricted user is silently partial.
- A full database or WMTEK package brings password hashes, API secrets and processor tokens.
- A ministry uploads a PAN file or Stripe mapping file.
- **Why it matters:** PCI scope expands (P13 story 172) and data is silently lost.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** - P13 PRD :543-545 story 172 (repo-ratified);
- q01-r5 E-26;
- q01-r1 full-database edge case;
- q01-r3 PX-11 and PX-12.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Detect these at intake and quarantine without parsing.
- **Spec language:** Intake rejects and quarantines, without parsing, any file matching card-number (Luhn plus pattern) or bank-credential patterns, credential-bearing table or column names, or unlisted tables, with Phase 29 disposal. Every snapshot file records its exporter's role. Row counts below the recorded screen control count block snapshot acceptance.
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: P13:543-545 (story 172) supports it. But 'quarantine without parsing' contradicts itself: detecting card-number content needs a bounded content scan. Phase 29 already owns scanning and custody (C11). (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Phase 29 intake runs a bounded scan for credential and PAN patterns plus partial-export manifest checks. Matching files are quarantined, no rows are staged, and handling and disposal follow Phase 29 rules.

#### v6-24: One person can declare, commit, sign off and go live, with no record of it

- **What could go wrong:** A rubber-stamped reconciliation ships a wrong total. Small ministries may truly have a single operator (assumption).
- **Why it matters:** Money sign-off goes unreviewed.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** - q01-r6 open question;
- decision-log.md:150 (no capability);
- single-operator reality is an assumption, to be verified by a ministry interview.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Proportionate: when money families are in scope, require a distinct finance approver by default, or record an explicit single-operator acknowledgment.
- **Spec language:** When any money family is in scope, the reconciliation sign-off is recorded by a principal holding the finance sign-off capability. If that principal also committed the run, the Switch records an explicit single-operator acknowledgment.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: No capability exists (decision-log:150). The fix is proportionate: a separate finance approver by default, or a recorded single-operator acknowledgment. That a single operator is the norm is an assumption. (corrected severity: medium)

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Footguns: the 'gifts-only' catch-up leaves out reversals of pre-boundary gifts that happen after the switch (refunds, chargebacks, disputes, ACH returns). P13 PRD:1767 makes contribution_postings append-only, and refund, ach_return and chargeback are posting kinds. P16 PRD:1295 says in-flight attempts can resolve after the boundary (repo-ratified). If only new gifts arrive, imported totals drift and reconciliation passes falsely. The catch-up needs a reviewed reversal lane routed to Phase 13 corrections. Severity high (inferred from the cited text).
- Footguns / other hazards: if the ministry already used Stripe and connects the same account to Asym, Asym's webhook will receive events for SiteStacker-originated charges. packages/api/src/stripe/webhooks.ts:341-410 handles payment_intent, charge.refunded, subscription and invoice events purely by provider ID (repo-integrated). The result is two channels for one charge, alongside the catch-up export. Unmatched events disappear as 'not found' with no gap signal, and matched IDs mutate history. Whether connected-account events reach this endpoint for SiteStacker traffic depends on the Connect topology. That is inferred and I did not verify it against Stripe docs. Severity high.
- Footguns: a switch in the middle of a tax year. Phase 7 receipts and Phase 19 year-end statements must cover gifts SiteStacker already receipted in the same year without issuing new receipts and without leaving statements partial. P13:1031-1035 sets the receipt boundary, D4 leaves the Phase 7 import fact undefined, and C22 requires partial history to be shown as partial. The review does not raise the year-end statement for the switch year. Severity medium-high.
- Testability: v6-18 says 'no default consumed', but C15 (Phase 11, ratified) says 'defaults apply only at create'. The spec must reconcile the two explicitly: catalog defaults must not stand in for imported source facts. Otherwise one ratified rule appears to contradict the other. Severity low.
- Other development hazards: during the declared freeze, SiteStacker giving forms are off but Asym giving is not yet live. Nobody owns donor-facing continuity: where public giving URLs point and how mail-in checks received during the freeze are recorded (O10, Phase 24 giving addresses, Phase 15 offline batches). Gifts can be lost or keyed twice. Severity medium (inferred).

### 5.6 Tenant safety

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v3). **Synthesis verdict:** A does not mix tenants by itself, but legacy provider IDs in live columns would hand Asym live control of a still-running executor. Visibility-bearing legacy columns would widen missionary access. The operator model is undecided and today only global super-admin exists. Shared installations or merchant accounts are out of v1 scope; their existence is unverified.

**What was checked:** Checked these Stripe paths on develop 629cb71dc: tenant resolution (tenant-client.ts:59-66), the donor billing portal (billing.ts:17-42), webhook tenant routing (event-store.ts:105-157, :242; refunds.ts:45-62; recurring.ts:87-121), admin refunds (refunds.ts:84-214) and uniqueness of provider-ID columns (migration grep). Also checked: the switch unit against SiteStacker installation, Site and gateway topology (r1 §3A/B11) and the Phase 13 binding model (P13 :664-667); intra-tenant visibility through the live missionary projection (donors.ts:404-441, :502-507; redaction.ts:38-46); operator cross-tenant authority (identity-and-access.md:218-220; principal/types.ts:47-50; is_super_admin RLS). Stripe Connect webhook docs verified.

#### v3-T1: Legacy provider IDs become live, cross-tenant-routable control

- **What could go wrong:** No Phase 13/14/16 owner targets exist, so v1 historic-gift or catch-up writers would land SiteStacker-era Stripe IDs in donors.stripe_customer_id, donations.stripe_payment_intent_id/stripe_charge_id or donor_pledges.stripe_subscription_id. Four failures follow. (a) Keyless tenants share the platform Stripe account, so a cus_ in tenant B can open another tenant's donor wallet in the Billing Portal. (b) Duplicate payment-intent IDs make webhook .maybeSingle() lookups throw. (c) A ministry onboarded by pasting its legacy Stripe secret key lets Asym refunds and the whole-account portal act on the account where SiteStacker still charges. (d) Copied customers keep the same cus_ on two accounts, and unscoped lookups cannot tell them apart.
- **Why it matters:** It can disclose payment details across tenants and lets Asym mutate a former executor's money objects, bypassing Phase 16 formal cutover (C9) and P13 :733-742 'observation is not control'.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** repo-integrated: tenant-client.ts:59-66 (blank key falls back to platform key); billing.ts:39-42; event-store.ts:150-155 (metadata.tenant_id, then unscoped payment-intent lookup), :242 (event.account stored, not used to route); refunds.ts:45-62; recurring.ts:87-121; migrations have no unique constraint on these columns (only stripe_raw_events_event_unique, 20260512190000:39); admin refunds.ts:140-214 has no provenance guard; init_schema.sql:20 stores a plaintext stripe_secret_key. verified-vendor-doc https://docs.stripe.com/connect/webhooks (2026-11-01): 'Each event for a connected account contains a top-level account property'. r4 verifier: Stripe PAN copy keeps customer IDs.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Keep legacy provider references only in Phase 30 staging and lineage, keyed by tenant, environment, provider, account and mode. Never write them to columns read by webhook, refund, portal, recurring or checkout code. Gate go-live of any migrated tenant on Phase 13/16 removing the platform-key fallback, routing by event.account to the Settlement Account Binding, adding account-scoped uniqueness, and storing no tenant secret key.
- **Spec language:** Legacy processor and SiteStacker identifiers are inert source evidence. Phase 30 stores them only in its staging and lineage records, keyed by tenant, environment, provider, provider account and mode. No Phase 30 run writes them to a field Asym reads to route webhooks, refund, open a portal, charge or resolve a tenant. A migrated tenant cannot go live while any tenant money path can fall back to the platform Stripe key, while webhook tenant resolution uses provider metadata or an unscoped lookup instead of event.account → Settlement Account Binding, or while the tenant has a stored processor secret key. AC: in a two-tenant fixture with one copied cus_ on two accounts, importing tenant B's legacy references changes no column read by donor-portal/billing, stripe/webhooks, stripe/refunds or stripe/recurring.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The cited code checks out (repo-integrated). tenant-client.ts:59-66 falls back to the platform key when the tenant key is blank. billing.ts:39-42 opens a portal for donor.stripe_customer_id. event-store.ts:150-155 takes metadata.tenant_id, then does an unscoped donations lookup by payment intent. event-store.ts:242 stores event.account but does not route on it. refunds.ts:45-62 and recurring.ts:105-121 do unscoped lookups. Only stripe_raw_events_event_unique exists (20260512190000:39). init_schema.sql:20 has the plaintext key. The 'cross-tenant-routable' framing is overstated. Stripe object IDs are unique across Stripe, and the lookups use maybeSingle, so routing to the wrong tenant needs the same legacy ID imported into two tenants (it would then error, not silently misroute). The real hazard is inside one tenant, when the ministry already used Stripe and Asym binds that same account through Connect. Events for objects SiteStacker created then reach Asym's webhook. If legacy sub_/pi_/cus_ IDs sit in donor_pledges/donations/donors, recurring.ts and the refund and portal code act on executors SiteStacker still runs. That is the active-active control P16 :1314 forbids. The proposed gate on Phase 13/16 removing the platform-key fallback restates ratified Phase 13 intent (P13 :664-667 deletes stripe_secret_key). It is a precondition for any tenant going live, not something Phase 30 owns. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Phase 30 keeps legacy provider references only in staging and lineage, keyed by tenant, environment, provider, account and mode. They are never written to live provider-ID columns read by webhook, refund, portal or recurring code. Only the Phase 13/16 adoption commands (#834 classifier) may promote a reference to live control. Record the Phase 13 Settlement Account Binding (no tenant key, no platform-key fallback) as an inherited precondition for go-live, not a new Phase 30 gate.

#### v3-T2: Switch unit undefined when an installation or merchant account spans more than one organization

- **What could go wrong:** An installation-wide CRM export, or a processor report from a shared (fiscal-sponsor or umbrella) merchant account, imported into one Asym tenant brings in another organization's supporters and gifts. Splitting one installation across tenants has no proven partition key, because Person Site semantics are undocumented.
- **Why it matters:** It discloses PII across organizations and produces wrong totals. Per-Legal-Entity × currency reconciliation cannot detect it, because the foreign rows are internally consistent.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / low
- **Evidence:** r1 §3A A2/A3 (Sites own gateways and may be separate legal entities; vendor V9-V11), B11 (Person Site many-to-many, undocumented); P13 :664-667 binding per Tenant × Legal Entity × environment × purpose (repo-ratified); C19; r2/r3/r4 propose processor-report catch-ups. Whether such installations or accounts exist is an assumption, unverified.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Define the v1 unit as one Asym tenant fed by one SiteStacker installation, with dedicated legacy merchant accounts. A census attestation checks this, and anything else is blocked for v1. Reject processor-report rows not attributable to census merchant accounts and mapped designations before staging, keeping only counts.
- **Spec language:** A v1 switch binds exactly one Asym tenant to exactly one SiteStacker installation. Readiness records, with ministry attestation, whether the installation or any legacy merchant account serves an organization other than this tenant's Legal Entities. If it does, the switch is blocked for v1. Catch-up or reconciliation rows that cannot be attributed to a census merchant account and a mapped designation of this tenant are rejected before staging, and only counts and the reason are retained.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The reviewer itself labels it an unverified assumption, with likelihood low. P13 :664-667 does bind per (Tenant, Legal Entity, environment, purpose), so multi-entity installations are a real design consideration. Scoping v1 to one installation per tenant is cheap and right. A high severity is not justified when the scenario's existence is unproven. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: The v1 census records the installation-to-tenant and merchant-account-to-tenant mapping. Shared or multi-organization installations or accounts are out of scope for v1. Processor-report rows that cannot be attributed to a census account are rejected before staging, with counts kept.

#### v3-T3: Intra-tenant visibility widening through legacy columns and lost source restrictions

- **What could go wrong:** The live missionary projection is scoped by the single column donors.missionary_id. It shows notes, tags, birthday, anniversary, spouse and addresses for named donors. A v1 writer mapping SiteStacker notes or tags into donors.notes or donors.tags exposes member-care and security notes to missionaries. Choosing missionary_id from Person Owner or a last designation is an access-control decision hidden in a mapping. Bulk-setting defaultAnonymousToRecipient=false overrides donor anonymity. SiteStacker group restrictions are census-only, and all Asym staff roles have full admin.
- **Why it matters:** Missionaries or junior staff could see member-care notes or the identity of anonymous donors (Phase 10 care/security classes; Phase 25/28 projection rules).
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** repo-integrated: missionary-portal/donors.ts:404-441 (projection incl. notes, tags), :502-507 (missionary_id scoping); redaction.ts:38-46 (anonymous unless the key is explicitly false); permissions.ts:36-41. r1 B12 (notes incl. care, health), C2 (permissions census-only). Decision log §8: no Party targets.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Imports go only through owner commands and never write visibility-bearing or free-text legacy columns. Visibility derives from the Phase 25/28 projection, with anonymity mapped per gift. Sensitive families default to restricted. The plan shows a newly-visible-to summary per missionary and role.
- **Spec language:** No Phase 30 run writes donors.missionary_id, donors.notes, donors.tags, the anonymity keys of donors.giving_preferences, or any successor field that grants a missionary, donor or role visibility; the owning projection derives visibility. Imported notes, activities, files, applications, prayer and care records default to the most restrictive visibility the target owner offers until a reviewer with the classification capability changes it. The no-write plan reports, per missionary and role, how many supporters become newly visible, and commit requires acknowledging it.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified. missionary-portal/donors.ts:404-430 returns notes, tags, birthday, spouse and similar fields to the missionary. :500-507 scopes donors by donors.missionary_id = profileId, so a legacy column carries visibility. An import that writes donors.missionary_id or notes would expose care or health notes (r1 B12). One correction: redaction.ts:38-46 defaults to anonymous unless the value is explicitly false. That default fails closed, so it supports the safe path rather than evidencing a risk. The 'newly-visible-to summary per missionary and role' in the plan is a nice-to-have, not a v1 requirement. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Imports go only through owner commands and never write donors.missionary_id, notes, tags or other free-text or visibility-bearing legacy columns. Visibility comes from the Phase 25/28 projection. Anonymity is mapped per gift, and an unknown value stays anonymous. Sensitive note families default to restricted. A per-missionary visibility diff is optional.

#### v3-T4: Asym-operated switches have no bounded cross-tenant authority

- **What could go wrong:** If Asym staff run switches (staff-led is the norm per r6; assumption), the only integrated cross-tenant mechanism is global, standing is_super_admin RLS plus service-role tools. Operators can plan or commit against the wrong tenant, and one account can read every ministry's staged exports.
- **Why it matters:** Cross-tenant read and write during the highest-risk operation of the product, contrary to the operator boundary in the identity-and-access contract.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** repo-published: identity-and-access.md:218 (operator bounded by an 'exact audited, purpose-bound, time-boxed tenant grant'), :220 (deferred work re-proves at fire time). repo-integrated: principal/types.ts:47-50 PlatformOperatorPrincipal has no consumer (r06 §7.6); 20260508000413:216-243 is_super_admin OR staff membership; #2295 OPEN removes is_super_admin. Who runs a switch is an open founder question (r6).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** The founder names the operator model. If Asym staff operate, each switch requires a per-tenant, time-boxed, purpose-bound operator grant with separate read-raw, commit and sign-off actions. The tenant is bound immutably by the server when the session is created and re-proved for each chunk. No super-admin or service-role shortcut.
- **Spec language:** Every switch, session, plan, run and catch-up carries an immutable tenant set by the server when created, never from request input. A non-member operator acts only under an active, audited, time-boxed operator grant for that tenant and purpose, and expiry or revocation stops further chunks. AC: an operator with grants in tenants A and B cannot commit a plan created in A while acting in B, and a super-admin with no grant sees zero staged rows.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified. identity-and-access.md:217 requires operators to be 'bounded by the exact audited, purpose-bound, time-boxed tenant grant', and :219 requires deferred work to re-prove that grant at fire time. principal/types.ts:46-50 is type-only ('operator plane ... out of scope'). crm RLS (20260508000413:212-243) still ORs is_super_admin until #2295 (OPEN). Who operates a switch is a genuine open founder question. The capability-split part of the fix duplicates v3-D3. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: The founder names the operator model. If Asym staff operate a switch, it requires the ratified per-tenant, time-boxed, purpose-bound operator grant, with the tenant bound by the server when the session is created and re-proved for each deferred chunk. Use no super-admin or service-role shortcut. Capability granularity follows v3-D3.

#### v3-M2: Monitor foreign rows in processor-report catch-ups

- **What could go wrong:** A legacy merchant account turns out to be shared after go-live. Catch-up processor reports then carry another organization's donors and gifts into this tenant.
- **Why it matters:** Late detection of the v3-T2 failure, during a multi-month tail.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / low
- **Evidence:** r2/r3/r4 processor-report catch-up proposals; v3-T2 assumption (unverified).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Count and alert on rejected non-attributable rows per catch-up run, and pause the switch's catch-ups on any hit.
- **Spec language:** Each catch-up run reports the count of rows rejected as not attributable to this tenant's census merchant accounts or designations. A non-zero count pauses further catch-ups for the switch until the switch owner re-confirms the merchant-account census.
- **Bucket:** monitor (signal: Count of catch-up or reconciliation rows rejected as not attributable to this tenant's census merchant accounts or designations, per run, plus a server-side count of rejected rows whose payer identity matches a Party in another tenant (counts only).; threshold: Any non-zero count in a run.; owner: The Phase 30 switch owner named per switch, with the ministry finance contact.; response: Pause further catch-ups for the switch, re-run the merchant-account census with the ministry, and if the account is shared, route the remaining facts through a money-owner-approved partition or finance exception review outside Phase 30.)
- **Skeptic ruling:** duplicate: This is the monitoring tail of v3-T2's rejection rule, and it rests on the same unverified assumption. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Fold into v3-T2: count rejected non-attributable rows in each catch-up run's evidence.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Tenant safety: webhooks mutating imported history on the same Stripe account. If a ministry already used Stripe and Asym binds that account through Connect, events for SiteStacker-era objects reach Asym's webhook (charge.refunded, disputes, subscription updates). event-store.ts:150-155 resolves these by payment intent, and refunds.ts/recurring.ts update matched rows, so imported historical gifts or pledges could be rewritten by the webhook outside import lineage and append-only ledger rules. Fix: Phase 13 must route events for imported or legacy objects to adoption and reconciliation review, not live mutation (repo-integrated evidence; inferred interaction).
- Tenant safety: donor billing portal against a legacy customer. billing.ts:39-42 opens a Stripe billing portal for donors.stripe_customer_id. If an import writes a legacy cus_ ID and the Party is later claimed or mis-merged, a donor could cancel or edit subscriptions SiteStacker still runs, or see another person's saved payment methods. v3-T1 cites billing.ts but does not name this donor-facing path. The portal must not open for un-adopted legacy customers.
- Database/RLS: inside a tenant, the composite FK covers only donations.donor_id (20261025100000:10-13). Imported donations naming missionary_id or fund_id from another tenant are not blocked structurally (no (tenant_id, missionary_id|fund_id) FK found). Phase 30 mapping must check that every resolved target ID is in the same tenant before invoking the command, not rely on the schema.
- Security/privacy: error files and issue-grid downloads re-exported as CSV need formula-injection neutralization and the raw-row capability. Without it, operators opening them in Excel are exposed (check r09 coverage; not raised in v3).
- Security/privacy: decision-log D9/O7 must be corrected to say #2295 does not revoke DML. Also, the review's inference that signup sets app_metadata.tenant_id is wrong: 20260226113000:223-226 reads server-set raw_app_meta_data. Left uncorrected, a later author may over-trust or misread the authorization baseline.

### 5.7 Database, RLS, and authorization safety

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v3). **Synthesis verdict:** The lineage carrier is writable by staff, and #2295 does not revoke that, so D9 and O7 must be corrected. No capability model covers the consequential switch acts, and background chunks act as the nil system actor. Tenant scoping in replay keys and structural backfill limits are spec and safeguard items. Composite-FK standards belong to target owners and are not set by Phase 30.

**What was checked:** Checked crm_record_links grants and policies (20260508000413:200-243; 20261024163000:39-41) against the actual #2295 scope (gh issue body) and authz.current_tenant_id (20260226113000:130-141, :224-226). Checked donations tenant nullability, default and FKs (init_schema.sql:175-191; 20261025100000:10-13, :80-104), the absence of composite FKs on missionary_id, fund_id and donor_pledges, and the absence of uniqueness on Stripe ID columns. Checked service-role and actor handling (tenant-guard.md:3, :230-240; workflows/events.ts:20-22), staff capabilities (permissions.ts:36-41), Phase 29 AC29-D01-04 and AC29-D04-02, and the write-boundary enforceability of refinement 2's never-overwrite rule.

#### v3-D1: Lineage carrier is client-writable and #2295 does not revoke it (corrects D9/O7)

- **What could go wrong:** Per-record lineage (refinement 4) drives replay, convergence, catch-up matching and future Option B. crm_record_links grants INSERT/UPDATE to authenticated. #2295 rewrites the policies to pure-tenant form without revoking grants. Staff, and possibly any user with a tenant JWT claim, can re-point a source ID to another Party. The next replay or catch-up then attaches gifts or history to the wrong person, behind forged-looking provenance.
- **Why it matters:** Lineage is the authority that convergence and never-overwrite rest on. A forgeable lineage silently misattributes money and history inside a tenant.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** repo-integrated: 20260508000413_crm_identity_mapping.sql:206-243 (GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE TO authenticated; staff-membership policies); 20261024163000:39-41. repo-published: #2295 (OPEN) body says 'Each authenticated policy is dropped and recreated as tenant_id IS NOT NULL AND tenant_id = (SELECT authz.current_tenant_id())', service_role unchanged, no revoke. current_tenant_id reads JWT app_metadata.tenant_id (20260226113000:130-141), which signup sets for any user (inferred, :224-226). Decision-log D9/O7 wrongly says #2295 revokes access.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Lineage and bindings are append-only rows written only by the server-side owner command (security-definer RPC or withTenant). Client roles get SELECT at most. File an explicit revoke of client DML (a new O7 ticket). Supersede bindings rather than updating them.
- **Spec language:** Source bindings and per-record lineage are append-only and written only by the owning server command. No client role holds INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE on them. Re-pointing creates a superseding row with actor, reason and run identity. AC: as authenticated staff, missionary and donor of the same tenant, INSERT and UPDATE on the binding store fail with 42501. Decision-log correction: O7, #2295 does not revoke direct DML on crm_record_links; a separate revoke is required.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The core claim is verified. 20260508000413:204-243 grants SELECT, INSERT and UPDATE to authenticated with staff-membership policies, and 20261024163000:39-41 only re-targets those policies TO authenticated. The #2295 body (OPEN) drops and recreates the policies in pure-tenant form, says 'service_role policies are unchanged', and contains no REVOKE. Decision-log D9 (:69) and O7 (:93) say #2295 'revokes direct access', which is wrong, and the correction is valid. The inference that signup sets app_metadata.tenant_id for any user is not supported. 20260226113000:223-226 READS raw_app_meta_data (server-set) and does not set it. #2295 criterion 2 also pins that a same-tenant donor fixture sees 0 rows on converted staff tables. The residual risk is any tenant staff member writing provenance directly, not arbitrary users. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Correct D9 and O7: #2295 narrows the policies but does not revoke DML. Before bindings count as trusted provenance, an explicit owner ticket revokes client INSERT and UPDATE on crm_record_links and crm_merge_candidates. Writes go only through the server-side owner command, and bindings are superseded, not updated.

#### v3-D2: Tenant correctness depends on application code for the rows Option A writes

- **What could go wrong:** A service-role import into today's tables accepts a donation with a missing tenant, which defaults to tenant ...0001. An anonymous (null-donor) historic gift bypasses the composite donor FK. References to another tenant's missionary or fund pass single-column FKs, and pledges have no same-tenant FK. Option A's final import is one huge service-role write per ministry, so one propagation bug reaches every row.
- **Why it matters:** Cross-tenant financial rows are hard to detect, and per-tenant reconciliation can still balance.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / low
- **Evidence:** repo-integrated: init_schema.sql:177 (tenant_id nullable, DEFAULT '00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001'); 20261025100000:10-13 (composite FK skipped when donor_id NULL); no FOREIGN KEY (tenant_id, missionary_id|fund_id) and none on donor_pledges (grep); C6 anonymous = null legal donor; r06 §7.1 withOperation hands a service-role client; tenant-guard.md:3 withTenant exists (positive). P29 AC29-D01-04 composite-key standard (PR #2430 custody volume :18).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Every table written by a Phase 30 run or the owner command it invokes has NOT NULL tenant_id with no default and composite same-tenant FKs. Runs execute only under withTenant(run.tenant_id). Legacy defaulted tables are not import targets.
- **Spec language:** Every table written by a Phase 30 run, or by an owner command it invokes, has a non-null tenant without a default and composite same-tenant references for every foreign key. Runs execute only inside withTenant(run.tenant_id). AC: a run payload with a missing or foreign tenant, or a reference to another tenant's missionary, fund, designation or Party, is rejected by the database (23502, 23503 or 42501), and an anonymous historic gift without a tenant cannot be inserted.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Verified. init_schema.sql:177 makes donations.tenant_id nullable with a default tenant. 20261025100000:10-13 notes the composite FK is unchecked when a column is NULL and closes that only for donor_id. No migration sets tenant_id NOT NULL or drops the default. The fix assigns requirements to target-owner tables (Phase 13 ledger and others) that Phase 30 does not own. Phase 30 only invokes owner commands, so the likelihood is low. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Phase 30 invokes only owner commands that carry an explicit tenant and run under withTenant(run.tenant_id). It records as an inherited dependency that money and Party targets must meet the AC29-D01-04-style composite same-tenant key standard. It does not set schema rules for other phases' tables.

#### v3-D3: No authorization model for the switch's consequential actions

- **What could go wrong:** Freeze and boundary declaration, legacy-state attestation, raw-row access, final commit, reconciliation sign-off, go-live and catch-up commit all default to any staff member, because every subrole has full admin and no Data Tools capability exists. Deferred chunks run as the nil system actor, so a committer who loses access is not re-checked unless the spec requires it.
- **Why it matters:** Unauthorized or unaccountable bulk writes of financial history, and attestations with no identifiable authorized attester.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** repo-integrated: packages/auth/permissions.ts:36-41 ('All staff subroles have full Mission Control admin access in MVP'); r06 §7.4, §7.8; workflows/events.ts:20-22 nil-UUID system actor. repo-ratified/published: C16 (P12 :2249, :2327); identity-and-access.md:220. r6 owner gap (Phase 12).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Distinct capabilities for plan; raw-row and error-file read; per-family commit (money families also need the money owner's capability); boundary; attestation; sign-off; go-live; catch-up commit. Re-prove per chunk, pause visibly on loss, and record every actor. Separation of commit and sign-off is a founder choice.
- **Spec language:** Freeze declaration, legacy-state attestation, final-run commit, reconciliation sign-off, go-live and catch-up commit are separate capabilities, none implied by a generic staff role. Each run chunk re-proves the committing actor's current capability for that target family. A failed re-proof pauses the run in a visible state. The attestation records the attester, their capability and the checklist version.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Verified. packages/auth/permissions.ts:37-41 says 'All staff subroles have full Mission Control admin access in MVP', and workflows/events.ts:18-23 records the nil-UUID system actor. The gap is real and likely. The eight-capability list is overengineered for a discovery-stage v1 and pre-empts Phase 12's capability model. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Require at minimum separate capabilities for running and planning an import, reading raw rows and error files, and committing or signing off. Money-family commits also require the money owner's capability. Re-prove capabilities per chunk and attribute every commit to a human actor, never only the nil system actor. Whether commit and sign-off are separated is a founder decision, routed to Phase 12.

#### v3-D4: Replay and convergence keys must be scoped by tenant and source installation

- **What could go wrong:** SiteStacker IDs are per-installation integers, Stripe copies keep the same cus_ IDs, and refinement 1 replays snapshots. A uniqueness or lookup key of (source system, source ID), or a byte-digest dedupe, collides across tenants or installations. It either fails and leaks existence, or converges onto another tenant's target if the lookup is unscoped under service role, a pattern already present in money code.
- **Why it matters:** A cross-tenant write or disclosure path inside the replay mechanism refinement 1 introduces.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / low
- **Evidence:** r1 K4 (internal Person id; vendor V31); r4 verifier (Stripe copy keeps customer IDs; verified-vendor-doc); repo-integrated unscoped lookups (refunds.ts:45-62; event-store.ts:105-131). repo-ratified/published P29 AC29-D04-02 (no bytes-only business dedupe), AC29-D01-04 (PR #2430 custody volume :58, :18).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Identity key = (tenant, environment, source system, installation or provider account, mode, entity type, source ID). Snapshot identity = (tenant, switch, manifest digest). Convergence lookups always include the tenant and run under withTenant.
- **Spec language:** Source identity for replay and convergence is (tenant, environment, source system, source installation or provider account, mode, entity type, source record ID). Snapshot identity is (tenant, switch, manifest digest). No uniqueness or lookup omits the tenant or the installation/account. AC: two tenants uploading byte-identical files with identical source IDs each converge only within their own tenant, and neither sees an error naming the other.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: This is sound and proportionate. Note that Stripe IDs are already unique across Stripe, so including environment and mode (test or live) matters more than including tenant for provider IDs. Internal SiteStacker Person IDs (r1 K4) do need installation scope. (corrected severity: medium)

#### v3-D5: Catch-up never-overwrite must be structural, not a run flag

- **What could go wrong:** If refinement 2's 'never overwrites supporter details' is a mode flag on a general import command, a mis-set flag or a later profile lets catch-up rows update Party fields. Phase 14's 're-import updates' and owner-constraints :371-372 pull the other way. Legacy tables have no per-field revision for compare-and-set.
- **Why it matters:** An allowed write could turn a valid Party row into a forbidden state, overwriting newer Asym edits with legacy data.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** Refinement 2 (task); repo-published roadmap.md:4349 ('a changed target is a conflict, not permission to overwrite'); owner-constraints.md:371-372; r4 owner gap (Phase 14 re-import updates); init_schema.sql:70-97 (only updated_at).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Catch-up profiles can reach only typed append-only money-fact commands with no Party, contact, consent or relationship update path. Final-run target writes use per-target revision compare-and-set bound into the plan.
- **Spec language:** A catch-up run can invoke only owner commands that append money facts and supersede nothing on a Party. No command reachable from a catch-up profile accepts Party, contact, consent or relationship changes. AC: a catch-up file that changes a supporter's address produces a reviewed discrepancy and zero Party writes.
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: This is consistent with roadmap.md:4349 ('a changed target is a conflict, not permission to overwrite') and with founder refinement 2. It is an implementation safeguard rather than a new finding. (corrected severity: medium)

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Tenant safety: webhooks mutating imported history on the same Stripe account. If a ministry already used Stripe and Asym binds that account through Connect, events for SiteStacker-era objects reach Asym's webhook (charge.refunded, disputes, subscription updates). event-store.ts:150-155 resolves these by payment intent, and refunds.ts/recurring.ts update matched rows, so imported historical gifts or pledges could be rewritten by the webhook outside import lineage and append-only ledger rules. Fix: Phase 13 must route events for imported or legacy objects to adoption and reconciliation review, not live mutation (repo-integrated evidence; inferred interaction).
- Tenant safety: donor billing portal against a legacy customer. billing.ts:39-42 opens a Stripe billing portal for donors.stripe_customer_id. If an import writes a legacy cus_ ID and the Party is later claimed or mis-merged, a donor could cancel or edit subscriptions SiteStacker still runs, or see another person's saved payment methods. v3-T1 cites billing.ts but does not name this donor-facing path. The portal must not open for un-adopted legacy customers.
- Database/RLS: inside a tenant, the composite FK covers only donations.donor_id (20261025100000:10-13). Imported donations naming missionary_id or fund_id from another tenant are not blocked structurally (no (tenant_id, missionary_id|fund_id) FK found). Phase 30 mapping must check that every resolved target ID is in the same tenant before invoking the command, not rely on the schema.
- Security/privacy: error files and issue-grid downloads re-exported as CSV need formula-injection neutralization and the raw-row capability. Without it, operators opening them in Excel are exposed (check r09 coverage; not raised in v3).
- Security/privacy: decision-log D9/O7 must be corrected to say #2295 does not revoke DML. Also, the review's inference that signup sets app_metadata.tenant_id is wrong: 20260226113000:223-226 reads server-set raw_app_meta_data. Left uncorrected, a later author may over-trust or misread the authorization baseline.

### 5.8 Overengineering

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v1). **Synthesis verdict:** The founder decision itself is minimal. The overengineering risk sits in the research layer: a 17-state machine, per-processor parsers, a full-history processor tie-out, an OAuth grant, about 130 bespoke edge-case requirements and a self-serve cockpit. Instead: a light fact-based Switch record with a derived stage, one SiteStacker profile plus processor-export presets, invariants plus a runbook, per-run screens plus a read-only summary until F18 is decided, and nothing built for B beyond identities and a per-family boundary.

**What was checked:** The founder decision and refinement (4) against published requirements: roadmap.md:4363 lineage and batch identity, :4401 acceptance, :4395 boundary and quarantine. Also the research proposals: the r5 §3.2 state machine and K6 per-family boundaries; r2, r3 and r4 processor profiles, processor tie-out and the OAuth observation grant; the edge-case lists in r1, r2, r3, r4, r5 and r6; the r6 §4 switch cockpit and new shared UI. Checked owner-constraints.md:411-413 (independent P20/P21 boundaries), P13 :659 (no OAuth), CONTEXT.md:2845-2846 (operator grant), and the P29 'no blanket new wizard' rule (PR #2430 line 736).

#### v1-07: Persisted 17-plus-state Switch machine and per-family boundary configuration exceed v1 needs

- **What could go wrong:** Most proposed states can be derived from manifests, runs and sign-offs. Persisting them creates a second source of truth that can drift from run states. Per-family CRM boundaries add paths v1 never exercises, because B was rejected.
- **Why it matters:** It adds cost on the critical path and conflicts with the P29 no-blanket-wizard rule.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** r5 :93-95, :69; owner-constraints.md:411-413 (money owners keep their own independent boundaries; repo-published); PR #2430 phase-29-files-documents.md line 736 (repo-ratified, unmerged).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Use a light Switch record that stores facts: tenant and source, one boundary (UTC, IANA zone, civil time), manifest references, attestations, signed report version, live-at, tail closure date and evidence, abort or supersede reason. Derive the displayed stage from these facts. Money owners keep their own boundaries.
- **Spec language:** v1 stores one switch boundary per Switch, applied to every Phase 30 family. Phase 20, Phase 21 and Phase 16 keep their own cutover records. The Switch persists facts, not a workflow state. Displayed stage is derived from recorded facts, and no stage can be set directly. Adding area-by-area switching later must not require changing per-record lineage, run identity or snapshot identity.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The proportionality point is sound: persist facts and derive the displayed stage, with one boundary, and money owners keep their own boundaries (owner-constraints.md:409-413, checked). The supporting cite is wrong. PR #2430 phase-29-files-documents.md:736 (read at the head SHA) is a Phase 29 file-UI rule ('no blanket new wizard, approval queue, CAPTCHA...'). It says nothing about Phase 30 workflow state models. This is a spec-time design preference, not a Q1 issue. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Keep it as a spec note: a light fact-based Switch record with one boundary and a derived stage. Remove the Phase 29 citation.

#### v1-08: Per-processor import profiles, full-history processor tie-out and an OAuth observation grant are disproportionate for v1

- **What could go wrong:** Asym builds and maintains parsers for eight or more gateways and report formats for an unknown first customer. It reconciles the source system's accuracy across closed years, and it reopens the ratified P13 'Do not use OAuth' rule for convenience.
- **Why it matters:** Every external format becomes a maintained adapter. Phase 30 owns fidelity, not money truth.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** r2 Q4, r3 owner gaps and PX-2, r4 §5.1 A, §6 item 3, Q9; roadmap.md:4377 and C1 (repo-published); P13 :659 (verified); decision-log.md:170 'Generic canonical CSV from field catalog'. The OAuth limit applies only to read_write on platform-controlled Standard accounts (https://docs.stripe.com/connect/oauth-standard-accounts via verifier, verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Use one SiteStacker profile plus one canonical external-money-facts template. The hard gate is the import against SiteStacker control totals. Tie out processor statements only for the open fiscal period and the tail, with a ministry attestation for prior periods. No OAuth in v1.
- **Spec language:** v1 has one SiteStacker source profile and one canonical external-money-facts template. It has no per-processor parser. The hard reconciliation gate compares the import with SiteStacker's own control totals. Processor statements are evidence: they are tied out for the ministry's open fiscal period and the recurring tail, and prior periods carry a ministry finance attestation. Asym does not connect to a ministry's legacy processor account in v1.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Rejecting per-gateway parsers, full-history processor tie-out and an OAuth amendment is proportionate. P13 :659 'Do not use OAuth' was checked. The replacement over-corrects. A template that ministry staff fill by hand from processor statements re-keys money facts manually. That loses the exact source-bound external references P13 relies on (stories 170-171) and adds transcription error. A cheaper and safer path is to keep the processor's own export as source bytes and map it through a Phase 30 mapping preset onto a canonical shape. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: v1 ships one SiteStacker profile and one canonical external-money-facts target. Processor CSVs supplied by the ministry are kept as exact source bytes (Phase 29 custody) and mapped through presets, not hand-keyed. Tie-out covers the open fiscal period and the tail; earlier periods carry a ministry attestation. No OAuth in v1.

#### v1-09: About 130 overlapping edge cases risk becoming bespoke requirements instead of invariants plus a runbook

- **What could go wrong:** E-01..E-52, X1-X20, 27 payment cases, PX-1..16, r4 §11 and r6 §10 overlap heavily. One acceptance criterion per scenario yields a huge, contradictory spec and duplicated tests, and runbook items get mistaken for software.
- **Why it matters:** Overlapping criteria drift, and tests multiply without adding assurance.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** Digest edge-case arrays. Existing invariants: r10-gap-g4.md:77 (absent is not deleted); roadmap.md:4365, :4395; P16 :1314; P13 :539-542. roadmap.md:4403 already requires published procedures.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Specify about ten invariants, each with representative production-shaped fixtures. Move operational cases into the published ministry switch runbook.
- **Spec language:** Switch edge cases are specified as invariants with representative fixtures, not as one requirement per scenario. At minimum: (1) a row absent from a later snapshot is never treated as deleted; (2) every fact is classified once against the boundary; (3) imports and catch-ups fire no outward effect; (4) every legacy executor stays external and read-only until a Phase 16 formal cutover; (5) unmatched money waits in match review; (6) a later snapshot converges on source bindings and never applies source-wins; (7) money totals never cross Legal Entity or currency; (8) post-go-live correction is roll-forward only; (9) the catch-up is bounded by a closure date; (10) provider IDs are inert evidence. Operational scenarios live in the published ministry switch runbook.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Invariants plus a runbook is the right shape, and roadmap.md:4403 requires published procedures. However, invariant (8) 'post-go-live correction is roll-forward only' (and §5.1 'any rollback to SiteStacker after go-live' as not in v1) brings in a decision the founder never saw. It can also be read against roadmap.md:4371, which requires showing what can be safely undone. Rolling back to SiteStacker and reversing an Asym batch are different things, and the wording blurs them. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Adopt the invariants approach. Reword (8) as 'no return of system-of-record authority to SiteStacker after go-live without a new founder decision; batch reversal follows roadmap.md:4371 and the target owners'. Surface it to the founder as an explicit question instead of folding it into the record.

#### v1-10: Self-serve ministry switch cockpit designed before deciding who runs a switch

- **What could go wrong:** A large UI build: stage page, readiness view, sign-off artifact, attestation screen, two new shared UI patterns and undefined P12 capabilities. Yet every nonprofit CRM conversion found is staff-led, and the first switches will likely be hand-held.
- **Why it matters:** It is the largest speculative UI cost and needs capabilities no owner has defined.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** r6 :61, :66 (vendor marketing and absence); r6 owner gaps (packages/ui; P12 capabilities); roadmap.md:4389 requires only per-run screens (repo-published); CONTEXT.md:2845-2846 Operator Tenant Grant (repo-published).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Add a frontier question: who operates a switch? Default for v1: an Asym migration specialist under an audited operator grant, with ministry approvals. v1 UI is the per-run screens plus a read-only switch summary.
- **Spec language:** F-new: Who operates a ministry switch in v1: the ministry admin self-serve, an Asym migration specialist under an Operator Tenant Grant, or a partner? Until this is decided, the v1 switch UI is limited to the per-run plan, commit, progress and result screens plus a read-only switch summary.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Valid. roadmap.md:4389 requires only per-run screens. CONTEXT.md:2845-2846 defines operator access as a time-boxed Operator Tenant Grant. 'Who operates a switch' is a real gap in the frontier and should be asked, not assumed. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Add the frontier question. Defer the cockpit, as proposed.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Problem validity: the founder asked specifically about ministries that already used Stripe, and the review does not handle that case. If SiteStacker charged through the ministry's own Stripe account, binding Asym to that same account is not 'a different account' under P16 :1314. The path is then proof-gated adoption (#711 'adopt-existing-Stripe', #834 classifier, both OPEN), not donor re-authorization. v1-20's assumption that every gift needs a donor step and v1-11's tail length may both be wrong for that cohort. The reverse hazard: once Asym is bound to the same account, Asym's Stripe event ingestion could record SiteStacker-run charges as Asym gifts, and the catch-up would then duplicate them. Whether SiteStacker uses Stripe Billing subscriptions or its own scheduler on stored payment methods is unverified. Severity high; resolve before recording through the per-ministry census.
- Operational burden: receipts and statements for tail gifts are not tied together. SiteStacker receipts what it charges during the tail (v1-15). Unless each catch-up gift carries source-bound already_receipted evidence (P13 story 170) and a Phase 7 import fact (D4, undefined), P19 cumulative coverage (phase-19 :944 'no existing current official coverage') could include those gifts in an Asym year-end statement and duplicate SiteStacker's receipts. Severity high. Owner: P7, P13 and P19 (F13, F5).
- Problem validity: the review's own §5.1 proposed record adds 'any rollback to SiteStacker after go-live' as not in v1 and invariant (8) 'roll-forward only'. That decision was never put to the founder. It should be asked explicitly, not folded into the amended record. Severity medium.
- Problem validity: no owner text defines a Phase 30 'Switch' (r10-gap-g4: 'no owner text sets the CRM switch model'). Recording A creates new Phase 30 scope (switch record, attestations, boundary) beyond roadmap.md:4363-4403. Under C3 that needs a roadmap and owner-constraints amendment at spec time. v1-12 covers only the processor half. Severity medium.
- UX/user friction: the staff CRM blackout is not addressed. Between 'pause editing in SiteStacker' and go-live, staff cannot record contacts, notes or address changes in either system. v1-16 covers giving only. The rehearsals should measure and time-box the pause, with a holding procedure for interactions during it. Severity medium.
- Operational burden: practice runs that change nothing still put a prospect's real donor PII into Asym before Phase 29 custody exists (PR #2430 unmerged) and possibly before any contract. F2 and F4 raise this, but the review does not connect it to Option A's rehearsal step. Severity medium. Already on the frontier as F2/F4.

### 5.9 UX/UI and user friction

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v1). **Synthesis verdict:** The data move is low-friction for donors. The friction is concentrated at four points: the 'off' attestation would cut messages that keep legacy gifts alive; switching forms off before Asym giving exists causes a giving outage; missionaries see false signals from live code and backfill lag; and tail donors face two systems, some cannot be contacted, and attrition is unmeasured. Fixes: the Kept-on list, a giving-continuity choice, display gates, one handover message per donor, and per-cohort monitoring.

**What was checked:** Re-fetched SiteStacker 151000122015 (receipt after every billing, cancellation method, Contribution Receipt and Recurring Scheduling Confirmation must be on, 7-30 day pre-billing notice via the Spark Recurring Schedule Reminder), 151000183830 (declined email with update-and-rerun link) and 151000191779 (recurring without login); all verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01. Live code: donors.ts:173-191, consent.ts:15-20, :81-85, :199-212, init_schema.sql:76, :82, foundation_1_schema.sql:334, tenant-client.ts:64-66. Owner text: P28 finance-stewardship.md:50-53, P16 :1068, :1230-1236, :1316, CONTEXT.md:5043, roadmap.md:4365. r6 §1-§4 and §9 with verifier corrections; FTC and GOV.UK via r6.

#### v1-15: Refinement (3) and r6 §9 would turn off SiteStacker messages that keep legacy recurring gifts alive and compliant

- **What could go wrong:** Gating on 'Spark automations off' (and r6's 'confirmed off' list) switches off several things while SiteStacker still charges: per-billing receipts with the cancellation path, the Mastercard 7-30 day pre-billing notice, and the declined-payment update-and-rerun link. Annual donors lose required notices and expiring cards silently end gifts. 'Mailchimp sync off' cannot reach missionary-owned lists.
- **Why it matters:** Donor drop-off with no visible failure, plus a card-network compliance gap for the merchant (the ministry).
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** 151000122015 'Merchants must send a receipt by email ... after every billing', 'must provide an online or electronic cancellation method', 'The Contribution Receipt notification must be on', 'at least seven days, but no more than 30 days, before the billing date' via the Spark Recurring Schedule Reminder. 151000183830 'reprocessed automatically'. 151000122392 missionary Mailchimp is a manual one-way export (via r1 verifier). All verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01. r2 edge case and r4 G2/G3 agree. Mastercard primary text not read.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Split the attestation into three lists: OFF at the boundary (forms, links and text-to-give, appeal and record-changing automations, Mailchimp sync, imports and rollbacks, staff edits); KEEP ON until handover (recurring engine, legacy receipts, recurring confirmations, declined message, schedule reminder, donor cancel and update); OUTSIDE ministry control (missionary Mailchimp lists). SiteStacker alone receipts what it charges.
- **Spec language:** Before Asym sending or automations activate, the ministry attests per item, with actor and time, that SiteStacker giving forms and checkout, giving links and text-to-give targets, appeal and record-changing automations, Mailchimp sync, imports and rollbacks, and staff CRM editing are off. Until each legacy recurring cohort is handed over or ended, the ministry keeps SiteStacker's recurring engine, its receipts for legacy charges, its recurring confirmation and update messages, its declined-payment message with the update link, its recurring schedule reminder and its donor cancellation path running. SiteStacker alone receipts gifts it charges. Missionary-owned Mailchimp lists are listed as outside ministry control.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Re-fetched 151000122015 (verified-vendor-doc, accessed 2026-11-01, modified 19 Sep 2023). It confirms the receipt after every billing, the cancellation method, the 'Contribution Receipt notification must be on', the 'Recurring Scheduling Confirmation notification must be on', and the 7-30 day reminder through Spark. Scope: the article is specific to PaymentSpring and Mastercard. The pre-billing notice applies only to plans billed less often than every six months, and whether it applies under other gateways is unverified. The direction is correct: refinement (3) as worded would switch off messages that keep SiteStacker-charged gifts recoverable and compliant. The three-list split is proportionate. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Adopt the OFF / KEEP ON / OUTSIDE-CONTROL split. State that the card-network obligations follow SiteStacker's documentation for the ministry's gateway, to be confirmed in the per-ministry census. Treat SiteStacker receipts for tail gifts as already_receipted evidence in the catch-up.

#### v1-16: Turning giving forms off at the freeze, before Asym giving is live, creates a giving outage

- **What could go wrong:** In r5's lifecycle, forms go off at S3 but Asym giving pages are published only at S8, after export, checks, import, reconciliation and sign-off. Donors meet dead forms and QR codes for days. The P13 Connect topology is unbuilt (plaintext-key prototype).
- **Why it matters:** Lost one-time gifts and missionary income, worst in peak season.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** r5 :137, :182-187 (proposed ordering); decision text; tenant-client.ts:64-66 (repo-integrated); C20 human publish issues Giving Addresses (decision-log.md:50, repo-ratified).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Make 'Asym giving live and verified' a precondition of the boundary. Forms go off at the same moment Asym giving and exact redirects go live. Pre-import Asym donors are resolved in the final plan's match review, with no auto-merge.
- **Spec language:** A switch boundary may be declared only when the ministry's Asym giving pages are published, its Phase 13 Settlement Account Binding is verified, and exact redirects for SiteStacker giving URLs are ready. SiteStacker giving forms go off at the boundary, never before Asym giving is live. Donors who give in Asym before the final import completes are resolved in the final plan's match review, with no automatic merge.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The outage risk is valid if forms go off at the freeze. But making 'Asym giving live' a precondition of the boundary couples the switch to the unbuilt Phase 13 connected-account topology. The current code is a plaintext-key prototype with platform fallback (tenant-client.ts:64-66). That compounds v1-02. A simpler option exists with machinery already proposed: leave SiteStacker one-time forms on until Asym giving is live, and bring gifts from that gap in through the reviewed catch-up. The founder should choose between these. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Record the requirement 'no giving outage'. Offer two sequencings: (a) Asym giving and redirects go live at the boundary; or (b) SiteStacker forms stay on until Asym giving is live, and gifts in that gap come through the catch-up. Either way, pre-import Asym donors go to match review with no auto-merge (C2).

#### v1-17: Missionaries see false lapsed, joined-today or support-dropped signals from live code and catch-up lag

- **What could go wrong:** Live code renders stored 'lapsed'/'at risk' as badges and maps unknown status to 'Active'. Schema defaults set joined_date and gift_date to today and status to 'active'. Monthly catch-up lag shows missing months. External read-only arrangements fall into an 'attention' bucket.
- **Why it matters:** Missionaries depend on this view for their livelihood. False signals cause anxiety, wrong outreach to long-time partners and loss of trust in Asym in the first weeks.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** packages/api/src/missionary-portal/donors.ts:173-191; supabase/migrations/20250101000000_init_schema.sql:76, :82; 20260214090000_foundation_1_schema.sql:334 (repo-integrated, verified). phase-28 finance-stewardship.md:50-53 'SHALL NOT label ... lapsed'; P16 :1068 'Unknown never defaults to healthy, lapsed, zero, or received'; P16 :1316 (repo-published, verified). r4 G15; r6 R19-R20.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Before any migrated tenant goes live: retire or coverage-gate person-level lapse labels; make import writers set explicit values or unknown; add a P16 D13/P28 'planned external tail' display with a last-updated date; set a catch-up cadence that keeps that date honest.
- **Spec language:** No migrated tenant goes live while the missionary donor list can render a person-level lapse label or default an unknown status to Active. No import or catch-up writer relies on a column default for joined, status or gift dates. Legacy recurring gifts in a planned external tail are shown to missionaries as given through the previous system, with the last catch-up date, and never as lapsed, missed or needing attention solely because of catch-up lag.
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
