- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Checked. donors.ts:173-191 maps unknown status to 'Active' and renders stored 'lapsed'. init_schema.sql has status DEFAULT 'active' and joined_date DEFAULT CURRENT_DATE. foundation_1 gift_date defaults to CURRENT_DATE. finance-stewardship.md:52-53 says the display 'SHALL NOT label ... lapsed'. P16 :1068 says 'Unknown never defaults to healthy, lapsed, zero, or received'. The display rules are owned by P28 and P16. Phase 30's part is the import-writer requirement plus a go-live gate. Applying P16 :1316 to external arrangements is inferred. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: As proposed. Split it into a Phase 30 import-writer rule (no reliance on column defaults) and a P28/P16 go-live dependency for display.

#### v1-18: Tail donors face two systems, phishing-shaped requests, and some cannot be contacted at all

- **What could go wrong:** History sits in Asym while recurring control stays in SiteStacker. SiteStacker update-card emails and Asym handover requests together look like phishing. The consent gate blocks transactional mail for do_not_contact and for bounce, spam and manual suppressions, so those gifts end silently. Some recurring donors have no SiteStacker login, and imported donors get no Asym account automatically.
- **Why it matters:** Re-authorization is where drop-off happens, and silent endings break P13 'no silent gap'.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** 151000183830 and 151000191779 (verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01); FTC phishing guidance via r6 M2 (verified-vendor-doc); packages/api/src/email/consent.ts:15-20, :81-85, :199-212 (repo-integrated, verified); roadmap.md:4365; r3 §4.3 (no measured attrition).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** SiteStacker stays the front door for legacy recurring changes during the tail, and Asym shows those arrangements as 'managed by previous system'. Send one handover message per donor through a P16/P17 contract with a 'sign in at the ministry site' path. List uncontactable donors for an owner ruling, never a Phase 30 bypass.
- **Spec language:** During the recurring tail, donors change or cancel legacy recurring gifts in SiteStacker, and Asym shows them as managed by the previous system. Each donor receives at most one handover request covering all their legacy arrangements, sent under a Phase 16/17 message contract. Donors whose consent or suppression state blocks transactional email are listed for an owner-approved path and are never contacted through import tooling.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: It overlaps v1-15 ('SiteStacker stays the front door') and v1-20 (handover). The distinct and verified part: consent.ts:15-20, :81-85 and :199-212 block even transactional email for do_not_contact and bounce, spam or manual suppression. 151000191779 shows recurring donors can exist without a login. Treating every Asym request as phishing-shaped is reasonable advice but has no measured basis. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Keep only the parts that do not overlap: one handover message per donor under a P16/P17 contract, the 'managed by previous system' display, and an owner ruling for uncontactable donors with no Phase 30 bypass.

#### v1-19: 'Freeze' and switch-time wording and time-zone handling

- **What could go wrong:** 'Freeze', 'midnight', mixed staff time zones or a DST change make staff stop editing at different moments and misclassify boundary-day gifts. 'Universal external freeze' is on the glossary Avoid list. SiteStacker's export time zone is undocumented.
- **Why it matters:** Confusing language at the highest-stakes moment, and misclassified gifts.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** low / medium
- **Evidence:** CONTEXT.md:5043 (repo-published, verified); GOV.UK A-Z style guide on midnight via r6 X3 (verified-vendor-doc); r5 K10, §3.1 (export time zone unverified).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Say 'switch time' for the recorded boundary and 'pause editing in SiteStacker' for the ministry action. Use a civil-day-aligned boundary in a named IANA zone, showing offset and viewer-local time. Send timestamps of unknown zone near the boundary to review.
- **Spec language:** The switch time is recorded as the start of a civil day in the ministry's named time zone and is shown with zone, UTC offset and the viewer's local time. User-facing copy uses 'switch time' and 'pause editing in SiteStacker', not 'freeze' or 'midnight'. Facts within one day of the switch time whose time zone is unknown go to review.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Mis-cite: the CONTEXT.md:5042 Avoid list ('date-only go-live ... universal external freeze') belongs to the Phase 21 'Field Account Operational Cutover' term. It is not a general glossary rule for a Phase 30 switch, though it is useful by analogy. Wording and time-zone precision are spec copy concerns. Mandating that the boundary align to the start of a civil day is a sensible default, not a requirement. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Spec copy note: 'switch time' vs 'pause editing in SiteStacker'; store the UTC instant plus the IANA zone; route timestamps near the boundary with an unknown zone to review. Cite the Phase 21 term as an analogy only.

#### v1-20: Handover attrition has no benchmark; monitor per cohort instead of assuming a number

- **What could go wrong:** Ratified P16 requires a donor-completed step for every moved recurring gift, and no measured attrition figure exists (vendor claims are marketing or unsourced). A cohort can quietly lose a large share of missionary support.
- **Why it matters:** It is the biggest money-at-risk variable in Option A for recurring-heavy ministries.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** P16 :1230-1236 'Complete with donor now' for replace/bind method; 'No bulk action may ... bind methods ... or restart donors' (repo-ratified, verified). r3 §4.3 (Tithe.ly 75%+ is vendor marketing); r6 P7 (Whole Whale 70-80%, unsourced).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Make handover completion a monitored per-cohort, per-missionary measure with a set response, and feed real rates back into the Design 1 vs proved-continuation decision.
- **Spec language:** Each recurring handover cohort reports its confirmation rate and amount at risk per missionary by day since the request. Crossing the agreed threshold triggers personal follow-up and a recorded decision to extend that cohort's external tail. Measured rates are reported to the founder after each switch.
- **Bucket:** monitor (signal: Share of a cohort's legacy recurring arrangements confirmed in Asym, and unconfirmed monthly recurring amount per missionary, by days since the handover request; threshold: Below 60% confirmed at day 21, or any missionary with more than 25% of monthly recurring support unconfirmed at day 21 (proposed starting values; recalibrate after the first switch); owner: Phase 16 owner with the ministry's named switch owner; response: Personal follow-up via missionaries or staff (as Phase 28 visibility permits); extend that cohort's external tail before the closure date; report measured rates to the founder for the Design 1 vs proved-continuation decision)
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: P16 :1230 lists 'replace/bind method' under 'Complete with donor now' (checked), and no attrition benchmark exists. The 60% and 25% at 21 days thresholds are invented starting values and should be labelled as assumptions. The concern assumes every moved gift needs a donor-completed step. That may not hold where the ministry already uses its own Stripe account and adoption on the same account is proved under #711 and #834 (see missed concerns). This is monitoring, not a change to the decision. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Monitor handover completion per cohort and per missionary. Leave thresholds to be set after the first switch, labelled as assumptions. Report the measured rates back to the founder.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Problem validity: the founder asked specifically about ministries that already used Stripe, and the review does not handle that case. If SiteStacker charged through the ministry's own Stripe account, binding Asym to that same account is not 'a different account' under P16 :1314. The path is then proof-gated adoption (#711 'adopt-existing-Stripe', #834 classifier, both OPEN), not donor re-authorization. v1-20's assumption that every gift needs a donor step and v1-11's tail length may both be wrong for that cohort. The reverse hazard: once Asym is bound to the same account, Asym's Stripe event ingestion could record SiteStacker-run charges as Asym gifts, and the catch-up would then duplicate them. Whether SiteStacker uses Stripe Billing subscriptions or its own scheduler on stored payment methods is unverified. Severity high; resolve before recording through the per-ministry census.
- Operational burden: receipts and statements for tail gifts are not tied together. SiteStacker receipts what it charges during the tail (v1-15). Unless each catch-up gift carries source-bound already_receipted evidence (P13 story 170) and a Phase 7 import fact (D4, undefined), P19 cumulative coverage (phase-19 :944 'no existing current official coverage') could include those gifts in an Asym year-end statement and duplicate SiteStacker's receipts. Severity high. Owner: P7, P13 and P19 (F13, F5).
- Problem validity: the review's own §5.1 proposed record adds 'any rollback to SiteStacker after go-live' as not in v1 and invariant (8) 'roll-forward only'. That decision was never put to the founder. It should be asked explicitly, not folded into the amended record. Severity medium.
- Problem validity: no owner text defines a Phase 30 'Switch' (r10-gap-g4: 'no owner text sets the CRM switch model'). Recording A creates new Phase 30 scope (switch record, attestations, boundary) beyond roadmap.md:4363-4403. Under C3 that needs a roadmap and owner-constraints amendment at spec time. v1-12 covers only the processor half. Severity medium.
- UX/user friction: the staff CRM blackout is not addressed. Between 'pause editing in SiteStacker' and go-live, staff cannot record contacts, notes or address changes in either system. v1-16 covers giving only. The rehearsals should measure and time-box the pause, with a holding procedure for interactions during it. Severity medium.
- Operational burden: practice runs that change nothing still put a prospect's real donor PII into Asym before Phase 29 custody exists (PR #2430 unmerged) and possibly before any contract. F2 and F4 raise this, but the review does not connect it to Option A's rehearsal step. Severity medium. Already on the frontier as F2/F4.

### 5.10 Source of truth, ownership, and domain invariants

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v4). **Synthesis verdict:** 'Only place' conflicts with ratified 'observation is not control'. Record the authority statement and the split between live and closed. Freeze and reconcile are assigned to an unowned P13 workstream, so propose the split and route owner edits. 'Never wins over Asym edits' is the wrong conflict model for append-only money; contradictions go to owner exceptions. Out-of-scope modules can leave SiteStacker as a live writer, which needs a census question and a founder rule.

**What was checked:** Which system holds each authoritative fact before, at and after the switch. Whether the wording 'Asym becomes the only place' matches the ratified money contracts. Who owns the freeze and reconciliation. Whether refinements (1) and (2) conflict with published clauses. Sources: P13 :159-165, :720-747, :1028-1037, :2192-2199; P16 :1264, :1314, :1316, :2581-2593; ADR-0058 :22-24; ADR-0106 :83-97; CONTEXT.md:5042; owner-constraints.md:370-371, :410-413, :424-426; P14 :1864-1866; roadmap :4363, :4377, :4395; platform-product-intent.md:315-316; #711 body (gh, 2026-11-01).

#### v4-01: 'Asym becomes the only place' is false by fact family; record an authority matrix

- **What could go wrong:** Staff and builders read the claim as Asym controlling everything from switch day. Asym then shows recurring gifts as stopped while SiteStacker's scheduler, PayPal or a gateway-native schedule keeps charging. Staff try to refund or cancel legacy charges from Asym. Reconciliation is declared complete while the old processor is still producing refunds, disputes and returns.
- **Why it matters:** It contradicts ratified money contracts. Observation is not control. External executors stay read-only until a formal cutover. Asym cannot lock external writers. Recording the claim as written records a promise the money owners have already ruled out.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** repo-ratified: P13 :735-737 ('may keep operating while it is observed, but observation is not control'); P16 :1264 (Externally controlled/read-only); P16 :1314 (formal cutover needs proof the old executor stopped); ADR-0058 :22-24; CONTEXT.md:5042 (avoid 'universal external freeze', 'date-only go-live'). Matches q01-r5 K1 and q01-r4 §7. That SiteStacker's scheduler is the executor is inferred (q01-r2; the verifier says it cannot be confirmed from public code).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Record Q1 with an authority matrix by fact family, measured from the Switch Boundary. Asym owns Party, contact, consent, relationships, custom data and staff work. The original processor owns settlement, refund, dispute and return of the charges it executed. Legacy executors own their schedules until a P16 formal cutover. Each legacy cohort has exactly one receipt issuer. P20 and P21 keep their own boundaries. Separate 'live' (supporter authority has moved) from 'closed' (every executor is stopped or cut over and the tail is closed).
- **Spec language:** From the Switch Boundary, Asym is the system of record for supporter identity, contact data, consent, relationships, custom data and staff work for the migrated Tenant. Asym does not control any legacy executor. Each legacy recurring arrangement remains Externally controlled/read-only (P16 K.1) until its own Phase 16 formal cutover. The original processor remains authoritative for the settlement, refund, dispute and return facts of every charge it executed, and Asym records those facts only through owner commands as append-only evidence. A Switch is 'live' when supporter authority has moved. It is 'closed' only when every legacy executor is proved stopped or formally cut over and the tail is closed.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Evidence holds (repo-ratified). P13 phase-13...:735-737 says 'may keep operating while it is observed, but observation is not control'. P16 :1263-1264 has the Externally controlled/read-only row, and :1314 requires formal cutover. But the founder's own refinement (4) already hands recurring and processor work to Phase 16, so 'false' overstates the case. The problem is the wording 'only place for supporters and their history', not the model itself. A full authority matrix across every fact family is heavier than Q1 needs. The useful new point is the split between 'live' and 'closed'. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Reword the Q1 record. Asym becomes the system of record for supporter/CRM data and imported history at the Switch Boundary. Processors and legacy executors stay authoritative for the charges, refunds and disputes they executed until a P16 formal cutover. P20/P21 boundaries stay independent (owner-constraints.md:406-413). Record 'live' (supporters moved) separately from 'closed' (every executor stopped or cut over and the tail closed). Leave the full matrix to the spec.

#### v4-02: Child sponsorship and other out-of-scope modules can keep SiteStacker as a permanent live writer for shared supporters

- **What could go wrong:** Asym excludes child sponsorship from the whole program, so a ministry that runs sponsorship keeps SiteStacker for it. Sponsors who also give to missionaries are then edited and charged in SiteStacker and in Asym. Unsubscribes and address changes diverge, and new sponsors never reach Asym. The same happens with any SiteStacker form, event or application, or SendGrid inbound capture, that keeps creating CRM people. In effect this is Option C, permanently, for that group.
- **Why it matters:** The roadmap forbids permanent dual writers. Consent and do-not-contact floors (C10) cannot be honoured across two live systems. C12 says Asym owns CRM truth. No research track treated this as a source-of-truth problem: r2 X26 points to a 'sponsorship owner' that does not exist in Asym.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** repo-published: platform-product-intent.md:315-316 ('Child sponsorship is out of scope'); roadmap.md:4395 ('permanent dual writers'); q01-r1 :247 (D24), :260 (Spark used 'primarily … [for] Child Sponsorship', via the r07 vendor doc), :435 (X5 mixed checkouts); q01-r2 :350; q01-r5 E-47. How common sponsorship is among target ministries: unverified (assumption).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Make residual live writers a census item and an eligibility gate. For v1 the founder chooses between two options. (a) Such ministries are not eligible. (b) A Residual External System declaration: SiteStacker stays system of record only for the named out-of-scope domain, with no sync either way, a stated count of overlapping people, and the ministry's written acceptance.
- **Spec language:** A Switch may be scheduled only when the census shows no SiteStacker module that will, after the Switch Boundary, create or update CRM people, contact data, consent or money for any person in the migrated population. Otherwise the founder must approve a Residual External System declaration naming the module, the out-of-scope domain, the overlapping-person count and the ministry's acceptance that Asym will not receive its changes. No v1 sync, refresh import or write-back exists for a residual system.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Evidence is accurate. openspec/contracts/platform-product-intent.md:315-316 says 'Child sponsorship is out of scope', and roadmap.md:4395 says not to promise permanent dual writers. But decision-log C13 (:43) already requires a visible exclusion for sponsorship records. What is genuinely new is that SiteStacker could stay a live writer for shared supporters. How often target ministries run sponsorship is unverified, so likelihood is an assumption. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Add one census question: does any out-of-scope module keep writing to people we migrate? For v1 the founder picks between ineligible and a declared residual system with no sync and written acceptance. The overlap count is part of the census, not a separate gate.

#### v4-03: Freeze, bulk reconciliation and processor coordination are still assigned to an unnamed Phase 13 workstream; Option A takes them by shorthand

- **What could go wrong:** The Phase 30 spec writes the freeze and reconciliation steps, but P13 text still assigns them elsewhere. Every owner then assumes someone else builds the processor half: coordination, vault transfer, reauthorization, connected-account readiness and executor replacement at scale. The switch ships with no path for recurring donors. #711 also routes broader historical import to Phase 36, which collides with Phase 30.
- **Why it matters:** C3 forbids a consumer taking a producer's authority by shorthand; the clause itself must be edited. The money-side duties have no owner.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** repo-ratified: P13 :743-746, :165, :2192-2199 ('cutover freeze and reconciliation, connected-account readiness, and recurring-executor replacement at scale'). repo-published: #711 body ('reserves broader historical import delivery to Phase 36'); roadmap :4363, :4377; document-authority.md:10-13. The verifier grep confirms the processor items are unassigned (q01-r4 §6). That the CRM ETL half belongs to Phase 30 is inferred.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Record a proposed split and route the clause edits before the Phase 30 spec. Phase 30: boundary record, attestation evidence, census, manifests, CRM and history reconciliation report, catch-up transport. Phase 13: legacy reference model, money control totals and source basis, commands for post-boundary externally collected money. Phase 16: classification, non-Stripe stop proof, confirmation or reauthorization, executor replacement. Operations runbook: processor coordination and vault logistics. Reconcile #711's Phase 36 routing.
- **Spec language:** Q1 adopts a ministry-declared freeze and reconciliation only within Phase 30 scope: boundary record, attestation evidence, census, manifests, and the CRM and history reconciliation report. Processor coordination, vault transfer, reauthorization, connected-account readiness and executor replacement remain with the Phase 13 'separate migration workstream' until P13 :743-746 and :2192-2199 are edited to name their owners. The Phase 30 spec cannot be published while those clauses assign freeze and reconciliation to an unnamed owner.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified (repo-ratified). P13 :743-745 gives 'processor coordination, credential-vault transfer, reauthorization campaigns, cutover freezes, and bulk reconciliation' to an unnamed 'separate migration workstream'. :2192-2199 repeats it as a reserved seam. The #711 body (gh, 2026-11-01) sends broader historical import to Phase 36. Option A's freeze and reconciliation steps take this work over without any clause assigning it. This is a real ownership gap. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Record it as an ownership question for the founder, with the reviewer's split as a proposal, not a decision: Phase 30 gets transport, census, manifest and reconciliation reports; P13/P16 keep their money and executor commands; an operations runbook covers processor and vault logistics. Also reconcile the Phase 36 routing in #711. Do not pre-write clause edits during discovery.

#### v4-04: Refinement (2) 'gifts-only, never wins over Asym edits' is the wrong conflict model for append-only money and has no owner command or receipt rule

- **What could go wrong:** Gifts-only leaves out refunds, reversals, disputes, returns and stops, so Asym overstates received money. 'Never wins' is field-level last-writer-wins language and decides nothing for append-only facts. No owner command exists for gifts collected after the boundary, because P13 D.6 covers pre-Asym gifts only. Receipting is split across several rules: already_receipted, a Phase 7 import fact with only two values, P7 E3 'can be receipted', and P14 holding import acknowledgments forever. Catch-up gifts can therefore get two receipts, none, or never be thanked.
- **Why it matters:** Money truth and donor-facing receipt compliance. The roadmap gives a seam (a bounded reviewed backfill) but no money command and no receipt rule.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** repo-ratified: P13 :726-727, :1028-1037 ('stamped with a pre-Asym provenance boundary'; the import fact has two values); P14 :1489-1490; phase-07 :339 (E3); roadmap :4395 ('Reviewed backfill is a separate bounded operation'). q01-r4 G1/G2 (the verifier notes owner-constraints :424-426 applies only to a standing feed); q01-r5 K2/K4; q01-r2.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Reshape refinement (2) as a reviewed legacy money-and-executor catch-up. It carries only append-only facts through Phase 13/16 owner commands, and each run is a separate bounded backfill. Contradictions go to owner exceptions. Each cohort declares one receipting system before its first run.
- **Spec language:** The post-boundary legacy catch-up carries only append-only money and executor-state facts for legacy executors: succeeded charges, refunds, reversals, disputes, ACH returns, failed attempts and stops. Each fact enters through a Phase 13 or Phase 16 owner command defined for externally collected post-boundary facts. It never creates, updates or deletes Party, contact, consent or relationship data. A catch-up fact that contradicts an Asym-held fact is never applied by precedence; it becomes an owner exception. Before the first catch-up run, each legacy cohort declares exactly one receipting system for its charges (SiteStacker, or Asym under a Phase 7 rule), recorded in run evidence.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified. P13 :1026-1038 says imported money is pre-advanced with provenance and a separate Phase 7 receipt-boundary fact. P14 :1487-1490 says imports stay held. roadmap.md:4395 says 'Reviewed backfill is a separate bounded operation'. 'Never wins over Asym edits' is a CRM field-conflict rule and does not fit append-only money. It also leaves receipting open for gifts SiteStacker charges after the boundary. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: As proposed, but say it plainly: catch-up appends money facts only through P13/P16 owner commands, each run is a separate reviewed backfill, contradictions go to owner exceptions, and each cohort names one receipting system before its first run.

#### v4-05: Refinement (1) narrows the published 're-import updates' clauses without editing them

- **What could go wrong:** owner-constraints :370-371 and P14 :1866 say re-import updates; roadmap :4363 says it converges. A later builder could implement source-wins updates on a second snapshot, overwriting Asym edits after Asym became the system of record. Or Phase 14 builds its identity around update semantics that Phase 30 never calls.
- **Why it matters:** C3 requires the conflicting clause to be edited. 'Exact repeat import' is undefined at roadmap :4387, so refinement (1) is a new founder narrowing, not an inherited rule.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** repo-published: owner-constraints.md:370-371; roadmap.md:4363, :4387. repo-ratified: P14 :1864-1866 ('Re-import updates instead of duplicating'). Verifier notes on q01-r5 and q01-r6.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Record refinement (1) with an explicit scope. Same manifest: replay with no effect. Any later snapshot: a new plan, with create proposals and a three-way diff for review, never source-wins. List the clause edits needed at publication.
- **Spec language:** Exact repeat import (v1): replaying the same accepted Source Snapshot Manifest under the same mapping converges on the same target identities and produces no new create, update or outward effect. Any other snapshot of the same source after a committed run is a new plan. Unmatched source records are create proposals. Changed source values on bound records are shown as three-way differences (prior source, current source, current Asym) for reviewed correction through the owner command. Asym values are never overwritten by source precedence. At Phase 30 publication, owner-constraints.md:370-371 and P14 :1866 are edited to scope 're-import updates' to this rule.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Partly wrong evidence. owner-constraints.md:368-372 is about consistent dedupe rules, not 're-import updates'. roadmap.md:4363 ('Reimport converges on the same permitted target identity') and P14 :1864 ('Re-import updates instead of duplicating') are the right clauses. Refinement (1) does not contradict them. It sets the v1 meaning of 'exact repeat import' (roadmap.md:4387) and is consistent with that line. The risk is that someone reads it as dropping convergence for later snapshots. The three-way diff is over-engineered at this stage. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Add one sentence to refinement (1): replaying the same manifest has no effect; any later snapshot is a new plan that converges on the stored source binding, and a changed target is a conflict (roadmap.md:4349), never source-wins.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Source of truth / data integrity (high): the gifts-only catch-up drops adverse facts that SiteStacker or Mailchimp receive after the boundary, such as unsubscribes, do-not-contact flags, deceased marks, address changes and erasure requests. A gifts-only rule means Asym ignores them, or a person who opted out is treated as contactable. Basis: decision-log F12 (:118) and g4 (:140), which say Mailchimp drift needs a fresh export per import, and P13 :745-747 on erasure. Fix: a narrow reviewed catch-up for adverse consent and identity facts, applied only to restrict, never to widen.
- Source of truth (medium): year-end statements for the switch year. Gifts before the boundary (imported, already_receipted), gifts after it charged by SiteStacker, and gifts after it charged by Asym all fall in one tax year. No rule says which system issues the annual statement for each cohort, or how to avoid duplicate or partial statements (Phase 19; P13 :1030-1034 receipt boundary). v4-04 covers receipts per gift only.
- Data integrity (medium): the Switch Boundary has no defined form. It needs to be an instant with a time zone and a tie rule, matched by source ID across overlapping export windows. Otherwise gifts dated on boundary day are missed or doubled. P16 uses donor-anchored civil-date schedules (P13 :733), so a date-only boundary conflicts. Several reviewer fixes say 'measured from the Switch Boundary' without defining it.
- Hidden coupling (medium): no go/no-go or abort path after the ministry-declared freeze. If the final reconciliation fails, the plan must say whether the freeze is extended, SiteStacker resumes as system of record, or a partial commit is rolled back. Reversal must not delete Parties or rewind financial history (roadmap.md:4371). Option A as recorded only assumes success.
- Data integrity / technical debt (low-medium): practice runs that 'change nothing' still put full supporter PII and payment metadata into Asym staging. Rehearsal snapshot retention, disposal and tenant isolation belong under P29 custody rules (PR #2430, unmerged) and are not stated. Repeated practice runs multiply stored copies.
- Hidden coupling (medium): donor self-service for legacy-executor cohorts after go-live. If supporters move to Asym while SiteStacker still charges their recurring gifts, the Asym portal cannot cancel or update those gifts (P16 :1264, external/read-only), and the SiteStacker donor portal may be off. Each cohort needs a stated cancellation and update channel. v4-08 cites q01-r4 G3 but does not make this a requirement.

### 5.11 Hidden coupling

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v4). **Synthesis verdict:** The legacy money reference scope is shaped around Stripe, and P14 import identity is not scoped to a source system (owner requirement). Live checkout reuses any imported cus_ forever (safeguard). The 'off' attestation is coupled to duties the tail still needs (replace it with surface states). A single switch day depends on five unbuilt owners (eligibility gate). P20 and P21 boundaries stay independent.

**What was checked:** The reference keys imported money must satisfy. Live code that reads columns an import might fill. Which donor-facing duties refinement (3)'s 'off' actually controls. The release dependencies a single switch day creates. Sources: P13 :528-536, :723-725, :655; #711 acceptance; P14 :1864; saga.ts:200-210, :293-301; 20260223170000_atomic_rpc_and_donation_saga.sql:1253-1257; billing.ts:17, :39-40; 20261005050728_field_policy_foundation.sql:500; tenant-client.ts:64-66; event-store.ts:154; P16 :2692; owner-constraints :408-413; ADR-0106 :83-90; #834, #809, #512 (OPEN) and PR #2430 (unmerged), checked via gh on 2026-11-01. The Stripe PAN-copy doc was re-verified.

#### v4-06: The legacy money reference key is Stripe-shaped, and Phase 14 import identity is not scoped to a source system

- **What could go wrong:** P13 and #711 require references bound to tenant, connected account, livemode and Settlement Account Binding. Ambiguous ones are quarantined. Gifts from iATS, Braintree, PaymentSpring, Authorize.net or PayPal, or with only a SiteStacker transaction ID, cannot supply those. Most history is then quarantined, or importers fill placeholder account/mode values that corrupt the key later refunds and disputes match on. Separately, P14's partial UNIQUE(tenant_id, external_ref) plus 're-import updates' lets colliding IDs from different record families, installations or PayPal silently update an unrelated credit or tribute.
- **Why it matters:** Append-only money cannot be cleanly re-keyed later. Refinement (4)'s lineage promise is only as good as the owners' keys. Several processors per Site × currency are normal for SiteStacker ministries.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** repo-ratified: P13 :534-536 (story 169: 'exact tenant, connected account, mode, Party, and source object'), :528-531, :723-725. repo-published: #711 body (carry 'Settlement Account Binding, connected-account, livemode'; ambiguous provenance 'remain blocked/quarantined'). repo-ratified: P14 :1864-1866. Gateway per Site: q01-r2 (151000118930 verified-vendor-doc; other gateways from vendor-served code, inferred). Actual ID collisions: inferred (no real export exists).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Ask Phase 13 and Phase 14 for a source-qualified legacy reference: (Tenant, source system, source installation, record family, source object ID), unique on the full tuple. Processor provenance (gateway, merchant account, environment, processor object ID) is optional and is never required to resolve to an Asym Settlement Account Binding.
- **Spec language:** Every imported money, credit or tribute root carries a source-qualified external reference (Tenant, source system, source installation, record family, source object ID), and uniqueness is enforced on that full tuple. Processor provenance (gateway, merchant account reference, environment, processor object ID) is recorded when evidenced and is never required to resolve to an Asym Settlement Account Binding. Acceptance (real database): two rows with the same source object ID from different record families or installations import as two distinct facts; the same tuple replayed converges on one fact; a non-Stripe legacy gift with complete source provenance is not quarantined for lacking a connected account.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified (repo-ratified). P14 :1864 specifies a partial UNIQUE (tenant_id, external_ref), with external_source only 'where ratified'. So two source systems, or two record families, with the same ID would collide and 're-import updates' would overwrite silently. P13 :534-536 story 169 and :723-725 bind references to 'connected account, livemode'. That is Stripe-shaped and fits non-Stripe SiteStacker gateways badly. #711 :9 confirms the Settlement Account Binding carry. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Ask P13/P14 to include the source system and record family in import identity uniqueness. Add source installation only if the census shows more than one installation per tenant. Processor provenance is optional evidence and never has to resolve to an Asym Settlement Account Binding.

#### v4-07: Live checkout and portal code would turn any imported provider ID into live control

- **What could go wrong:** An import or staff edit writes a legacy cus_ into donors.stripe_customer_id. Checkout reuses it for every new gift, and the completion RPC never replaces it (COALESCE: first writer wins). On a new account, Asym checkout fails permanently until someone repairs the database. After a Stripe self-serve copy, which keeps customer IDs, SiteStacker-era saved cards become visible and chargeable through Asym with no P16 authorization, and the whole-account Billing Portal can change legacy objects. Separately, the platform-key fallback and metadata routing could put a migrated tenant's money on Asym's own account.
- **Why it matters:** Money and authorization. This extends q01-r4 H1-H3 with the checkout reuse, the first-writer-wins RPC and Mission Control editability, which make the damage persistent. It contradicts P13 :655, 'Asym is never in the flow of funds'.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** repo-integrated: saga.ts:207-210, :300; 20260223170000_atomic_rpc_and_donation_saga.sql:1255; billing.ts:39-40; 20261005050728_field_policy_foundation.sql:500 (visible and editable in mission_control); tenant-client.ts:64-66; event-store.ts:154. verified-vendor-doc: https://docs.stripe.com/get-started/data-migrations/pan-copy-self-serve?copy-method=full (accessed 2026-11-01): 'Copied customers have the same customer ID as in the original account'. Whether a UI exposes the field: unverified.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** An inert-evidence rule in the Phase 30 spec, a real-database test, and a go-live gate against the plaintext key and the fallback path. Phase 13 removes the fallback and the COALESCE rule when the binding lands.
- **Spec language:** Phase 30 and every owner import or catch-up command store legacy provider identifiers (customer, payment method, subscription, charge, mandate) only as inert source evidence. No import, catch-up or staff mapping writes donors.stripe_customer_id, donor_pledges.stripe_*, or any column read by a live checkout, portal or webhook handler. Acceptance (real database): a donor imported from a row carrying cus_… keeps a null live customer column, and that donor's next Asym checkout creates a new customer on the bound account. A Switch cannot be scheduled while the tenant resolves Stripe through tenants.stripe_secret_key or the platform-key fallback.
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified (repo-integrated). packages/api/src/donate/saga.ts:209-210 reuses any existing stripe_customer_id. 20260223170000...sql:1255 uses COALESCE to keep the first value. packages/api/src/donor-portal/billing.ts:39-40 opens a billing portal for donors.stripe_customer_id. 20261005050728...sql:500 makes it editable in mission_control. An imported or PAN-copied customer ID would therefore become live control. The proposed go-live gate and test duplicate each other. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Phase 30 spec rule: an import never writes donors.stripe_customer_id or any other live-control processor column. Processor IDs go only to source-qualified evidence. Add one real-database test of that rule. Removing the fallback stays with P13 when the binding lands.

#### v4-08: Refinement (3) ties one 'off' attestation to duties the tail still needs, and misses surfaces SiteStacker cannot control

- **What could go wrong:** Turning off Spark, forms and Mailchimp sync stops several duties while SiteStacker or PayPal still charges: the Mastercard pre-billing reminder (a Spark automation), decline handling, receipts if treated as automations, and the cancellation path. Lists already exported to missionaries' own Mailchimp accounts, PayPal subscriptions, gateway receipts and gateway-native schedules carry on regardless. Or receipts are left on and Asym also activates, so donors get duplicate sends.
- **Why it matters:** Every donor-facing duty needs exactly one owning system at all times. An attestation cannot cover systems it does not control. P16 REC-19 already forbids provider and Asym sends duplicating each other.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** verified-vendor-doc via q01-r2: 151000122015 (pre-billing reminder through Spark), 151000183830 (decline reruns). Via the r1 verifier: 151000122392 (missionary Mailchimp, a manual one-way export). q01-r4 G3 (cancellation method). PayPal srt cap: inferred from vendor-served code. repo-ratified: P16 :2692 (REC-19).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Replace refinement (3) with a Legacy Outward Surface Register. Each surface is recorded as off at boundary, kept on until tail closure (with owner and end condition), or not used, together with its evidence type. Each duty per cohort has one responsible system. Asym activation of a duty is blocked while the matching legacy surface is kept on.
- **Spec language:** Before go-live, the ministry completes a Legacy Outward Surface Register covering every SiteStacker, processor and missionary-held outward surface. Each surface is recorded as off at boundary, kept on until tail closure (with owner and end condition), or not used. Each donor-facing duty (receipt, pre-billing notice, decline notice, cancellation path, marketing) for each legacy cohort has exactly one responsible system. Asym cannot activate a duty for a cohort while a legacy surface for that duty and cohort is kept on. Surfaces outside SiteStacker's control are listed as such and never shown as covered by a SiteStacker setting.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Real conflict. P16 REC-19 (:2692) says mandatory Visa MIT notices override quiet periods and provider ownership must not duplicate Asym sends. If SiteStacker keeps charging recurring gifts until the P16 handover while Spark pre-billing reminders are turned off, required notices could stop. The vendor-doc citations come second-hand through q01-r2; I did not re-fetch them. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Replace the blanket 'off' attestation with a short per-surface disposition list (off at boundary / on until tail closure, with owner / not used). Block Asym activation of a duty while the matching legacy surface stays on. Evidence type per surface is enough; a separate named register is not needed in v1.

#### v4-09: One switch day ties go-live to five unbuilt owners and invites a false link with the P20/P21 cutovers

- **What could go wrong:** Option A needs every family target ready at once, but none of the owner commands exist: #834, #809 and #512 are open, P29 PR #2430 is unmerged, and the P7 import fact is undefined. Without the P16 classifier, legacy recurring gifts have no Asym representation, missionaries see support drop, and there is pressure to ship a shortcut writer. Builders may also line up Field Account activation and posting cutover with the Phase 30 boundary and read 'complete' as activation.
- **Why it matters:** Release sequencing and blast radius are concentrated on one date. C8 and owner-constraints :410-413 say the P20/P21 boundaries stay independently authoritative even when aligned.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** repo-integrated: decision-log §8 (nothing implemented). gh 2026-11-01: #834, #809, #512 OPEN; PR #2430 OPEN and unmerged. repo-published: owner-constraints.md:410-413. repo-ratified: ADR-0106 :83-90. q01-r4 G15; q01-r6 §7.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** A readiness gate: every family has a qualified owner command or an accepted visible exclusion, and every recurring cohort has at least a P16 external/read-only target. Plus a boundary-separation rule.
- **Spec language:** A Switch can be scheduled only when every source family in the census has either a qualified owner import command or an accepted visible exclusion, and every legacy recurring cohort has at least a Phase 16 external/read-only classification target. The Switch Boundary governs Phase 30 import scope only. It never activates, proves or substitutes for a Phase 20 Posting Ownership Cutover or a Phase 21 Field Account Operational Cutover, which keep their own boundaries even when set to the same instant. Acceptance: a Switch whose census lists a recurring cohort with no classification target cannot leave readiness.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Facts check out: #834, #809, #512 and PR #2430 are OPEN (gh, 2026-11-01). But roadmap.md:4367 already says 'Target readiness, not a generic upload success, determines what can be committed', and owner-constraints.md:410-413 already keeps P20/P21 boundaries independent. The concern mostly restates published rules for Q1. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: In the Q1 record, cite roadmap.md:4367 and owner-constraints.md:410-413. Before a ministry's switch, each family is committable through its owner or visibly excluded, and each recurring cohort has at least a P16 external/read-only disposition.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Source of truth / data integrity (high): the gifts-only catch-up drops adverse facts that SiteStacker or Mailchimp receive after the boundary, such as unsubscribes, do-not-contact flags, deceased marks, address changes and erasure requests. A gifts-only rule means Asym ignores them, or a person who opted out is treated as contactable. Basis: decision-log F12 (:118) and g4 (:140), which say Mailchimp drift needs a fresh export per import, and P13 :745-747 on erasure. Fix: a narrow reviewed catch-up for adverse consent and identity facts, applied only to restrict, never to widen.
- Source of truth (medium): year-end statements for the switch year. Gifts before the boundary (imported, already_receipted), gifts after it charged by SiteStacker, and gifts after it charged by Asym all fall in one tax year. No rule says which system issues the annual statement for each cohort, or how to avoid duplicate or partial statements (Phase 19; P13 :1030-1034 receipt boundary). v4-04 covers receipts per gift only.
- Data integrity (medium): the Switch Boundary has no defined form. It needs to be an instant with a time zone and a tie rule, matched by source ID across overlapping export windows. Otherwise gifts dated on boundary day are missed or doubled. P16 uses donor-anchored civil-date schedules (P13 :733), so a date-only boundary conflicts. Several reviewer fixes say 'measured from the Switch Boundary' without defining it.
- Hidden coupling (medium): no go/no-go or abort path after the ministry-declared freeze. If the final reconciliation fails, the plan must say whether the freeze is extended, SiteStacker resumes as system of record, or a partial commit is rolled back. Reversal must not delete Parties or rewind financial history (roadmap.md:4371). Option A as recorded only assumes success.
- Data integrity / technical debt (low-medium): practice runs that 'change nothing' still put full supporter PII and payment metadata into Asym staging. Rehearsal snapshot retention, disposal and tenant isolation belong under P29 custody rules (PR #2430, unmerged) and are not stated. Repeated practice runs multiply stored copies.
- Hidden coupling (medium): donor self-service for legacy-executor cohorts after go-live. If supporters move to Asym while SiteStacker still charges their recurring gifts, the Asym portal cannot cancel or update those gifts (P16 :1264, external/read-only), and the SiteStacker donor portal may be off. Each cohort needs a stated cancellation and update channel. v4-08 cites q01-r4 G3 but does not make this a requirement.

### 5.12 Failure modes

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v2). **Synthesis verdict:** There is no safe failure defined between boundary and go-live, or during the tail. Needed: a declared maximum pause with abort as the default (partial go-live would reopen B), a tenant-level invisible-until-live gate, stop proof never inferred from absence, sourcing per executor for PayPal and gateway money, and tail monitors.

**What was checked:** I checked the q01-r5 state machine, abort and rollback paths (X1-X4), snapshot rejection and the late tail, ADR-0106 :83-104, the Phase 16 :1286-1318 stop-proof and cutover text, issue #809, the missionary portal donor reader (donors.ts:503-506), the PayPal srt evidence from the verifier, and O11.

#### v2-F1: Freeze overrun with no pre-decided fallback; no system is authoritative between boundary and go-live

- **What could go wrong:** The final export set is rejected late, re-export depends on WMTEK, and owner acceptance is pending. The freeze stretches to days. Staff edit SiteStacker or Asym before go-live, and donor requests and mailed checks have no system of record.
- **Why it matters:** This is the most likely way a planned switch fails. Without a pre-agreed action the call gets made under pressure.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** q01-r5 S3 ('usually less than 48 hours', MS Learn via r5), S5, X1 and X2. q01-r6 abort edge case. v2-B1. C22. roadmap.md:4387 (Parties first slice).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Declare a maximum freeze length. Pre-select the deadline action: either abort before commit and unfreeze, or a history-incomplete go-live with Parties and contactability live and gift history following dark under incomplete coverage (proposed, needs founder acceptance). Keep a freeze-window intake log.
- **Spec language:** Each Switch declares a maximum freeze length before the boundary. If go-live criteria are not met by that deadline, the Switch takes its pre-selected action: abort before commit and unfreeze SiteStacker, or go live for the families that passed (Parties and contactability mandatory) with every other family recorded as history not yet imported and coverage declared incomplete. Requests received during the freeze are logged and entered in Asym after go-live as ordinary edits.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: A declared maximum freeze length and a pre-chosen deadline action are proportionate. Option (b) is the problem: a 'history-incomplete go-live' with gift families following later is effectively Option B (area by area), which the founder rejected for v1. It also contradicts 'Asym becomes the only place for supporters and their history'. It should not be proposed as a default. (corrected severity: medium-high) Corrected fix: Declare a maximum freeze length when scheduling. The default deadline action is to abort before commit and unfreeze SiteStacker. Keep a freeze-window intake log. Raise partial go-live only as an explicit founder question that would reopen B.

#### v2-F2: Committed is not live: committed rows leak into live surfaces before sign-off, and an abort after commit has no defined dark state

- **What could go wrong:** Parties committed at S6 become visible to missionaries, donor claim matching and reports before reconciliation, with false Lapsed or joined-today signals. If the switch then aborts, q01-r5 X2's 'quarantine cohort' depends on a state no owner defines, and deletion is forbidden, so two systems show the same people.
- **Why it matters:** ADR-0106 ratifies discardable staging before an atomic authority transition. The Q1 answer has no such fence for CRM.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** repo-integrated: packages/api/src/missionary-portal/donors.ts:503-506 (tenant+missionary filter only, legacy schema). ADR-0106 :86-91 and :96-97. roadmap.md:4371. Decision-log §8 (#570, no Party tables). q01-r5 X2. q01-r6 §10.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** For new tenants without live users, gate the whole tenant: missionary and donor surfaces, checkout and claim matching, automations and coverage stay off until one atomic go-live. A per-cohort not-yet-live owner state is needed only where the census finds live users. An abort after commit keeps the data dark for a superseding Switch.
- **Spec language:** Data committed under a Switch is not visible to missionary or donor surfaces, checkout or claim matching, reports, automations or coverage until one atomic, audited go-live transition. For a tenant without live users this is a tenant-level gate; for a tenant with live users the target owners provide a cohort-level not-yet-live state before any switch is scheduled. An abort after commit leaves the data not live and available to a superseding Switch and never deletes a Party.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The cited reader (missionary-portal/donors.ts:503-506, checked) is the legacy donors table, and the target Party readers are unbuilt (#570), so the 'leak' is hypothetical rather than current. For the expected case (a new tenant with no live users), a tenant-level go-live gate is the simple, sufficient answer, as the review itself proposes. Likelihood 'high' is overstated. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: For a new tenant, keep missionary and donor surfaces, checkout and claim matching, automations and coverage off until one atomic go-live. If the census finds live users, require an owner-defined not-yet-live state before scheduling. An abort after commit leaves the data dark for a superseding Switch.

#### v2-F3: Stop proof inferred from the absence of charges in a Phase 30 snapshot

- **What could go wrong:** Catch-up evidence saying 'no legacy charges since X' is treated as old-executor stop proof. A charge after the snapshot's as-of time, or a PayPal, gateway-native or rerun charge invisible to SiteStacker, then produces active-active double charging.
- **Why it matters:** Double-charging donors is the most damaging failure in the handover.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** Phase 16 :1314. Issue #809 body (local suppression is not stop proof; Stripe-focused). q01-r4 G20. Payment.js :1388-1422 srt (inferred from vendor code, verifier-confirmed). q01-r2 gateway-native schedules and cron-restart edge case.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Phase 30 evidence states its as-of time and which executors it can observe. Phase 16 stop proof requires cancellation evidence for each executor class: the SiteStacker schedule cancelled and its rerun path neutralised, the PayPal profile cancelled, and gateway-native schedules cancelled.
- **Spec language:** Phase 30 catch-up evidence states its as-of time and the executors it can observe. Absence of legacy charges in Phase 30 evidence is never sufficient old-executor stop proof; Phase 16 requires executor-side cancellation evidence for each executor class.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Most of this is already ratified. Phase 16 :1314 requires 'proof the old executor stopped', and issue #809 (open, checked) says never to treat Asym's local suppression as provider-stop proof. The genuine residual is that #809 has only a Stripe adapter (providers/stripe.ts), so stop-evidence classes for the SiteStacker cron, PayPal and gateway-native schedules are undefined. This overlaps v2-E2. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: One owner requirement on Phase 16, merged with E2: define stop-evidence classes for non-Stripe executors (SiteStacker schedule plus rerun path, PayPal profile, gateway-native schedule). Phase 30 catch-up evidence states its as-of time and which executors it can observe.

#### v2-F4: Refinement 2 assumes SiteStacker records every post-switch money fact

- **What could go wrong:** Several kinds of money activity never reach SiteStacker. PayPal Standard subscriptions keep charging after SiteStacker is off, up to 52 cycles. Gateway-native schedules, gateway-dashboard refunds, chargebacks and ACH returns, and transactions deleted after a refund are also missing. That money appears in neither system, and a SiteStacker-only reconciliation shows no variance.
- **Why it matters:** Financial truth is lost silently.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** Verifier r1-missed item 2 (Payment.js srt clamp 2-52). q01-r2 summary (gateway-native schedules; chargebacks undocumented in SiteStacker). K8. q01-r3 PayPal catch-up profile.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Source the catch-up per executor: SiteStacker, the ministry's PayPal reports, and each processor's reports. Run level-2 reconciliation per merchant account × currency at every catch-up.
- **Spec language:** A reviewed catch-up of money facts collected or reversed by any legacy executor after the boundary, sourced per executor (SiteStacker, the ministry's PayPal account and each processor's reports), reconciled per merchant account × currency, never writing supporter details and never winning over Asym edits.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Refinement 2 sources the catch-up only from SiteStacker. Money that never reaches SiteStacker includes PayPal Standard subscriptions (Payment.js srt, inferred from vendor code, verifier-confirmed), gateway-native schedules, and dashboard refunds or returns. That money would be silently lost. The level-2 reconciliation part duplicates v2-B2. One dependency is missing: per q01-r4:113 M1, Asym cannot read the old processor account without a migration-only read binding (an owner amendment to Phase 13 D1), so processor sourcing depends on ministry-supplied reports or that amendment. (corrected severity: high (likelihood medium)) Corrected fix: Source the catch-up per executor: SiteStacker, the ministry's PayPal reports, and each processor's reports. State whether processor data comes from ministry-supplied reports or a Phase 13 read-only observation binding. Reconciliation follows B2.

#### v2-F5: Tail evidence decays and attestations drift unnoticed

- **What could go wrong:** SiteStacker licensing lapses or the processor account closes before dispute windows end. A ministry re-enables a form for a campaign, or catch-ups silently stop. None of this is visible today (O11).
- **Why it matters:** The tail is where the 'only place' promise is weakest.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** O11 (decision-log §4). q01-r5 S9-S10 and E-52. q01-r4 contract-end edge case. q01-r6 'form attested off' edge case.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Switch-level tail monitors routed to a named owner.
- **Spec language:** Each open Switch tail is monitored for catch-up recency, attestation breaches, level-2 variance and source-shutdown risk, with alerts to the named Switch owner.
- **Bucket:** monitor (signal: (1) Days since the last successful catch-up per executor. (2) Any post-boundary one-time gift or new SiteStacker person in a catch-up. (3) Unexplained level-2 variance per merchant account × currency. (4) SiteStacker contract end or processor account closure dated before the planned tail closure.; threshold: (1) More than the cadence plus 7 days. (2) One or more rows. (3) Not zero. (4) Any.; owner: The named Switch owner (Phase 30 operations or customer success) together with the ministry finance contact.; response: (1) Run the catch-up or record why not. (2) Re-attest, list affected donors for the receipt owner, and raise an urgent exception. (3) Block tail closure and investigate. (4) Take final exports and processor reports before shutdown, or extend the tail.)
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: A reasonable monitor-bucket item; O11 confirms post-handoff failures are invisible. The thresholds are illustrative and fine at discovery level. (corrected severity: medium-low)

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Brittleness: Legal Entity assignment presupposed. B2's per-Legal-Entity reconciliation assumes every SiteStacker row has a reviewed Legal Entity, but owner-constraints.md:375-377 forbids inferring Legal Entity from Site, provider or a default entity. The switch needs an explicit, reviewed Legal Entity assignment rule per merchant account and Site (see r10-gap-g8) before reconciliation means anything. inferred from repo-published.
- Edge cases: same Stripe account, SiteStacker still holds its keys. The founder asked about ministries already on Stripe, and the review never handles this case. SiteStacker charges saved payment methods off-session with the ministry's keys or Connect access (q01-r4:113). Revoking that access at freeze kills the recurring tail that refinements 2 and 4 rely on; keeping it leaves a live executor on the very account Asym may later bind. Phase 16 :1314 treats a different application as formal cutover even on the same account. Credential revocation therefore has to be sequenced per executor cohort, not at freeze. inferred from repo-ratified + q01-r4.
- Failure modes / edge cases: switch year split across two systems. A switch inside a tax year splits each donor's year between SiteStacker (pre-boundary gifts already receipted, plus SiteStacker self-service year-end statements) and Asym (tail and new gifts). The review does not say which system issues that year's year-end statement, or how duplicate or partial statements are prevented. Phase 19's Late Fact Obligation (phase-19 :976-990, via q01-r5 A28) and Phase 7 already-receipted evidence (phase-13 :1029-1036) govern this, but the switch plan needs an explicit rule. inferred.
- Lifecycle/idempotency: a row that disappears from a later catch-up. A gift present in the final export may vanish from later catch-ups, for example a transaction deleted after a refund (K8). q01-r5:119 handles absence only for rehearsal drift. Catch-ups need the same rule: absence is evidence for finance review, never an automatic reversal and never silently ignored. inferred.
- Lifecycle/concurrency: the same source transaction changes status between catch-ups. ACH can move pending → settled → returned (Phase 16 :1295; Stripe ACH window via q01-r5:199). Without status transitions keyed to the scoped source identity (L2), each catch-up could create a new money fact instead of advancing or reversing the existing one. L5 covers only reversals that staff recorded. inferred.
- Failure modes: processor-level checks depend on unauthorized access. F4 and B2 rely on processor-side data, but q01-r4:113 says the current Phase 13 M1 topology gives Asym no read access to the old account. Without either ministry-supplied reports or a Phase 13 D1 owner amendment (a read-only observation binding), level-2 reconciliation and per-executor catch-up cannot run. This dependency must be named before recording.

### 5.13 Lifecycle, temporal correctness, concurrency, and idempotency

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v2). **Synthesis verdict:** Refinement 1 is safe only if identity always rests on source bindings, never on the snapshot. Cross-source duplicates need one authoritative source per executor × period and a source-qualified reference scope. Backfills need three time axes. The Switch Boundary must not set P16, P20 or P21 boundaries. Reversal idempotency is a Phase 13 rule. Carrying match decisions and post-go-live corrective re-plan are frontier items.

**What was checked:** I checked refinements 1, 2 and 4 against roadmap.md:4347-4401, owner-constraints.md:373-385 and :411-413, REQ28-R20/R21 (shared-engine idempotency), Phase 29 founder-brief.txt:151 (PR #2430), Phase 13 :722-724, :1031-1035 and :1936-1937, Phase 16 :1295 and :1314, ADR-0058, ADR-0106 :99-104, the CONTEXT.md cutover glossary, and r10-gap-g4 §4.3.

#### v2-L1: Refinement 1 must not make the snapshot the idempotency key

- **What could go wrong:** Keying on snapshot identity duplicates records on several paths: a forgotten-file extension, a superseding Switch after an abort-after-commit, catch-ups that overlap the final window, and byte-identical re-uploads. Parties that were merged or erased are recreated on replay.
- **Why it matters:** The shared engine already has ratified binding-based recognition. A digest is not business identity. Owner-constraints 're-import updates' would be narrowed by shorthand (C3).
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** relationships.md:396-403 (REQ28-R21: 'row number/file hash/tool name alone is not identity authority'; bindings qualify Tenant, workspace, source instance, entity kind; no reviving erased content). Phase 29 founder-brief.txt:151 on PR #2430 ('A matching digest proves byte equality, not identical provenance … or business significance'; unmerged). roadmap.md:4363 and :4387. owner-constraints.md:373-374. r10-gap-g4.md:254. O6.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Record refinement 1 as a narrowing of update behaviour only. Key Parties on source-record bindings and money on scoped external references. Replay of the same manifest is a no-op. Bindings follow Phase 4 survivors and keep erasure tombstones. Allow one non-terminal Switch per tenant and source instance, and one committing run per family. Edit the owner-constraints clause.
- **Spec language:** Exact repeat import in v1 means re-running the same sealed source manifest: it recognises all prior accepted work and proposes no new effects. Independently of the manifest, every import and catch-up run converges on the source-record binding (Tenant, source system, source instance, entity kind, source ID); file digest, row number or manifest identity alone is never identity. A binding whose target was merged follows the Phase 4 survivor; a binding whose target was erased blocks re-creation without retaining identifying data. A tenant and source instance have at most one non-terminal Switch, and each Switch has at most one committing run per target family at a time. In v1, owner-constraints 're-import updates' is narrowed for Party fields to corrective replay, and the clause is edited to say so.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The refinement never says the snapshot is the idempotency key, so this is a pre-emptive clarification, not a defect. Binding-based recognition is already ratified for the shared engine pattern (REQ28-R21, relationships.md:396-403, checked), and roadmap.md:4363 requires convergence. Telling the session to 'edit the owner-constraints clause' is out of scope for discovery (no spec). C3 makes that a later spec-stage edit, to be logged now as a discrepancy. The concurrency rule (one non-terminal Switch per tenant and source instance) is proportionate. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: When recording refinement 1, add one clause: 'replaying the same sealed manifest is a no-op; identity always converges on source-record bindings, never on file or manifest identity, and follows Phase 4 survivors and erasure'. Log the owner-constraints.md:373-374 narrowing as a D-item for the spec stage instead of editing it now.

#### v2-L2: The same gift arrives from several sources, and the legacy external-reference scope is Stripe-shaped

- **What could go wrong:** One gift can appear as a SiteStacker transaction, a processor settlement row and a Historic Giving row, producing up to three money facts. The Phase 13 reference scope (connected account, livemode) has no slot for SiteStacker, PayPal or other merchant accounts, and imported rows have no Stripe event IDs for idempotency.
- **Why it matters:** Widening the catch-up to processor and PayPal reports makes cross-source duplication likely. Story 171 forbids fuzzy linking, so only exact keys plus a declared basis can prevent it.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** phase-13 :722-724 ('scoped to the exact tenant, connected account, livemode, source object'). :1937 (idempotency on Stripe ingest event_id and saga keys). :539-542 (story 171). q01-r1 K19. Verifier: Stripe copy keeps cus_ IDs.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Declare one authoritative source basis per gift population × period. Other bases are reconciliation evidence linked by exact cross-reference IDs. Processor-only rows go to finance review. Owner requirement on Phase 13: define the non-Stripe reference scope. Add test fixtures with identical IDs across merchant accounts and sources.
- **Spec language:** For each gift population and period the Switch records exactly one authoritative source basis. Rows from any other basis are reconciliation evidence and link to the authoritative row only by an exact exported cross-reference ID; they never create a second money fact. Legacy external references are scoped to Tenant, source system and instance, executor or gateway, merchant account, environment, object kind and ID.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Checked: Phase 13 :722-724 scopes legacy references to 'tenant, connected account, livemode, source object', which has no slot for a non-Stripe gateway or merchant account. Invariant 7 (:1937) is keyed on Stripe ingest events. Story 171 (:539-542) forbids fuzzy linking. Once F4 widens sources, cross-source duplicates are likely. The fix is proportionate, and the reference-scope change is correctly framed as an owner requirement. (corrected severity: high (likelihood medium-high))

#### v2-L3: The catch-up conflates late-arriving pre-boundary facts with post-boundary legacy-executed facts

- **What could go wrong:** Some catch-up rows are really pre-boundary facts: a pre-boundary ACH that settles late, a check dated before the boundary but keyed after it, or a rerun of a pre-boundary due date. Treating them all as tail facts gets the receipt treatment (pre-Asym already_receipted versus the open Phase 7 fact) and the coverage treatment wrong. Coverage already declared complete changes after the fact, and first-gift conclusions become wrong.
- **Why it matters:** The roadmap requires occurrence and recorded times and forbids turning historical events prospective. ADR-0106 already uses three time axes for late facts.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** q01-r5 S9 ('classified as late-tail facts against the boundary'), E-02 and E-20. Phase 16 :1295. ADR-0106 :99-104 ('source-effective/pre-cutover, discovery, and current record times'). roadmap.md:4363 and :4395. phase-13 :1031-1035. Phase 19 Late Fact Obligation (q01-r5 A28). C22.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Give each row source-effective, source-recorded and discovery times. Classify by source-effective time into three lanes: late pre-boundary history, post-boundary legacy money, or post-boundary reversal of a pre-boundary gift. Version coverage as 'complete through boundary as of T'.
- **Spec language:** Every catch-up row records its source-effective, source-recorded (where available) and discovery times and is classified by source-effective time into late pre-boundary history, post-boundary legacy-executed money, or post-boundary reversal of a pre-boundary gift; each class has its own coverage, receipt and late-fact treatment. History coverage is declared 'complete through the boundary as of {time}' and is revised when late pre-boundary history arrives.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: ADR-0106 :99-104 (checked) ratifies source-effective, discovery and record times for late pre-cutover facts. Phase 16 :1295 confirms pre-boundary payments may resolve after the boundary. Phase 13 :1029-1036 separates pre-Asym receipt evidence. Three lanes keyed on source-effective time is proportionate. (corrected severity: medium)

#### v2-L4: 'One boundary for every family' collides with ratified per-family and per-executor boundaries

- **What could go wrong:** Reusing the switch instant as the Phase 20 posting-ownership boundary splits old-processor payouts. Reusing it as the Phase 16 protected boundary turns every planned tail charge into an urgent reconciliation or refund exception, which means an incident storm or wrongful refunds.
- **Why it matters:** Refinement 4 works only if each boundary stays owned by its owner. q01-r5 K6 says 'same instant for all families'.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** ADR-0058 :7-12 ('cannot split a payout, Deposit Group … merely because dates cross'). CONTEXT.md Posting Ownership Cutover Avoid list ('calendar-date-only cutoff', ~:6952-6957). owner-constraints.md:411-413. Phase 16 :1295 and :1314. ADR-0106 :86-88. q01-r5 K6.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** The Switch records only the CRM/gift-history boundary. It references, and never sets, the Phase 20 and 21 boundaries. Each legacy executor cohort gets its own Phase 16 boundary at formal cutover. Keep the terms distinct in the glossary.
- **Spec language:** The Switch Boundary governs only which source facts are pre-switch history for CRM and gift-history import. It never sets or implies a Phase 20 Posting Ownership Cutover, a Phase 21 Field Account Operational Cutover or a Phase 16 executor boundary; the Switch references those owner-set boundaries where they exist. A legacy-executed charge after the Switch Boundary but before its cohort's Phase 16 cutover is a planned tail fact, not a control-loss exception.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The target is research claim q01-r5 K6, not the founder's answer or refinement 4. The founder never said one boundary governs every family. The correction is right: ADR-0058 :7-12 forbids splitting a payout, CONTEXT.md:6955 lists 'calendar-date-only cutoff' under Avoid, and owner-constraints.md:411-413 makes Phase 20 and 21 boundaries independently authoritative. It belongs in the research-correction table (RC1), not as a concern against the decision. (corrected severity: low-medium) Corrected fix: Keep as RC1. When recording, note that the Switch Boundary covers only CRM and gift-history classification, and that a legacy charge before its cohort's Phase 16 cutover is a planned tail fact, not a control-loss exception.

#### v2-L5: A catch-up reversal can duplicate an Asym-recorded correction

- **What could go wrong:** Staff refund in the old processor and record the reversal in Asym. The next catch-up imports the same refund again. The Phase 13 clamp rejects only total over-reversal, so a duplicated partial refund passes.
- **Why it matters:** Two valid actions together break money conservation. This is the concurrent-edit or duplicate-delivery race the roadmap asks to be tested.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** phase-13 :1936 (invariant 6, refund clamp against effective total). :1937 (invariant 7, Stripe-event idempotency only). q01-r4 refund-after-switch edge case. q01-r5 E-05. roadmap.md:4401.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Make legacy reversals idempotent on their source reversal identity. Staff must enter that identity when recording old-processor actions. A catch-up reversal with no matching identity, on a gift that already has an Asym correction, is held for finance review.
- **Spec language:** A reversal of an imported legacy gift is idempotent on its source reversal identity. Staff recording an old-processor refund in Asym enter that identity. A catch-up reversal that matches no recorded identity but targets a gift that already has an Asym-recorded correction is held for finance review.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The risk is real: invariant 6 (:1936) clamps only total over-reversal, and invariant 7 is Stripe-keyed. But reversal idempotency is a money rule owned by Phase 13 (C1; roadmap.md:4377), so Phase 30 cannot define it. Requiring staff to type a gateway refund ID on every legacy refund is brittle. (corrected severity: high (likelihood medium-low)) Corrected fix: Owner requirement on Phase 13: legacy reversals are idempotent on a scoped source-reversal identity, recorded wherever it is available. Phase 30 rule: a catch-up reversal against a gift that already has any post-import Asym correction is held for finance review, never auto-posted or auto-skipped.

#### v2-L6: Practice runs that persist nothing push every review decision into the freeze window

- **What could go wrong:** Only mappings are remembered. Match choices, value maps and exclusions made in rehearsal must be redone on the final plan under freeze pressure, which leads to rubber-stamping or an overrun.
- **Why it matters:** It feeds v2-F1. Rehearsal value is lost.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** roadmap.md:4347 (remembered tenant mappings only). :4349. q01-r5 S1 ('never authorize the final commit'). REQ28-R20-02 (relationships.md:388).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Save reviewer decisions without writing targets, keyed by binding plus a per-record content digest. Propose them again on the final plan where source and target are unchanged. The final plan still needs its own commit authority.
- **Spec language:** Reviewer decisions taken during practice runs (mappings, value maps, match choices, exclusions) are saved without writing targets and are proposed again on the final plan for every record whose source content and target revision are unchanged; changed records return to review. Carried decisions are labelled as carried and still need the final plan's commit authority.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Valid friction concern; roadmap.md:4347 already remembers tenant mappings. Keying every reviewer decision on a per-record content digest is heavier than v1 needs, and 'medium-high/high' overstates it: mappings and value maps (the bulk) are already carried. Match-review choices are the real residual, and they belong to Phase 4. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Carry mappings, value maps and exclusion rules from practice runs (already remembered). Re-propose rehearsal match decisions only as labelled suggestions on unchanged records, subject to Phase 4 review. The final plan keeps its own commit authority.

#### v2-L7: There is no corrective path for a mapping error found after go-live

- **What could go wrong:** Exact replay is the only v1 import path and refresh imports were rejected. A mapping error found after go-live therefore means hand-correcting thousands of records or a reversal that may be blocked.
- **Why it matters:** Mapping errors are common, the roadmap requires 'changed mapping' proof, and the per-field base is already identified as missing.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** roadmap.md:4401. r10-gap-g4.md:118-130 (per-field base). owner-constraints.md:378-385. roadmap.md:4349.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Corrective re-plan of the same sealed manifest with the fixed mapping. A field is updated only if its current value equals the last-imported value. Asym edits are conflicts. Money changes only through owner corrections. Store the last-imported value per bound field.
- **Spec language:** Re-running the same sealed manifest with a corrected mapping produces a reviewed update plan: a field is proposed for update only if its current value equals the value last imported for that binding; fields edited in Asym since import are conflicts; money facts change only through their owner's correction path.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: This is a fair gap, but it pre-decides frontier F1's 'three-way field memory' question (decision-log:107), which is a founder question, not a spec requirement to impose. roadmap.md:4401 'changed mapping' acceptance covers the need in principle. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Record a follow-up founder question: after go-live, can a corrected mapping re-plan the same sealed manifest, and does that require storing last-imported values per field (three-way memory)? Money stays with owner corrections either way.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Brittleness: Legal Entity assignment presupposed. B2's per-Legal-Entity reconciliation assumes every SiteStacker row has a reviewed Legal Entity, but owner-constraints.md:375-377 forbids inferring Legal Entity from Site, provider or a default entity. The switch needs an explicit, reviewed Legal Entity assignment rule per merchant account and Site (see r10-gap-g8) before reconciliation means anything. inferred from repo-published.
