- Edge cases: same Stripe account, SiteStacker still holds its keys. The founder asked about ministries already on Stripe, and the review never handles this case. SiteStacker charges saved payment methods off-session with the ministry's keys or Connect access (q01-r4:113). Revoking that access at freeze kills the recurring tail that refinements 2 and 4 rely on; keeping it leaves a live executor on the very account Asym may later bind. Phase 16 :1314 treats a different application as formal cutover even on the same account. Credential revocation therefore has to be sequenced per executor cohort, not at freeze. inferred from repo-ratified + q01-r4.
- Failure modes / edge cases: switch year split across two systems. A switch inside a tax year splits each donor's year between SiteStacker (pre-boundary gifts already receipted, plus SiteStacker self-service year-end statements) and Asym (tail and new gifts). The review does not say which system issues that year's year-end statement, or how duplicate or partial statements are prevented. Phase 19's Late Fact Obligation (phase-19 :976-990, via q01-r5 A28) and Phase 7 already-receipted evidence (phase-13 :1029-1036) govern this, but the switch plan needs an explicit rule. inferred.
- Lifecycle/idempotency: a row that disappears from a later catch-up. A gift present in the final export may vanish from later catch-ups, for example a transaction deleted after a refund (K8). q01-r5:119 handles absence only for rehearsal drift. Catch-ups need the same rule: absence is evidence for finance review, never an automatic reversal and never silently ignored. inferred.
- Lifecycle/concurrency: the same source transaction changes status between catch-ups. ACH can move pending → settled → returned (Phase 16 :1295; Stripe ACH window via q01-r5:199). Without status transitions keyed to the scoped source identity (L2), each catch-up could create a new money fact instead of advancing or reversing the existing one. L5 covers only reversals that staff recorded. inferred.
- Failure modes: processor-level checks depend on unauthorized access. F4 and B2 rely on processor-side data, but q01-r4:113 says the current Phase 13 M1 topology gives Asym no read access to the old account. Without either ministry-supplied reports or a Phase 13 D1 owner amendment (a read-only observation binding), level-2 reconciliation and per-executor catch-up cannot run. This dependency must be named before recording.

### 5.14 Data integrity risks

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v4). **Synthesis verdict:** Reconciliation as worded checks the import against itself and drops the Legal Entity and source-basis partitions. The snapshot needs a manifest definition. Backfill facts can attach to merged or erased people unless they resolve through binding plus survivor. Schema defaults invent dates, statuses and currency. Residual writes need detection, not attestation alone.

**What was checked:** Whether the stated reconciliation catches known SiteStacker export failures: the Historic Giving double count (K19), ledger lines (K20), permission-filtered or truncated exports, pending ACH, post-boundary rows, and gateway-side refunds. What 'same snapshot' means for idempotency. How catch-up facts resolve people after merges and erasures. Whether schema defaults invent facts. Sources: roadmap :4349, :4363, :4369; owner-constraints :431-436; C11 (PR #2430); P13 :539-542, :745-747; decision-log D10, D16; init_schema.sql:76, :82; foundation_1_schema.sql:334-338, :382; donors.ts:174-192; P16 :1068.

#### v4-10: 'Counts and per-currency totals' is under-partitioned and checks the import against itself

- **What could go wrong:** Reconciling against the files that were imported passes even with a double count (Historic Giving plus Contributions), ledger lines, an export filtered by the exporter's permissions or truncated, pending ACH imported as succeeded, a fee-covered gross that differs from the gift, or refunds and disputes made in the gateway dashboard that SiteStacker never recorded. It also drops the Legal Entity and source-basis partitions the roadmap requires, and source basis is undefined.
- **Why it matters:** Finance sign-off rests on a tautology. Receipts, statements, Field Account openings and first-gift facts (C22) all inherit the error.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** repo-published: roadmap.md:4369 ('by Legal Entity, original currency and source basis'); owner-constraints.md:431-436; decision-log D16. verified-vendor-doc via r1: 151000122348 (K19 overlap; ledger rows carry record_type=ledger). q01-r5 E-02, E-25, E-26, K5; q01-r2 (gateway-dashboard refunds not documented); q01-r6 point 3.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** A conservation proof against independent controls (a source report or screen count, or a processor statement, not used as import input), per Legal Entity × currency × source basis × legacy merchant account. Phase 13 defines source basis and the rule of one basis per population and period.
- **Spec language:** For each Legal Entity × original currency × source basis × legacy merchant account cohort, the reconciliation report shows an independent control total, taken from a source report, source screen count or processor statement not used as import input, and proves it equals imported + excluded-with-reason + quarantined + assigned-to-tail, for count and minor-unit amount, with every variance itemised. Each gift population × period has exactly one source basis (defined by Phase 13). An overlap proof is mandatory where Contributions and Historic Giving are both supplied. Historic Giving rows with record_type=ledger are never gifts. Any unexplained variance blocks go-live. Acceptance: a fixture with the same gifts in both datasets yields a blocking overlap variance, not a doubled total.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Valid that 'per-currency totals' is narrower than the published partition 'by Legal Entity, original currency and source basis' (roadmap.md:4369; owner-constraints.md:431-436). The point about checking the import against itself is also valid. But that partition is already a requirement, so Q1 only has to adopt it. Partitioning by legacy merchant account is an extra not grounded in owner text. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Q1 reconciliation uses the published partition (Legal Entity × original currency × source basis) and at least one independent control per partition (a source report or screen count, or a processor statement, not used as import input). Add merchant-account partitioning only where processor statements are the control.

#### v4-11: 'Same snapshot' is undefined, so replay idempotency and decisions carried from practice runs can corrupt data

- **What could go wrong:** If 'same' means identical bytes, re-downloading unchanged data duplicates on replay. If it means business keys only, a changed export is silently applied. Match decisions from a practice run get reused after the source changed (Move Payment Method, re-designation, merge, deletion), so a gift attaches to the wrong person or a missing row is treated as deleted.
- **Why it matters:** Duplicate or misattached money and identity in the first committed import is exactly what practice runs are meant to prevent. C11 forbids bytes-only business dedupe, while roadmap :4349 binds plans to bytes; refinement (1) does not say which governs replay.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** repo-ratified/published (unmerged): C11, PR #2430. repo-published: roadmap.md:4349, :4363. q01-r1 X2/X3; q01-r5 K5, E-44; r10-gap-g4 (no change feed; absence does not prove deletion).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Keep four things separate. The manifest (P29 byte identities, filters, exporter role, version, time, screen counts) identifies what was received. The commit idempotency key is the source-qualified record identity plus the owner command identity. Carried decisions are reused only when the content fingerprint is unchanged. Absence is reported, never acted on.
- **Spec language:** A Source Snapshot Manifest records the Phase 29 byte identities, export filters, exporter role, source version, export time and source screen counts for one Switch. Commit idempotency is keyed on the source-qualified record identity and the target owner's command identity, never on bytes alone. A reviewer decision from a practice plan is reused only if the source record's content fingerprint is unchanged; otherwise it is shown for review again. A record absent from a later snapshot is reported as 'absent from snapshot' and never deleted or reversed automatically. Acceptance: committing byte-different but content-identical exports twice creates no new facts; a practice-run match on a record whose payment method moved to another person is shown again.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Valid that 'same snapshot' needs a definition. The P29 byte identity on unmerged PR #2430 and roadmap.md:4349 (plan bound to exact source bytes) give the basis. The commit-key part repeats v4-06. 'Absence never proves deletion' is already in decision-log g4 (:140). (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Define a snapshot as the manifest bound to P29 byte identities plus export filters and time. Reuse a decision carried over from a practice run only when the record's content fingerprint is unchanged. Record identity follows v4-06.

#### v4-12: Catch-up and late facts can attach to merged, erased or non-existent people

- **What could go wrong:** A catch-up gift is bound to Party A, which Asym staff merged into B after go-live; the lookup writes to the tombstone or fails, and merge-redirect storage is still unsettled. An erased donor is re-identified from processor reports. A post-boundary new donor makes the catch-up create a Party through the import path, which is a supporter write.
- **Why it matters:** Misattached money, a re-identified erased person, and contradiction of P13 story 171 (quarantine, never link by email, name or fingerprint) and P13 :745-747 (no re-identification).
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** repo-ratified: P13 :539-542, :745-747. decision-log D10 (merged_into_donor_id vs donor_merge_redirects vs parties.merged_into_party_id); O6; #512 OPEN (gh 2026-11-01). q01-r5 K3, E-22, E-50.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Resolve Parties only through the stored source binding followed by the Phase 4 survivor-resolution read. A missing or ambiguous binding, or an erased subject, goes to finance exception review. Refinement (2) should say 'never creates or updates' supporters, not only 'never overwrites'.
- **Spec language:** Catch-up and late-fact runs resolve the Party only through the stored source binding followed by the Phase 4 survivor-resolution read. A fact with no binding, an ambiguous binding or an erased subject is quarantined for finance exception review (P13 story 171) and never creates, updates or re-identifies a Party automatically. Acceptance: a catch-up gift for a source person whose Asym Party was merged after go-live lands on the survivor; a catch-up gift for an erased person yields an exception carrying no personal data.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified. P13 :745-747 says erased donors stay pseudonymized and must not be re-identified from provider metadata. Decision-log D10 notes there are three merge-redirect mechanisms, and #512 is OPEN. A gifts-only catch-up that resolves people by matching could attach money to merged or erased subjects or create new ones. Overlaps v4-04 on the wording. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: As proposed: resolve Parties only through the stored source binding, then the Phase 4 survivor read. Missing, ambiguous or erased goes to finance exceptions. Refinement (2) says 'never creates or updates supporters'.

#### v4-13: Schema defaults and status mapping invent facts for imported rows

- **What could go wrong:** Blank import columns become gift_date and joined_date of today, currency usd, source 'direct', status 'active' and preferred_language 'en'. normalizeDonorStatus maps unknown values to Active and drives the Lapsed/At Risk labels, so imported partners look new, healthy and English-speaking.
- **Why it matters:** These wrong derived facts look plausible and go unnoticed. They contradict C22, P16 :1068 ('Unknown never defaults to healthy, lapsed, zero, or received') and Phase 2's legacy attribution (C21, D7).
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** repo-integrated: 20250101000000_init_schema.sql:76, :82; 20260214090000_foundation_1_schema.sql:334-338, :382; packages/api/src/missionary-portal/donors.ts:174-192. repo-ratified: P16 :1068. Consolidates q01-r4 §2.7 and q01-r6 (with its verifier's corrected citations).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Write only through owner commands that take explicit values or an explicit unknown. Add a real-database test that no default fired. Projections show 'history incomplete' instead of health labels.
- **Spec language:** No import or catch-up row relies on a column default for any business fact (date, currency, status, language, source or attribution); unknown is stored as unknown through the owner command. Acceptance (real database): for every import-origin row, default-bearing columns equal source values or an explicit unknown marker, and missionary and portal projections render imported Parties without person-level lapse or health labels until coverage is declared.
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified (repo-integrated). 20250101000000_init_schema.sql:76 sets status DEFAULT 'active' and :82 sets joined_date DEFAULT CURRENT_DATE. 20260214090000_foundation_1_schema.sql:334-338 sets gift_date DEFAULT CURRENT_DATE, currency 'usd' and source 'direct'. missionary-portal/donors.ts:174-192 falls back to 'Active'. P16 :1068 says 'Unknown never defaults to healthy, lapsed, zero, or received'. This is an implementation safeguard, not a reason to change Q1. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: As proposed, but test the explicit-value write path, not every default. A projection label for incomplete history belongs to its projection owner.

#### v4-14: Residual SiteStacker writes after go-live need detection, not only attestation

- **What could go wrong:** A forgotten form, text-to-give keyword, QR code or staff habit keeps writing gifts or people into SiteStacker after the boundary, while the attestation stays green. New donors and one-time gifts then exist only in SiteStacker.
- **Why it matters:** Option C by accident. The missing money surfaces only at year-end.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** q01-r5 E-22; q01-r6 edge case (a catch-up containing gifts from a form attested as off); r10-gap-g4 (Asym cannot freeze SiteStacker).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Each catch-up plan classifies post-boundary rows. One-time gifts, new source person IDs and form-origin rows are residual-write signals that block the commit pending re-attestation.
- **Spec language:** Every catch-up plan reports post-boundary source rows that are not occurrences of a legacy recurring arrangement classified at the boundary. Any such row blocks that plan's commit pending re-attestation of the Legacy Outward Surface Register.
- **Bucket:** monitor (signal: Count of post-boundary source rows in each catch-up or processor report that are not charges of a legacy recurring arrangement classified at the boundary (one-time gifts, new source person IDs, form-origin rows).; threshold: Any one row.; owner: The Phase 30 run owner for the Switch, with the ministry admin who signed the attestation.; response: Hold the commit for those rows. Require re-attestation of the Legacy Outward Surface Register. List affected donors for the receipt owner. Route the rows through v4-12 quarantine.)
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Reasonable, but mostly a detection add-on to v4-08's attestation. The cited evidence is review cross-references, not owner text. Treating a new source person ID as a block may be too blunt, since recurring-donor updates can legitimately appear. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Each catch-up plan flags rows that look like residual writes after the boundary (one-time gifts, gifts from forms attested off) for reviewer acknowledgement. They block only when they contradict the declared surface dispositions.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Source of truth / data integrity (high): the gifts-only catch-up drops adverse facts that SiteStacker or Mailchimp receive after the boundary, such as unsubscribes, do-not-contact flags, deceased marks, address changes and erasure requests. A gifts-only rule means Asym ignores them, or a person who opted out is treated as contactable. Basis: decision-log F12 (:118) and g4 (:140), which say Mailchimp drift needs a fresh export per import, and P13 :745-747 on erasure. Fix: a narrow reviewed catch-up for adverse consent and identity facts, applied only to restrict, never to widen.
- Source of truth (medium): year-end statements for the switch year. Gifts before the boundary (imported, already_receipted), gifts after it charged by SiteStacker, and gifts after it charged by Asym all fall in one tax year. No rule says which system issues the annual statement for each cohort, or how to avoid duplicate or partial statements (Phase 19; P13 :1030-1034 receipt boundary). v4-04 covers receipts per gift only.
- Data integrity (medium): the Switch Boundary has no defined form. It needs to be an instant with a time zone and a tie rule, matched by source ID across overlapping export windows. Otherwise gifts dated on boundary day are missed or doubled. P16 uses donor-anchored civil-date schedules (P13 :733), so a date-only boundary conflicts. Several reviewer fixes say 'measured from the Switch Boundary' without defining it.
- Hidden coupling (medium): no go/no-go or abort path after the ministry-declared freeze. If the final reconciliation fails, the plan must say whether the freeze is extended, SiteStacker resumes as system of record, or a partial commit is rolled back. Reversal must not delete Parties or rewind financial history (roadmap.md:4371). Option A as recorded only assumes success.
- Data integrity / technical debt (low-medium): practice runs that 'change nothing' still put full supporter PII and payment metadata into Asym staging. Rehearsal snapshot retention, disposal and tenant isolation belong under P29 custody rules (PR #2430, unmerged) and are not stated. Repeated practice runs multiply stored copies.
- Hidden coupling (medium): donor self-service for legacy-executor cohorts after go-live. If supporters move to Asym while SiteStacker still charges their recurring gifts, the Asym portal cannot cancel or update those gifts (P16 :1264, external/read-only), and the SiteStacker donor portal may be off. Each cohort needs a stated cancellation and update channel. v4-08 cites q01-r4 G3 but does not make this a requirement.

### 5.15 Security and privacy risks

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v3). **Synthesis verdict:** There is no inherent flaw. Gaps: no PAN, secret or credential scan at intake; a second live donor store with no privacy forwarding during the tail; real PII in practice runs before custody and agreement exist (F2); no minimization for excluded or restricted data; no explicit 'no legacy credentials' rule. Tokens and mapping files are restricted evidence.

**What was checked:** Checked PCI and credential exposure paths in the switch: full database or WMTEK packages, processor PAN files, iATS unmask, and refinement 3 attestation evidence (r1 A4/C4/C5, V33/V47/V55/V56; r2; r3). Checked for detection or quarantine rules in the Phase 29 spec (PR #2430 head 1483a349) and Phase 30 owner text (none found), and Phase 13 PCI intent (:542-544, :743-746). Checked the plaintext tenant-key path against P13 :659-667, and privacy duties with two live stores during the tail (P13 :746-747; C10; FTC CAN-SPAM guide verified). Checked custody and retention for practice snapshots (C11, F2, F11, D13), the claim and invitation design (identity-email-normalization.md:66-69; #509; #510), Phase 10 restricted-tier aliasing (Phase 10 PRD :95-99), and the PCI high-value-token concept (snippet-only).

#### v3-S1: Credential- and card-bearing artifacts can enter Asym during the switch

- **What could go wrong:** Ministries asked for exports may send: full database or WMTEK packages (password hashes, per-user API secrets, gateway credentials, missionaries' Mailchimp API keys, pre-login tokens); an old processor's PAN file by mistake; an iATS-style unmasked card export; or screenshots and config exports of settings pages as refinement 3 'evidence'. No Phase 29 or Phase 30 owner text requires card, bank-number or secret detection at Phase 30 intake.
- **Why it matters:** Holding PAN expands Asym's PCI DSS scope against ratified intent. Holding live third-party credentials lets staff or attackers charge donors on legacy gateways or mail from missionaries' accounts.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** critical / medium
- **Evidence:** repo-ratified: P13 :542-544 (story 172: never handle raw card or bank credentials), :743-746 ('Asym never handles raw PAN, CVV/CVC, or full bank credentials'). repo-published absence: PR #2430 custody volume F30 row :368, :380 have no detection rule; r09 §4.4 checklist lacks it. r1 A4/C4/C5 (vendor V33, V47, V55, V56); r2 iATS unmask (third-party); r3 mistaken PAN upload; P29 AC29-D05-02 and AC29-D05-05.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Allowlisted intake families and columns. Card, bank-number and secret detection before parse or stage. Positive results quarantine without parsing or preview and go to Phase 29 disposal, with no override. Refuse whole-database packages unless a reviewed table and column allowlist is applied before Asym receives them. The attestation is a structured checklist with no screenshots or config exports.
- **Spec language:** Phase 30 never accepts raw card numbers, CVV/CVC, full bank account numbers, password hashes, session or pre-login tokens, or API or gateway credentials. Intake runs card, bank-number and secret detection before parsing. A positive result quarantines the file without parsing or preview, starts Phase 29 disposal, and cannot be released into staging by any override. The legacy-system attestation is a structured checklist answered per item by an identified attester; Asym does not request or store screenshots or configuration exports of integration, gateway or API settings. AC: a CSV with one valid test PAN, a file with a bcrypt column and a file with an sk_live_-shaped string are each quarantined unparsed; a CRM export with non-Luhn numeric IDs is not.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Ratified intent is verified at P13 :542-544 and :743-746. The PR #2430 F30 row (custody :368) and AC29-D22-01 (:380) indeed have no PAN or secret detection rule. 'Critical' is overstated. SiteStacker's normal exports are not shown to contain PAN, so the realistic path is a mistaken upload or a whole-database dump. 'Detection before parse' contradicts 'quarantine without parsing', because detection is a content scan. The 'no override' rule needs a remedy path, re-export, to handle Luhn false positives. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Accept only allowlisted families and columns. Scan content for card, bank-account and secret patterns before staging. A hit quarantines the file without preview, and the only remedy is a corrected re-export. There is no in-Asym override, and the file goes to Phase 29 disposal. Refuse whole-database dumps in v1. The attestation is a structured checklist.

#### v3-S2: No legacy processor or SiteStacker credentials in Asym; tail inputs are exported files

- **What could go wrong:** A months-long tail creates pressure to automate catch-ups by obtaining and storing the ministry's old Stripe, PayPal, Braintree or SiteStacker API keys. The prototype already stores plaintext tenant secret keys. Asym would then hold read_write authority over accounts where another executor runs, and a leaked key could refund, charge or read donors.
- **Why it matters:** It contradicts ratified Phase 13 access rules, violates C9's external read-only executor status, and reintroduces the v3-T1 hazards.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** repo-ratified: P13 :661 ('Do not use OAuth'), :664-667 ('never a stored tenant key'). repo-integrated: init_schema.sql:20 stripe_secret_key TEXT; tenant-client.ts:42-66. r1 C4 (V33: API requests act 'with the authority of the user'); r4 'migration-only read-only observation grant' needs a Phase 13 amendment; verifier note on OAuth read_only scope (docs.stripe.com/connect/oauth-standard-accounts, verified-vendor-doc).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** v1 catch-up and census inputs are ministry-exported files under Phase 29 custody. Asym never requests, stores or uses legacy credentials. Migrated tenants bind only through the Phase 13 Settlement Account Binding. A read-only observation grant requires a Phase 13 amendment first.
- **Spec language:** During readiness, switch and tail, Asym never requests, receives, stores or uses credentials for a legacy processor account, SiteStacker, Mailchimp or another legacy integration. Catch-up and census inputs are ministry-exported files under Phase 29 custody. A migrated tenant has no value in any stored-secret field; its money access is the Phase 13 Settlement Account Binding only.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: This restates ratified P13 :661 (no OAuth) and :664-667 (never a stored tenant key). It is correct and useful as an explicit Phase 30 rule. It partially overlaps v3-T1's evidence on the stored key, and its severity is lower because it mostly reaffirms existing intent. (corrected severity: medium)

#### v3-S3: The late tail leaves a second live donor store outside Asym's privacy controls

- **What could go wrong:** SiteStacker stays live for legacy recurring gifts, receipts, decline emails and the dashboard, still holding the full donor database, staff and donor logins, API keys and pre-login tokens. Missionary Mailchimp lists keep sending. Erasure, do-not-contact and unsubscribe requests honoured in Asym are not executed in SiteStacker, processors or missionary lists. Catch-ups can re-identify erased donors. Staff removed in Asym keep SiteStacker access. There is no SiteStacker deletion decision at decommission.
- **Why it matters:** Erasure and opt-out duties bind the ministry across both systems; FTC: opt-outs within 10 business days and responsibility cannot be contracted away. Departed-staff access is a standing PII exposure. 'Asym becomes the only place for supporters' is false for privacy duties during the tail.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** r5 K1/K2/E-50; r2 recurring-tail recommendations; r1 C1/C4/C5 (V56 pre-login tokens, 3-day default); verifier on 151000122392 (manual one-way export to missionary-owned Mailchimp); repo-ratified P13 :746-747 (erased donors not re-identified from provider metadata); C10. Verified official guidance https://www.ftc.gov/business-guidance/resources/can-spam-act-compliance-guide-business (2026-11-01): 'You must honor a recipient's opt-out request within 10 business days'. Applicability to nonprofit solicitations unverified.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** The switch record carries a 'forward to legacy systems' list of post-boundary privacy requests until a named ministry user marks each executed. Catch-ups honour Asym erasure and suppression. At the boundary, shrink SiteStacker staff access, revoke per-user API keys and rotate integration credentials. The decommission checklist records the SiteStacker data deletion or retention decision.
- **Spec language:** From the boundary until tail closure, each privacy request recorded in Asym for a migrated supporter is listed on the switch record as 'pending in legacy systems' until a named ministry user marks it executed in SiteStacker, each legacy processor and each connected missionary mailing account. Catch-up runs never create or restore contact, consent or identity data for a Party that is erased, suppressed or marked do-not-contact in Asym. The boundary checklist includes reducing SiteStacker staff access to named minimum users, revoking per-user API keys and rotating integration credentials; the decommission checklist records the ministry's SiteStacker data deletion or retention decision.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Option A leaves SiteStacker charging recurring gifts until the Phase 16 handover, so a second live donor store genuinely persists. P13 :746-747 (erased donors are not re-identified) is verified. The reviewer correctly flags that CAN-SPAM applicability to nonprofit solicitation is unverified, and it is generally scoped to commercial messages, so do not cite it as binding. Revoking and rotating SiteStacker credentials are ministry-side checklist items that Asym cannot enforce. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: The switch record keeps a 'forward to legacy systems' list of post-boundary suppression and erasure requests, each closed by a named ministry user. Catch-ups honour Asym erasure and suppression. A ministry-attested decommission checklist covers SiteStacker access reduction, credential rotation and the data deletion or retention decision.

#### v3-M1: Monitor privacy requests not yet executed in legacy systems

- **What could go wrong:** Forwarded erasure or opt-out items sit unexecuted in SiteStacker, processors or missionary lists while those systems keep mailing or holding data.
- **Why it matters:** It is the operational failure mode of v3-S3 during a tail expected to last months.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** v3-S3 evidence; FTC CAN-SPAM guide 10-business-day opt-out rule (verified official guidance, 2026-11-01).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Track the age and count of open 'pending in legacy systems' items per switch, and block tail closure while any are open.
- **Spec language:** Tail closure and decommission sign-off are blocked while any 'pending in legacy systems' privacy item is open. Items older than 10 business days escalate to the ministry admin and the Asym switch operator.
- **Bucket:** monitor (signal: Age of the oldest open 'pending in legacy systems' privacy item, and the open-item count, per open switch.; threshold: Any item open more than 10 business days (FTC opt-out window used as a conservative operational limit, not a legal conclusion for nonprofits).; owner: The ministry's named switch owner, with the Phase 30 switch-record owner for visibility.; response: Escalate to the ministry admin and the Asym switch operator, block tail closure and decommission sign-off, and hold any catch-up row touching an affected Party.)
- **Skeptic ruling:** duplicate: This is the monitoring half of v3-S3's forward-to-legacy list, and blocking tail closure is already implied there. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Fold into v3-S3.

#### v3-S4: Practice runs need real donor data before custody, retention and contract basis exist

- **What could go wrong:** Each practice run is a full copy of the donor database (addresses, birthdays, notes, bank holder names, routing numbers, possibly sponsored children). Phase 29 custody is unimplemented, and F2 asks whether a prospect's export may be held at all. Practice data ends up on developer machines, in preview or demo environments (the demo seed defaults to the production ref; hosted databases drift), or in shared drives and email, with no retention and possibly no processing terms.
- **Why it matters:** Multiple ungoverned copies of the most sensitive dataset the product handles, held before the controls the roadmap requires.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** C11 (P29 custody, PR #2430 unmerged); decision log §8 (no private import storage or malware scanning), F2, F11, D13 (scripts/seed-demo.sh:6 defaults to the production ref); r5 S1 (multiple rehearsals, fresh export each); repo grep found no DPA or processing-terms text in program docs (unverified).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Accept real exports only into Phase 29 custody for the target tenant (or one designated environment with production-equivalent controls agreed with Phase 29), never local, preview or demo. Dispose of practice snapshots and derivatives N days after reconciliation acceptance or abort. No real export before the customer agreement and processing terms; synthetic or redacted data until then.
- **Spec language:** Real SiteStacker exports are accepted only into Phase 29 custody for the target tenant, never into local, preview or demo environments. Every practice snapshot and its staging derivatives are disposed N days after the switch's reconciliation acceptance or abort, and disposal is recorded. No real export is accepted before the ministry's customer agreement and data-processing terms are in force.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Phase 29 custody is unmerged (PR #2430). The decision log notes there is no private import storage or scanning, and rehearsals need fresh real exports. Requiring processing terms before receiving real donor data is a proportionate legal precondition. The absence of a DPA is reported as unverified, correctly. (corrected severity: high)

#### v3-S5: Imported unverified emails become claim and invitation targets

- **What could go wrong:** A post-switch 'claim your account' invitation (r1 question) sent to imported placeholder, shared, stale or recycled emails lets whoever controls the inbox claim the record and see decades of giving, addresses and possibly vault-copied methods.
- **Why it matters:** It could disclose a supporter's history to the wrong person. Phase 4's design mitigates per-record invitations, but a switch-driven bulk path would be new.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** repo-integrated: identity-email-normalization.md:66-69 (email_verified_at set only by the claim path). repo-published: #509 (shared email and household are not authority), #510 (one invitation per call, consent-gated, no bulk). roadmap.md:4365 (no account grant from imported rows). r1 C1/K22 as corrected by the verifier; r1 §7 placeholder and shared emails.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** The switch issues no invitations or claim proofs. Imported email is recorded as unverified legacy contact data. Census-flagged placeholder, shared or role emails make a Party invitation-ineligible until staff verify it. Bulk invitations require a separate Phase 4/17 design.
- **Spec language:** Imports never set email verification or claim state, and the switch sends no account invitations. Parties whose imported email is flagged placeholder, shared or role-based are excluded from any later legacy invitation until a staff member records verification.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: This is consistent with roadmap.md:4365 (no account grant from imported rows) and with #509/#510. The fix is proportionate. (corrected severity: medium)

#### v3-S6: Excluded and restricted-tier data has no minimization rule

- **What could go wrong:** Visible exclusion dispositions for child sponsorship (C13) could retain children's names, photos and letters in staging, issue grids and error files. Restricted-tier missionaries could be committed before classification: Phase 10 rejects a restricted level without an alias, but a mapping that sets no level leaves the country-risk default or standard. Applicant passports, background checks and care notes can surface in error downloads.
- **Why it matters:** Minors' and at-risk workers' data is the highest-harm category in the program. A 'visible exclusion' that keeps the PII defeats minimization.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** C13; r1 K21 (sponsorship money mixed in Contributions), V50 (household children), V73 (child letters), B14 (passports, background checks); repo-ratified Phase 10 PRD :95-99 (A1-A3: security_level, alias required, import without alias rejected); C17.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Exclusion dispositions keep only source key, family, reason and counts. Identifiable excluded content is dropped at parse, or held in restricted staging disposed with the snapshot. A Phase 10 classification gate applies before worker and missionary Party commit. Issue grids and error files redact care and security classes and require the raw-row capability.
- **Spec language:** An excluded record's disposition retains only the source key, family, reason and counts, never its personal content; content identifiable as excluded is not persisted past parsing. No worker or missionary Party is committed until its Phase 10 security level is set by a reviewer or derived from an explicit country mapping; restricted and high-risk Parties require an alias before commit. Error downloads and issue grids redact care- and security-classified values.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Excluded families (sponsorship, child data, passports and background checks per r1) need minimization, and the Phase 10 alias and security_level gate is ratified. It overlaps v3-T3 on restricted classes but adds the disposition-minimization rule. (corrected severity: high)

#### v3-S7: Vault tokens and processor mapping files are high-value evidence

- **What could go wrong:** The inventory keeps processor references, possibly gateway vault tokens, and the Stripe mapping file linking legacy IDs to live methods with fingerprints. If these sit in general staging, error files or exports, anyone who later holds merchant credentials can use them to initiate charges.
- **Why it matters:** It widens exposure for no Phase 30 purpose. PCI guidance (2011 supplement, per press coverage) treats tokens usable to initiate transactions as potentially in scope.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** r1 §4 (references, tokens if exported, brand, last4, expiry); r3 mapping-file edge case; PCI SSC Tokenization Guidelines Info Supplement (2011) via press coverage, snippet-only (PDF at pcisecuritystandards.org not rendered).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Keep only references Phase 16 names. Store tokens and mapping files as restricted provider evidence, excluded from grids, error downloads and exports, and disposed with the snapshot unless Phase 16 retains them in its own custody.
- **Spec language:** Gateway vault tokens and processor mapping files are restricted provider evidence. They are excluded from issue grids, error downloads and exports, and retained only if the Phase 16 owner names them as required cutover evidence.
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The PCI tokenization evidence is snippet-only. Processor tokens and old-to-new mapping files are not PAN and are out of PCI scope when irreversible. They are sensitive operational evidence, but this largely duplicates the custody and restricted-evidence handling in v3-S1 and v3-S6. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Keep only the references Phase 16 names. Exclude tokens and mapping files from grids and downloads, and dispose of them with the snapshot unless Phase 16 retains them.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Tenant safety: webhooks mutating imported history on the same Stripe account. If a ministry already used Stripe and Asym binds that account through Connect, events for SiteStacker-era objects reach Asym's webhook (charge.refunded, disputes, subscription updates). event-store.ts:150-155 resolves these by payment intent, and refunds.ts/recurring.ts update matched rows, so imported historical gifts or pledges could be rewritten by the webhook outside import lineage and append-only ledger rules. Fix: Phase 13 must route events for imported or legacy objects to adoption and reconciliation review, not live mutation (repo-integrated evidence; inferred interaction).
- Tenant safety: donor billing portal against a legacy customer. billing.ts:39-42 opens a Stripe billing portal for donors.stripe_customer_id. If an import writes a legacy cus_ ID and the Party is later claimed or mis-merged, a donor could cancel or edit subscriptions SiteStacker still runs, or see another person's saved payment methods. v3-T1 cites billing.ts but does not name this donor-facing path. The portal must not open for un-adopted legacy customers.
- Database/RLS: inside a tenant, the composite FK covers only donations.donor_id (20261025100000:10-13). Imported donations naming missionary_id or fund_id from another tenant are not blocked structurally (no (tenant_id, missionary_id|fund_id) FK found). Phase 30 mapping must check that every resolved target ID is in the same tenant before invoking the command, not rely on the schema.
- Security/privacy: error files and issue-grid downloads re-exported as CSV need formula-injection neutralization and the raw-row capability. Without it, operators opening them in Excel are exposed (check r09 coverage; not raised in v3).
- Security/privacy: decision-log D9/O7 must be corrected to say #2295 does not revoke DML. Also, the review's inference that signup sets app_metadata.tenant_id is wrong: 20260226113000:223-226 reads server-set raw_app_meta_data. Left uncorrected, a later author may over-trust or misread the authorization baseline.

### 5.16 Scalability and performance risks

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v5). **Synthesis verdict:** Mostly planning risk. The pause and outage length cannot be predicted from no-write rehearsals, so the representative migration must measure committed throughput. SiteStacker parser bounds must be measured, not the D17 bounds (D12). Backfill cost grows with history because there is no change feed, so diff by source ID with an evidence-based lookback. Inngest fan-out design and the Historic Giving aggregate basis choice are implementation notes.

**What was checked:** I checked the #2347 bounds (body line 22) and #2087 AC28-R25-01 limits, the #2087 comment 6393929266 pin IMPORT_TENANT_CONCURRENCY=1, and the D17 per-profile clause (via r10-gap-g1:164). I also read Phase 29 AC29-D23-01 (PR head) and the Inngest usage limits (https://www.inngest.com/docs/usage-limits/inngest, verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01). Other inputs: apps/admin/vercel.json (no maxDuration); workflows/events.ts:75-91; SiteStacker long-export errors (r10-gap-g3:105, snippet); the 'no change feed' finding (g4); r5 §3.4 limits of rehearsal; and the MS Learn go-live checklist (verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01). No verified size distribution of SiteStacker ministries exists; every volume used is labelled an assumption.

#### v5-S1: The freeze and outage window cannot be predicted, because practice runs that write nothing never exercise commits

- **What could go wrong:** The final window covers dependency-ordered commits of about 11 stages, one committing run per cohort, tenant concurrency 1, owner acceptance and finance sign-off. No-write plans measure neither write throughput nor owner-command latency, so the first real switch is the first time anyone learns the outage length. For a large ministry it can overrun a weekend window.
- **Why it matters:** It drives the giving outage, leaves staff idle and creates pressure to cut corners on switch day.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** r5 §3.4: 'What a rehearsal cannot prove: Chunk recovery under real commit ... (nothing commits)' (proposed, citing r10-gap-g4:174). #2087 comment 6393929266 (D17-scoped pin). MS Learn go-live checklist: 'Test data migration several times before cutover' and 'make sure performance fits in the cutover time window' (verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01). Cutover weekend 'usually less than 48 hours' (verified-vendor-doc via r10-gap-g4:194).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Have the representative migration measure committed throughput per family at a declared size profile on production-shaped infrastructure. Each go/no-go computes the expected outage from the tenant's census and selects the v5-M2 option. Time the manual export in practice runs. Block scheduling for tenants above the measured profile.
- **Spec language:** The representative SiteStacker migration records committed rows per minute per family (with owner commands, quarantine and chunk recovery) for a declared size profile. Each Switch's go/no-go report states the tenant's census counts, the expected commit duration and online-giving outage computed from that profile, and the timed duration of the ministry's final-export procedure from the last practice run. A tenant whose counts exceed the measured profile cannot be scheduled until a larger profile is measured.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Verified: r10-gap-g4:174 says no-write plans cannot prove chunk recovery or late commits 'because nothing commits'. r10-gap-g4:194 quotes MS Learn on mock cutovers and windows under 48 hours. roadmap.md:4403 already requires a representative SiteStacker migration, so measuring committed throughput there is proportionate. This is a planning and outage risk, not a data-integrity risk, and it overlaps v5-M2 (the outage option choice). Medium fits better than high. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Have the representative migration (roadmap.md:4403) record committed throughput per family and the manual export time. Each go/no-go estimates the outage from the tenant census. Treat 'block tenants above the measured profile' as a default the founder may override.

#### v5-S2: The only numeric parser bounds published are D17's, and a ministry export exceeds them by orders of magnitude

- **What could go wrong:** #2347 (Ready) hard-codes 10,000 rows, 500,000 slots, 32,768-byte cells and 16,384 columns, and #2087 adds 10 MiB per upload. With r1's 'every column ticked' exports, a 60-column Contributions file caps at about 8,333 rows and a 150-column People file at about 3,333 (assumed column counts). Any ministry with more than 10,000 transactions breaches the row bound (assumption: most with a few years of recurring giving). A breach on switch day blocks go-live.
- **Why it matters:** Either the SiteStacker profile cannot use the shared reader, or shared constants pinned by D17 tests get raised, against D17 'not universal' and Phase 29's rule that unknown limits block a profile.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** #2347 body line 22 (repo-published). #2087 AC28-R25-01 '10 MiB ... 500,000 parsed slots across the whole file/workbook including explicit empty slots' (repo-published). phase28-d17 review.md:395-399 via r10-gap-g1:164 (repo-ratified). AC29-D23-01 (PR #2430 head, unmerged). decision-log D12 and §6.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Make bounds a per-profile field, with D17 keeping its ratified values. Publish measured SiteStacker profile bounds before any switch is scheduled. Accept partitioned manifest members only when their union reconciles to the UI counts.
- **Spec language:** Parser and staging bounds are per-profile configuration. The SiteStacker staff-migration profile publishes measured bounds (bytes, rows, slots, columns, cell bytes, files per manifest, wall time) before any Switch is scheduled. A family exceeding them is partitioned into manifest members whose union must reconcile to the source UI count with no overlap. D17 bounds are never the SiteStacker profile's bounds.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Duplicate in substance of decision-log D12 and section 6 (#2347 has no per-profile bounds; D17 limits only), which the founder has already been flagged on. The only new content is publishing measured SiteStacker bounds and reconciling partitioned exports. The partitioning point is already in r10-gap-g3:105. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Reference D12 and add: SiteStacker profile bounds are measured and published before scheduling, and the union of partitioned exports reconciles to the UI control counts (r10-gap-g3:105).

#### v5-S4: Catch-up cost grows with history, not with the delta, because SiteStacker has no change feed

- **What could go wrong:** SiteStacker has no change feed, filtering by modified date is unverified, and long exports error out (2.48). Refunds appear as new rows, but schedule status changes, re-designations and Move Payment Method edit existing rows. A date-filtered catch-up misses those edits, and a full re-export each cycle costs as much as the final export: about 19 families plus partitions, processor files and Mailchimp files.
- **Why it matters:** Each cycle repeats the ministry admin's export effort, exports fail, and changes are missed.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** decision-log.md:140 (no change feed). r10-gap-g4:272 (modified-date filter unverified). r10-gap-g3:105 (2.48 long exports error; snippet-only). r1 D13 (refund is a new transaction) and X2 (Move Payment Method, re-designation) (verified-vendor-doc via r1).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Bound each catch-up: transactions from boundary minus 120 days, plus the full Recurring views. Diff by source ID. Take one full pre-closure snapshot to surface older-row changes as conflicts. Record export time and partition count per cycle.
- **Spec language:** A late-tail catch-up manifest contains (a) Contributions transactions dated from the Switch Boundary minus 120 days to the export time and (b) the complete Recurring and Recurring Campaign views. The plan diffs by source ID against the previous manifest. One full final snapshot is taken before tail closure to surface changes to older rows as source-changed conflicts. Each catch-up records the ministry's export elapsed time and partition count.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: No change feed (decision-log.md:140) and an unverified modified-date filter (r10-gap-g4:272) are verified, so full-history re-export per catch-up is a real cost. Partly duplicates v5-D2 and v5-M5. The '120 days' window is an unlabelled assumption: it resembles card chargeback windows but SiteStacker refunds and re-designations have no documented limit (q01-r1 D13). It should not be fixed in the decision record. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Record that catch-ups diff by source ID against the prior snapshot and that the lookback window is set from evidence on the first real export (label any number an assumption). One full pre-closure snapshot surfaces older-row changes as conflicts.

#### v5-S6: Proving overlap between Contributions and Historic Giving row by row may be infeasible at scale without a shared ID

- **What could go wrong:** Historic Giving copies native Contributions income automatically (K19). If the copied rows lack the Contributions transaction ID, one-to-one overlap proof across years of history becomes either unsafe fuzzy money matching or a very large manual review.
- **Why it matters:** Money families are blocked, or lifetime totals and first-gift facts double (C22).
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** r1 K19 (q01-r1:149): 'It is unknown whether the copied rows carry the Contributions transaction ID' (verified-vendor-doc via r1 for the overlap; the column is unverified). Phase 13 story 171 (repo-ratified).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Choose the gift basis per period by aggregate proof (counts and per-currency totals by period and Site). Import only the chosen basis and keep the other as provenance. Pair rows only where a shared ID exists.
- **Spec language:** For each period the kit selects exactly one gift basis (Contributions or Historic Giving), supported by aggregate count and per-currency total comparisons by period and Site. Row-level pairing between the two sources uses only an exported shared identifier. Without one, the non-selected source for that period is retained as provenance and never imported as gifts.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Largely restates q01-r1:149 (K19), which already recommends picking 'one source basis per gift population and period' and proving the overlap. The shared-ID column is unverified, as stated. This is not new risk, and at that level it is not a scalability issue. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Cite K19. Choose the per-period basis by aggregate counts and per-currency totals by period and Site, and pair rows only on an exported shared ID.

#### v5-S3: Inngest platform limits make 'one run per family' a fan-out design, not one function

- **What could go wrong:** A design of one function per family, one step per chunk, with outcomes kept in step results, breaks at 1,000 steps per function, 4 MiB per step return and 32 MiB of run state. Step duration is capped by the host timeout (vercel.json sets no maxDuration; the plan default is unverified). Plan limits (for example, Free tier: 5 concurrent steps, 100,000 queue depth, 500,000 events per month) are unverified for Asym. Per-row events would also defeat batch-origin suppression.
- **Why it matters:** Runs would fail or stall partway through, in the middle of the switch window.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** https://www.inngest.com/docs/usage-limits/inngest (verified-vendor-doc, accessed 2026-11-01). owner-constraints.md 'Background processing via Inngest: chunked ... batch-origin event suppression' (repo-published). workflows/events.ts:75-91 strict envelope; single app asym-core-workflows per r06 6.1 (repo-integrated). apps/admin/vercel.json (repo-integrated).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Hold the run, cursor and per-row outcomes in Postgres. Dispatch idempotent chunk executions keyed by (run, chunk index) with no per-row events. Size chunks from measured wall time below the host timeout. Record the plan limits in the measured profile.
- **Spec language:** Import runs hold plan, cursor and per-row outcomes in Postgres. Inngest executes bounded chunks keyed by (run, chunk index), each chunk commit is idempotent on that key, and the cursor never advances past an uncommitted lower chunk. No import emits a per-row event. Chunk size is set so measured p99 chunk wall time stays below half the host function timeout. The deployment records its Inngest plan limits and host timeout as part of the measured profile.
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified: packages/api/src/workflows/events.ts:77-91 is a strict, identifier-only envelope, and apps/admin/vercel.json sets no maxDuration. A Postgres-held run and cursor with idempotent chunk dispatch is consistent with owner-constraints.md:386-388 and roadmap 'Recoverable processing'. This is ordinary implementation guidance, not a Q1 risk. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Keep as an implementation safeguard for the spec. It has no bearing on the switch-model decision.

#### v5-S5: The quarantine and exception backlog can grow unobserved across catch-ups

- **What could go wrong:** Each catch-up adds to quarantine: unlinked processor facts, unmatched tail Parties (K3) and absent rows. With no threshold, the backlog grows until tail closure becomes impossible, and run failures are invisible after handoff today (O11).
- **Why it matters:** Financial accuracy decays silently and the tail never closes.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** Phase 13 story 171 (repo-ratified). decision-log.md:97 (O11). r5 E-22 and E-49 (proposed).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Show open migration exceptions by class and age on each Switch, with a threshold that pauses further catch-ups and escalates to Phase 13 finance exception review.
- **Spec language:** Each Switch shows open migration exceptions by class and age. A catch-up whose quarantined share exceeds the configured threshold (default 5%), or any exception older than 30 days, pauses the next scheduled catch-up and notifies the Switch's run owner and finance approver. Tail closure requires zero unexplained exceptions.
- **Bucket:** monitor (signal: Quarantined share of rows per catch-up; count and age of open migration exceptions by class (unlinked processor fact, unmatched Party, absent row, source-changed conflict).; threshold: More than 5% of a catch-up's rows quarantined, any exception older than 30 days, or any unexplained exception at the tail-closure review.; owner: The Switch's named Phase 30 run owner, with the ministry finance approver.; response: Pause further catch-ups for that Switch, escalate money exceptions to Phase 13 finance exception review, and record a disposition for each before the next cadence resumes.)
- **Skeptic ruling:** duplicate: Covered by O11 (run-level terminal states and visibility) plus Phase 13 story 171 finance exception review. A per-Switch exception age and threshold view is a reasonable monitor detail under O11. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Add 'open migration exceptions by class and age per Switch, with a pause threshold for further catch-ups' to O11.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Migration/rollout: The 'freeze' cannot cover money. q01-r2:14 (verified-vendor-doc: SiteStacker CRON 151000097037 and article 151000115212) shows SiteStacker's own cron charges card and ACH recurring gifts from tokens in the ministry's processor vault. Refinement (2) therefore needs SiteStacker's recurring engine left running after the boundary while forms and Spark are off. If a ministry reads 'Asym becomes the only place' or refinement (3) as 'switch SiteStacker off', or lets the licence lapse, recurring revenue stops at once. The Q1 record should say which SiteStacker functions stay on until each Phase 16 cohort cutover. Severity high.
- Migration/rollout: Double charges at handover. If vault tokens are copied or adopted for Asym or Phase 16 collection while SiteStacker's cron still holds the same tokens, both executors can charge the same donor (P16 :1314 forbids active-active, but stop proof for SiteStacker's cron schedules is unverified). Nothing shows SiteStacker can bulk-disable or evidence-stop individual recurring schedules. That capability is a Phase 16 readiness unknown the review did not raise. Severity high.
- Dependency: Refinement (2)'s 'gifts-only' catch-up drops schedule state changes made in SiteStacker during the tail: donor cancellations, amount or date edits, and Move Payment Method (q01-r1 X2). Phase 16 adoption (#834) would then classify stale schedules, and a donor cancellation recorded only in SiteStacker could be lost (P16 :1293 treats cancellation as top priority). v5-S4 mentions 'full Recurring views' only as a diff input. The record should say that schedule-state deltas route to Phase 16 as evidence, while supporter details are still never overwritten. Severity high.
- Dependency/scalability: One ministry may run several processors and merchant accounts split by Site, currency and payment method (q01-r2:18, verified-vendor-doc 151000118930 plus vendor-served code). The review's tail, reconciliation and stop-proof concerns assume one processor. Reconciliation and tail closure need a per-processor, per-merchant dimension, and Phase 16 cutover multiplies accordingly. Severity medium.
- Dependency: Deferred History, final-snapshot custody and catch-up manifests (v5-D1, v5-D2, v5-S4) all assume Phase 29 private custody and F11 retention. Phase 29 is an unmerged spec (PR #2430), nothing is implemented (decision-log.md:150), and F2 asks whether Asym may hold a prospect's export before custody exists. The review should have made Phase 29 implementation readiness an explicit go/no-go dependency. Severity medium.
- Migration/rollout: Switch-date timing against the tax year. A mid-year boundary splits a donor's year between imported history and Asym gifts for Phase 19 year-end statements and Phase 7 receipts, and the Phase 7 import fact is undefined (D4). F13 lists cutover-year statements, but the review did not tie this to scheduling the switch, for example avoiding Q4 or year-end boundaries until Phase 7 and Phase 19 define combined-year behaviour. Severity medium.

### 5.17 Operational burden

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v1). **Synthesis verdict:** A moves the burden into a long, manual money tail with no owner for the processor half. It needs a bounded tail policy, an ownership split routed to P13/P16, a hard gate against the platform-key fallback, and switch-year statement rules tied to a ministry-configured window, not a fixed date.

**What was checked:** P16 :1236, :1264, :1312-1317; P13 :165, :743-746, :2192-2199; owner-constraints.md:424-430; roadmap.md:4395, :4403; decision-log D13; CONTEXT.md:2845-2846; packages/api/src/stripe/tenant-client.ts:59-66 (platform-key fallback, verified); phase-19 :944, :976-982; Stripe processor-export and dispute windows via r5 (verified-vendor-doc); r4 §6 workstream analysis with verifier corrections.

#### v1-11: Recurring tail is unbounded: SiteStacker licence, catch-ups and two-system support continue indefinitely

- **What could go wrong:** P16 formal cutover (#809, #834) is unbuilt, ACH is blocked until D.7, and processor exports take days to months. On top of that come about 120 days of disputes and 60 days of returns. The ministry pays for SiteStacker and runs catch-ups for months, possibly forever, which drifts into the money-side 'permanent dual writer'.
- **Why it matters:** Recurring cost and staff time, a growing review backlog and authority confusion. It also raises the chance SiteStacker is turned off early (v1-01).
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** P16 :1314, :1236, :1264 (repo-ratified); #809 and #834 OPEN; https://docs.stripe.com/get-started/data-migrations/pan-import 'a few days to several months' (verified-vendor-doc via r5, 2026-11-01); r5 S9, E-49; roadmap.md:4395. SiteStacker licence terms unverified.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Record a tail policy: a target maximum length, a fixed cadence, a closure rule (hand over, or end with donor notice and a restart invitation), a final catch-up after dispute windows, and monitoring.
- **Spec language:** Each switch records a recurring-tail closure date at scheduling, a fixed catch-up cadence, and a final catch-up after the dispute and return windows for the last legacy charge. By the closure date, every legacy recurring arrangement is either handed over under Phase 16 or ended by the ministry with donor notice and an invitation to restart in Asym. None continues untracked after closure. Extending the closure date is a recorded ministry decision with a reason.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Correct. P16 :1264 allows external read-only as a steady state, and #809 and #834 are OPEN, so nothing forces the tail to close. Caveats: the SiteStacker licence and tail-mode terms are unverified. A closure rule that ends donors' gifts is a founder product choice with money at stake, not an obvious fix. It should be offered as an option, with recorded extension allowed, as the reviewer partly does. The tail-length overlap with v1-01 is acceptable: v1-01 is wording, this concern is bounding. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Ask the founder for a tail policy: target closure date, cadence, final catch-up after dispute and return windows, and monitoring. Present 'end with donor notice' and 'extend per cohort' as founder options, not a default.

#### v1-12: Option A's freeze and reconcile steps overlap the processor-side migration workstream, which has no owner

- **What could go wrong:** P13 assigns processor coordination, vault transfer, re-auth campaigns, cutover freezes, bulk reconciliation, connected-account readiness and executor replacement at scale to an unnamed workstream. Either Phase 30 absorbs these by shorthand, which C3 forbids, or each switch becomes ad hoc founder work.
- **Why it matters:** Tribal-knowledge work repeats for every ministry with recurring giving, and the money rules have no enforcer.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** phase-13 :743-746, :2192-2199, :165 (repo-ratified, verified). roadmap.md:4377, :4363 (CRM ETL effectively Phase 30, verifier-corrected). decision-log C3. Phase 30 text never mentions processors (grep absence via r4 verifier).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Record the split. Phase 30 owns freeze attestation, boundary, snapshots and reconciliation reports. P13 owns readiness and mapping evidence. P16 (with P17/6) owns stop proof and re-auth. A named ops role owns processor coordination and the runbook.
- **Spec language:** Within a switch, Phase 30 records the ministry-declared freeze attestation and the boundary, receives snapshots, and produces reconciliation reports. It does not coordinate processors, transfer vaults, run re-authorization campaigns or prove that an old executor stopped. Those duties are assigned by owner edits before the first switch: connected-account readiness and vault-mapping evidence to Phase 13; old-stop proof and donor re-authorization or confirmation to Phase 16 with Phase 17/6 messaging; processor coordination to a named operations role with a published runbook.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Checked. P13 :743-746 and :2190-2200 give processor coordination, vault transfer, re-authorization campaigns, cutover freezes and bulk reconciliation to an unnamed 'separate migration workstream', and P13 :157-166 lists it as a non-goal. C3 forbids Phase 30 taking these over by shorthand. Option A's 'freeze' and 'reconcile' collide with this text directly. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: As proposed. Record the split and flag that the owner edits to P13 and P16 are needed before any switch is offered.

#### v1-13: Each switch needs undesigned operator and developer work: hosted census, months-long operator access, live-code hazards

- **What could go wrong:** Three gaps. (1) The retained-state census gate has no procedure or owner (D13), inviting ad hoc database work. (2) Operator access is time-boxed but the tail lasts months. (3) A rehearsal or go-live on the platform-key fallback could take donor money into the wrong account.
- **Why it matters:** Recurring developer involvement per tenant, and a rollback-hostile failure if live money is touched.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** roadmap.md:4403; decision-log.md:73 (D13); CONTEXT.md:2845-2846; packages/api/src/stripe/tenant-client.ts:64-66 (repo-integrated, verified); r4 G9, G14. Operator grant implementation unverified.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** A scripted read-only census with fixed output run by a named role; a migration-purpose operator grant template renewed per catch-up; a hard gate requiring a verified P13 binding and no possible platform-key fallback.
- **Spec language:** No rehearsal or switch may run for a tenant that lacks a verified Phase 13 Settlement Account Binding or that could resolve to the platform Stripe key. The retained-state census is a scripted read-only procedure with recorded output, run by a named role, and it never involves direct database repair. Asym operator access for a switch uses a migration-purpose Operator Tenant Grant, renewed per catch-up as an audited action.
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Item 1 restates the open discrepancy D13 (decision-log.md:73). Item 3 is checked: tenant-client.ts:64-66 falls back to the platform STRIPE_SECRET_KEY. But that is a Phase 13 precondition for any tenant taking gifts, not something specific to a switch, and its relevance here is mainly through v1-16. Item 2 (operator grant renewals) is reasonable but unverified, because grant implementation status is unknown. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Keep the hard gate: no rehearsal or go-live while the tenant could resolve to the platform key or lacks a verified P13 binding. Leave the census ownership in D13. Treat the operator-grant template as a spec item that depends on the answer to the v1-10 frontier question.

#### v1-14: Cutover year forces manual statement and receipt work

- **What could go wrong:** After a mid-year switch, SiteStacker issued household statements and Asym issues legal-donor statements. P19 cumulative coverage excludes items without compatible source-eligible posted cash. Decommissioning before year-end leaves no issuer for the pre-switch portion.
- **Why it matters:** Manual finance reconciliation, two statements with different totals for donors, and possible annual-receipting compliance gaps (jurisdiction unverified).
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** phase-19 :944, :976-982 (repo-published, verified); r4 G8; r1 edge case; r6 M4 (snippet-only: churches issue two statements).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Warn against boundaries between mid-November and the year-end run. The runbook requires SiteStacker statements for the pre-boundary portion before decommission. P7/P19 rule on the cutover year (F13).
- **Spec language:** Scheduling a switch boundary between 15 November and the ministry's year-end statement run requires an explicit ministry acknowledgement. Before decommissioning SiteStacker, the ministry confirms that statements or receipts for the pre-boundary part of the switch year have been issued from SiteStacker or are covered by a Phase 7/19 rule.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The cutover-year problem is real. P19 :944 cumulative coverage was checked. The fixed '15 November' warning is arbitrary and assumes a calendar year in the US. Fiscal and receipting years vary by jurisdiction and ministry. A more important coupling is missed: tail gifts that SiteStacker receipts would otherwise be swept into Asym cumulative statements (see missed concerns). (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Warn when the boundary falls within a ministry-configured window before its statement or receipting period end, not on a fixed date. Before decommissioning, require that statements for the pre-boundary portion come from SiteStacker. P7 and P19 rule under F13.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Problem validity: the founder asked specifically about ministries that already used Stripe, and the review does not handle that case. If SiteStacker charged through the ministry's own Stripe account, binding Asym to that same account is not 'a different account' under P16 :1314. The path is then proof-gated adoption (#711 'adopt-existing-Stripe', #834 classifier, both OPEN), not donor re-authorization. v1-20's assumption that every gift needs a donor step and v1-11's tail length may both be wrong for that cohort. The reverse hazard: once Asym is bound to the same account, Asym's Stripe event ingestion could record SiteStacker-run charges as Asym gifts, and the catch-up would then duplicate them. Whether SiteStacker uses Stripe Billing subscriptions or its own scheduler on stored payment methods is unverified. Severity high; resolve before recording through the per-ministry census.
- Operational burden: receipts and statements for tail gifts are not tied together. SiteStacker receipts what it charges during the tail (v1-15). Unless each catch-up gift carries source-bound already_receipted evidence (P13 story 170) and a Phase 7 import fact (D4, undefined), P19 cumulative coverage (phase-19 :944 'no existing current official coverage') could include those gifts in an Asym year-end statement and duplicate SiteStacker's receipts. Severity high. Owner: P7, P13 and P19 (F13, F5).
- Problem validity: the review's own §5.1 proposed record adds 'any rollback to SiteStacker after go-live' as not in v1 and invariant (8) 'roll-forward only'. That decision was never put to the founder. It should be asked explicitly, not folded into the amended record. Severity medium.
- Problem validity: no owner text defines a Phase 30 'Switch' (r10-gap-g4: 'no owner text sets the CRM switch model'). Recording A creates new Phase 30 scope (switch record, attestations, boundary) beyond roadmap.md:4363-4403. Under C3 that needs a roadmap and owner-constraints amendment at spec time. v1-12 covers only the processor half. Severity medium.
- UX/user friction: the staff CRM blackout is not addressed. Between 'pause editing in SiteStacker' and go-live, staff cannot record contacts, notes or address changes in either system. v1-16 covers giving only. The rehearsals should measure and time-box the pause, with a holding procedure for interactions during it. Severity medium.
- Operational burden: practice runs that change nothing still put a prospect's real donor PII into Asym before Phase 29 custody exists (PR #2430 unmerged) and possibly before any contract. F2 and F4 raise this, but the review does not connect it to Option A's rehearsal step. Severity medium. Already on the frontier as F2/F4.

### 5.18 Observability and auditability gaps

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v6). **Synthesis verdict:** A is auditable only if it names its durable records: Switch, per-family boundary, attestation items, sign-off versions, the declared executor list, and actor. It must also monitor for the writer it cannot lock, using overlap-and-gap checks per ADR-0106/0058. D9 and O7 wrongly rely on #2295. Run visibility during the pause is an O11 addition.

**What was checked:** I read the decision log (C1-C23, O11, section 8) and the Option A refinements against: ADR-0106:85-104 and ADR-0058:18-28 (cutover with a writer Asym cannot lock); Phase 13 PRD :728-747 and :2190-2200; Phase 16 PRD :1291-1316 and :2581-2593; and workflow-studio 03-runtime-and-recovery.md:26. In code I checked workflows/events.ts:16-22 (system actor), workflows/summaries.ts:6-33, audit_logs (init_schema:325-336) and the crm_record_links grants and policies (20260508000413:200-240; 20261024163000:39-41). I also read the #2295 body (gh, 2026-11-01), re-fetched the SiteStacker Change Log article 151000096704 (2026-11-01), and read r10-gap-g4, r10-gap-g6, q01-r2, q01-r5 section 3 and q01-r6.

#### v6-01: No post-switch overlap-and-gap monitoring; the ministry attestation is the only control

- **What could go wrong:** Asym cannot see SiteStacker: there is no change feed or read API. Any of these can keep writing after go-live with nobody noticing until a donor complains or a catch-up happens to contain the row:
- a forgotten giving form, text-to-give link or QR code;
- PayPal Standard subscriptions, which PayPal runs and which may never appear in a SiteStacker re-export;
- gateway-native schedules (ARB, Braintree or PaymentSpring plans);
- staff still keying checks in SiteStacker;
- missionaries' own Mailchimp lists.
- **Why it matters:** Option A's promise depends on there being no competing writer. The repo's ratified rule for a prior writer Asym cannot lock is 'No known overlap in the inspected scope, followed by post-activation overlap-and-gap monitoring'. Option A has the final inspection but no monitoring.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** Ratified precedent:
- ADR-0106:94-96 (repo-ratified);
- ADR-0058:22-24 (repo-ratified: 'cannot atomically lock an independent connector ... post-activation overlap detection therefore remain active');
- P13 PRD :735-736 'observation is not control' (repo-ratified).

Why SiteStacker writes are invisible to Asym:

- r10-gap-g4.md:78: no change feed (documented vendor absence);
- q01-r2 section 0 and q01-r1 verifier: PayPal subscriptions and gateway-native schedules sit outside SiteStacker exports (inferred).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Make an overlap-and-gap check part of every catch-up cycle until the tail closes. Compare post-boundary rows from processor reports (per merchant account x currency) and from a fresh filtered SiteStacker export against the Switch's declared list of external executors. Record the inspected scope at go-live and use the ratified proof wording.
- **Spec language:** At go-live the Switch records its inspected scope (accepted snapshot manifest, processor reports through a stated instant, attestation set) and states 'No known overlap in the inspected scope'; Asym never states that SiteStacker was frozen or switched off. Until tail closure, every catch-up cycle runs an overlap-and-gap check. Any post-boundary source fact that cannot be attributed to a listed external executor (executor type, gateway, merchant account, source schedule ID) is an unexplained post-boundary fact. It blocks the next catch-up commit and opens an urgent switch exception for the named ministry owner and the Asym switch operator.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Checked. ADR-0106:94-96 says that where a prior writer cannot be locked, the truthful proof is 'No known overlap in the inspected scope' followed by post-activation overlap-and-gap monitoring. ADR-0058:22-24 says Asym 'cannot atomically lock an independent connector' and that post-activation overlap detection stays active. P13 PRD:735-736 says 'observation is not control'. All three are repo-ratified. Founder Option A has an attestation and nothing after it. The fix follows ratified precedent and is proportionate. Comparing against processor reports depends on the ministry supplying them, so the inspected scope must be recorded and must not be assumed. (corrected severity: high)

#### v6-02: Refinement 4 leaves out the records an audit needs: Switch, per-family boundary, attestations, sign-off versions, executor lineage

- **What could go wrong:** Refinement 4 lists only run identity, source-snapshot identity and per-record lineage. After a disputed switch, nobody could reproduce:
- the exact boundary instant and time zone;
- who declared the freeze;
- what each attestation item said;
- which reconciliation report version finance signed;
- which executor, gateway and merchant account charged a late-tail gift.

Without these, classifying a gift as history or late-tail cannot be reproduced, and Phase 16 stop proof and Phase 7 receipt decisions cannot be audited.

- **Why it matters:** The roadmap already requires batch identity, occurrence and recorded times, historical/cutover disposition, coverage and per-record lineage. No owner text defines a switch-level record, and run evidence is invisible today (O11).
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** - roadmap.md:4363 (repo-published);
- decision-log.md:97 (O11);
- ADR-0106:85-104, the activation-manifest precedent (repo-ratified);
- q01-r5 section 3.1 and K6 (proposed);
- q01-r6 owner gaps;
- q01-r2: lineage should carry gateway, merchant account and executor type (inferred).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
