- **Best fix:** Specify one durable Switch record and its children. Store a boundary per source family (all equal in v1) and test that classification reads each family's own boundary, so Option B readiness is proven by test, not by schema presence.
- **Spec language:** Each ministry switch has one durable Switch record (Tenant; source system and instance; state; named roles). It owns:
- a per-source-family boundary (UTC instant, declared IANA zone and civil time, half-open; equal for all families in v1);
- snapshot manifests (per-file digest, filter, exporter role, source version, export time, UI control counts);
- runs (plan digest, approver, committer, terminal outcome);
- attestation items (item key, scope, state, actor, time, evidence reference or 'none supplied');
- reconciliation report versions and their sign-offs;
- catch-up runs with their boundary classification;
- tail closure.

Per-record lineage resolves to switch, manifest, file, source ID or row, run and plan. For money it also resolves to executor type, gateway and merchant account. Acceptance: a classifier test with two families at different boundaries passes.

- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The gap is real. Refinement 4 names run, snapshot and lineage identity, but there is no Switch record and nowhere to store attestations or sign-offs. ADR-0106:85-89 does require an exact boundary for every predecessor source family, so storing a boundary per family follows precedent. Requiring a test that classification reads each family's own boundary goes further than v1 needs, since all boundaries are equal in v1. That test can wait until Option B is actually scoped. The executor-lineage part is inferred (q01-r2) and is not a requirement. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Specify one durable Switch record. Give it a boundary per source family (equal in v1), attestation items, sign-off versions, and the list of external executors it declared. Defer the classification test that proves Option B until Option B is scoped.

#### v6-03: Background execution loses the committing human, and no capability exists for switch acts

- **What could go wrong:** Final runs and catch-ups will run as background jobs. Today:
- workflow steps act as the nil-UUID WORKFLOW_SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID;
- audit_logs.user_id has no foreign key;
- the event envelope has no actor slot;
- the principal model has no consumer.

So every import write would be attributed to 'the workflow executor', and C16's re-proof of authority when deferred jobs fire cannot happen. No Phase 12 capability covers declaring the boundary, committing, signing off or going live.

- **Why it matters:** Staff cannot safely correct a switch without knowing who approved and committed each plan. A committer who loses access mid-freeze would keep writing under system authority.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** - packages/api/src/workflows/events.ts:16-22 (repo-integrated);
- r06 6.3/V4, 6.9 and 7.6 (verified);
- decision-log.md:46 (C16, repo-ratified) and :150 (no capability);
- q01-r6 owner gaps.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Carry the approved plan digest and the committing principal through background execution, and re-prove authority per chunk. Keep the system actor for mechanical steps only.
- **Spec language:** These switch acts record the human principal, the Phase 12 capability exercised and the time: boundary declaration, each attestation item, final-run commit, catch-up commit, reconciliation sign-off, go-live, abort and tail closure. Background steps executing an approved plan record the plan digest and committing principal and re-prove that principal's current authority before each chunk (C16). A run whose committer loses authority pauses as authority_lost with a visible next action; it never continues as the workflow system actor.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Verified that packages/api/src/workflows/events.ts:17-22 records WORKFLOW_SYSTEM_ACTOR_ID as the nil UUID. The decision log already records both halves of this concern: section 8 says 'background jobs cannot yet carry a staff actor's authority', and C16 says deferred jobs re-prove the owner's authority when they fire. The missing capability is recorded at :150. The concern restates known constraints and adds nothing that changes the Q1 answer. Re-proving authority per chunk is consistent with C16. (corrected severity: medium)

#### v6-04: The lineage and binding carrier (crm_record_links) is writable by any staff member, and the decision log pins the revoke on #2295, which does not do it

- **What could go wrong:** Any tenant staff member can INSERT and UPDATE crm_record_links directly. Decision-log D9 and O7 say #2295 will revoke that access. The #2295 body only rewrites the authenticated policies to a pure-tenant predicate and removes is_super_admin(); INSERT and UPDATE stay granted. Bindings and lineage, which are the convergence key for repeat import and catch-up, could be rewritten without audit. A rewritten binding causes a duplicate on the next catch-up, or money attached to the wrong Party.
- **Why it matters:** Refinement 4's per-record lineage is only trustworthy if the carrier is append-only and command-written. The safeguard the decision log relies on does not exist.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** - 20260508000413_*.sql:204 and :220-240, re-scoped in 20261024163000_authz_definer_execute_lockdown.sql:39-41 (repo-integrated);
- gh issue view 2295, body and done criteria, read 2026-11-01 (repo-published);
- decision-log.md:69 (D9) and :93 (O7).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Correct D9 and O7 now and name the real owner and ticket for the revoke. Write bindings only through a typed, audited, append-only command, with a real-database test that direct DML is denied.
- **Spec language:** Source-record bindings and per-record lineage are written only through the owner's typed command. Direct authenticated INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE on the binding carrier is revoked, and a real-database test proves it. Every binding change appends a history row with actor and reason. Decision-log D9 and O7 are corrected: #2295 rewrites policy predicates and does not revoke direct DML; owner [TBD] and ticket [TBD] own the revoke.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified. In 20260508000413_crm_identity_mapping.sql, :204 grants SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE to authenticated, and :220-245 limit INSERT/UPDATE only by has_staff_membership. 20261024163000:39-41 only re-scopes these policies TO authenticated. The body of gh #2295 (read 2026-11-01) rewrites them to a tenant-only predicate and removes is_super_admin. It does not revoke staff DML. If anything, the pure-tenant form may stop checking staff role. So D9 and O7 (decision-log.md:69 and :93) misstate the remedy. It is a factual error in the working record, and nothing imports through this table today, so medium rather than high. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Correct D9 and O7 to say that #2295 rewrites the predicate and does not revoke DML. Record the revoke as an unticketed owner requirement for Phase 12/4/9. Keep the 'typed audited command plus a test that direct DML is denied' requirement for the spec.

#### v6-05: Option A packs the O11 run-visibility gap into the freeze window

- **What could go wrong:** Every final run happens inside a short freeze window (proposed under 48 hours). A stalled or dead-lettered run surfaces only as a dispatch row on the unlinked /workflows page, plus a tenant-policy notification with no import meaning. Nobody learns which run of which switch stalled, and every hour extends a freeze in which giving forms are off and staff cannot edit.
- **Why it matters:** It turns a known gap into ministry-visible downtime and pressure to go live with unverified runs.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** - packages/api/src/workflows/summaries.ts:6-13 (repo-integrated);
- r10-gap-g6.md:109, :166 and :196-197;
- decision-log O11;
- q01-r5 S3 (MS Learn <48h, verified-vendor-doc via r5).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Show run-level state on the Switch page during the frozen-to-live window, actively notify named roles, and block go-live until every in-scope run is terminal and verified.
- **Spec language:** Between boundary declaration and go-live, every in-scope run shows one run-level state on the Switch page (queued, running, paused_for_review, authority_lost, failed_needs_action, completed_verified). The named Asym switch operator and ministry owner are notified within [N] minutes of non-progress or a terminal failure. Go-live is blocked while any in-scope run is non-terminal, unverified, or failed without an accepted visible exclusion.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** duplicate: O11 (decision-log.md:97) already records it. summaries.ts:6-13 confirms the state list. The concern says itself that its effect is no_change. 'Actively notify named roles' adds delivery scope that O11 leaves to the spec. Blocking go-live until every in-scope run is terminal is a reasonable line to add under O11. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Add to O11: go-live is blocked until every in-scope run is terminal and verified.

#### v6-06: The attestation is unevidenced self-declaration with no scope

- **What could go wrong:** 'The ministry confirms' leaves nothing to re-check in a dispute, or when a catch-up shows a gift from a form attested as off. Without scope, 'Mailchimp off' misses lists already exported to missionary-owned accounts, and 'forms off' misses a second Site.
- **Why it matters:** An attestation that cannot be audited is not evidence. Some SiteStacker evidence sources do exist and are cheap to capture.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** - SiteStacker Change Log records 'users who made changes, where the change was made, and a date/time stamp' for 'many of the changes'; no export, filter or retention is documented (https://training.sitestacker.com/support/solutions/articles/151000096704, modified 25 Sep 2023, accessed 2026-11-01, verified-vendor-doc);
- gateways are per Site (151000118930 via q01-r2);
- missionary Mailchimp is a manual export (151000122392 via q01-r1 verifier);
- q01-r6 edge case.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Record each attestation item per scope, with an optional evidence artifact in Phase 29 custody and an explicit 'none supplied' when there is none. Capture a Change Log view covering boundary to final export where available. Display the claim only as 'ministry attested'.
- **Spec language:** Each attestation item is recorded per scope (Site, Site Channel, connected missionary account where applicable) with state confirmed_off | kept_on_with_reason | not_used, actor, time, and either a Phase 29 evidence reference (screenshot, configuration export or Change Log view) or 'no evidence supplied'. Asym displays 'ministry attested' and never 'off'.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Self-declaration without scope is a fair concern, but I did not re-fetch the SiteStacker Change Log article, so it is unverified by me. The cited text itself says it covers only 'many of the changes' and documents no export, so making a Change Log capture part of the requirement is not justified. Asking for an optional evidence artifact and an explicit 'none supplied' is proportionate. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Record each attestation item per Site, gateway and list, with an optional evidence artifact in Phase 29 custody, or an explicit 'none supplied'. Display it as 'ministry attested'. Drop the Change Log capture requirement.

#### v6-07: Monitor tail and external-executor drift (charges after stop proof, or from unlisted executors)

- **What could go wrong:** Legacy executors keep charging during the recurring tail. A charge after a recorded Phase 16 stop, or from an executor nobody listed, means a duplicate charge or money with no owner.
- **Why it matters:** Phase 16 forbids two systems charging at once and treats post-boundary provider attempts as urgent reconciliation or refund exceptions.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** - P16 PRD :1314 and :1295 (repo-ratified);
- P16 :1293 cancellation-pending wording;
- q01-r2 section 0;
- q01-r4 section 11.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Run a per-cycle drift monitor recorded on the Switch, with a planned tail-closure date.
- **Spec language:** The Switch records a planned tail-closure date and runs the tail drift monitor every catch-up cycle. Closure requires zero unexplained post-boundary facts and stop proof or a recorded end for every listed external executor.
- **Bucket:** monitor (signal: Per catch-up cycle: the count and amount of post-boundary charges per external executor x merchant account x currency, from processor reports and SiteStacker exports; and the age of each 'Cancellation requested - provider confirmation pending' item.; threshold: Urgent: any charge on a schedule after its recorded stop proof, or any charge attributable to no listed executor. Escalate: a pending cancellation older than one billing cycle, or the tail reaching its planned closure date with charges still arriving.; owner: Asym switch operator (signal); Phase 16 owner (stop proof and schedule state); ministry finance (refunds in the old processor).; response: Hold activation of the related Asym schedule, open a refund exception, notify the donor through the Phase 16/17 path, and record the outcome on the Switch. Escalate to the ministry and founder if closure slips.)
- **Skeptic ruling:** duplicate: Duplicate of v6-01. It describes the same per-cycle overlap-and-gap or drift monitor against the declared external executors, with the same evidence (P16:1314, :1295). Folding the tail-closure date into v6-01 is enough. (corrected severity: low)

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Footguns: the 'gifts-only' catch-up leaves out reversals of pre-boundary gifts that happen after the switch (refunds, chargebacks, disputes, ACH returns). P13 PRD:1767 makes contribution_postings append-only, and refund, ach_return and chargeback are posting kinds. P16 PRD:1295 says in-flight attempts can resolve after the boundary (repo-ratified). If only new gifts arrive, imported totals drift and reconciliation passes falsely. The catch-up needs a reviewed reversal lane routed to Phase 13 corrections. Severity high (inferred from the cited text).
- Footguns / other hazards: if the ministry already used Stripe and connects the same account to Asym, Asym's webhook will receive events for SiteStacker-originated charges. packages/api/src/stripe/webhooks.ts:341-410 handles payment_intent, charge.refunded, subscription and invoice events purely by provider ID (repo-integrated). The result is two channels for one charge, alongside the catch-up export. Unmatched events disappear as 'not found' with no gap signal, and matched IDs mutate history. Whether connected-account events reach this endpoint for SiteStacker traffic depends on the Connect topology. That is inferred and I did not verify it against Stripe docs. Severity high.
- Footguns: a switch in the middle of a tax year. Phase 7 receipts and Phase 19 year-end statements must cover gifts SiteStacker already receipted in the same year without issuing new receipts and without leaving statements partial. P13:1031-1035 sets the receipt boundary, D4 leaves the Phase 7 import fact undefined, and C22 requires partial history to be shown as partial. The review does not raise the year-end statement for the switch year. Severity medium-high.
- Testability: v6-18 says 'no default consumed', but C15 (Phase 11, ratified) says 'defaults apply only at create'. The spec must reconcile the two explicitly: catalog defaults must not stand in for imported source facts. Otherwise one ratified rule appears to contradict the other. Severity low.
- Other development hazards: during the declared freeze, SiteStacker giving forms are off but Asym giving is not yet live. Nobody owns donor-facing continuity: where public giving URLs point and how mail-in checks received during the freeze are recorded (O10, Phase 24 giving addresses, Phase 15 offline batches). Gifts can be lost or keyed twice. Severity medium (inferred).

### 5.19 Dependency and integration risks

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v5). **Synthesis verdict:** A needs several unbuilt owner commands at once (the eligibility kit rule). It depends on an uncontracted vendor whose export shape can drift (pinned headers, licence continuity attestation). Linking tail money depends on unverified processor IDs in exports (quarantine volume measured at readiness). Senders outside Asym fall outside the zero-effect proof (attested-only scope). The Stripe prototype must not serve a migrated tenant.

**What was checked:** I checked whether the owners Option A depends on are ready: roadmap.md:4320-4420, owner-constraints.md:329-455 and the open issues #570, #645, #512, #513, #711, #834, #809, #692, #2347 and #2087 (gh GET, 2026-11-01). I read the Phase 13 PRD (:155-167, :536-546, :655-665, :720-746, :2188-2200) and the Phase 16 PRD (:1236, :1279, :1314). In current Stripe code I read tenant-client.ts:44-66, billing.ts:39-40, event-store.ts:154 and recurring.ts:95. I also used the SiteStacker and vendor evidence in r1, r2, r7, g3 and the verifier corrections, the Phase 29 spec AC29-D22-01 and AC29-D23-01 on PR #2430 head 1483a349, and the Stripe rate-limits doc (verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01).

#### v5-D1: One switch requires every in-kit owner command at once; missing owners silently turn Option A into area-by-area

- **What could go wrong:** Each family can be imported only through its owner's typed import command, and none exists yet. Either no ministry can switch until the slowest owner ships, or ministries switch with history families left out and import them later. Importing them later is a per-family sequence, which is Option B (rejected) arrived at through owner readiness.
- **Why it matters:** It decides whether the first switch can happen in the Phase 30 timeline, and whether 'Asym becomes the only place for supporters and their history' is true at the moment it is promised.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** owner-constraints.md Phase 30 'Owner-domain import adapters ... activate only after the target phase and its typed import command exist' (repo-published). roadmap.md:4387 'Begin with a complete low-risk source-to-Party import ... then add ... money/document/content targets' (repo-published). Open on 2026-11-01: #570 Party spine, #645, #512, #513, #711 (routes broader historical import to Phase 36), #834, #809. The Phase 7 import fact is undefined (D4). Phase 29 AC29-D23-01 says 'Unknown limits block that profile' (PR #2430 head, unmerged). Nothing is implemented (decision-log.md:150).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Define a Minimum Switch Kit that must be owner-ready and committed before go-live. Record every other family as Deferred History: keep its final-snapshot bytes in Phase 29 custody, import it later only as a manifest extension bound to the same Switch Boundary, and declare its coverage incomplete until then. The founder decides whether historic gifts are in the kit.
- **Spec language:** Q1 (amended): A ministry switch goes live only when every Minimum Switch Kit family is owner-ready, committed and reconciled. The v1 Minimum Switch Kit is [founder to list; proposed: Parties and households, contact points, consent and suppression evidence]. A family outside the kit or not owner-ready at the switch is recorded on the Switch as Deferred History. Its final-snapshot artifacts are retained under Phase 29 custody, and it may be imported later only as a manifest extension bound to the same Switch Boundary. Until that import is reconciled, the family's history coverage is declared incomplete. 'Asym becomes the only place for supporters and their history' applies to committed families; for Deferred History it means 'no other system is authoritative', not 'history is present'.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Evidence checks out: owner-constraints.md:389-393 says owner-domain import adapters 'activate only after the target phase and its typed import command exist' (repo-published). roadmap.md:4387 sets the Party-first order. decision-log.md:150 confirms nothing is implemented. The word 'silently' overstates it. The gating is explicit, and roadmap.md:4365-4367 already says 'Target readiness, not a generic upload success, determines what can be committed'. The real gap is narrower: the Q1 promise that Asym becomes 'the only place for supporters and their history' outruns what can be committed at go-live. The Minimum Switch Kit idea is proportionate. Binding Deferred History to Phase 29 custody depends on Phase 29, which is spec-only (PR #2430 unmerged), and on F11 retention, which is still undecided. Whether historic gifts are in the kit is already frontier F13. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Reword the decision so 'only place' covers just the families declared in the kit at go/no-go. Every other family gets an explicit not-yet-covered history-coverage status (O3, and keep D18's two meanings of 'coverage' apart). Keep its final-snapshot bytes only once Phase 29 custody and F11 retention exist. Leave the historic-gift scope to F13. Do not design the manifest-extension mechanics yet.

#### v5-D6: Option A's freeze and reconciliation are assigned by name to an unowned separate migration workstream

- **What could go wrong:** Phase 13 gives 'cutover freezes, and bulk reconciliation', 'connected-account readiness' and 'recurring-executor replacement at scale' to a 'separate migration workstream' that no phase owns. Recording Option A's freeze and reconciliation as a Phase 30 decision takes over a producer's duty by shorthand, which C3 forbids. Without an owner, the processor half (vault transfer, re-authorization, executor replacement) never starts and the tail never closes.
- **Why it matters:** This is the governance dependency that decides whether the tail can close, and whether the Q1 record conflicts with ratified Phase 13 text.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** phase-13 PRD :743-746 and :2192-2199 (repo-ratified). phase-13 :165 assigns CRM/historical-giving ETL to 'the migration workstream'; that part is effectively Phase 30's per roadmap.md:4341 and :4363 (inferred, per the r4 verifier). C3 is in docs/ai/document-authority.md:10-13 and :30-38.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Record the split: Phase 30 owns the Switch record, boundary, freeze attestation evidence and reconciliation report; Phase 16 or a named owner owns stop proof, re-authorization and executor replacement; Phase 13 owns connected-account readiness; an ops runbook covers processor coordination. Edit the Phase 13 clauses to name these owners before the first tenant switch.
- **Spec language:** Phase 30 owns the Switch record, Switch Boundary, freeze attestation evidence and the switch reconciliation report as import-run evidence. Processor coordination, credential-vault transfer, re-authorization campaigns, connected-account readiness and recurring-executor replacement at scale remain outside Phase 30. They must be assigned to a named owner by edit of Phase 13 :743-746 and :2192-2199 before the first tenant switch. Go-live does not depend on them; tail closure does.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Verified. Phase 13 :743-746 assigns 'processor coordination, credential-vault transfer, reauthorization campaigns, cutover freezes, and bulk reconciliation' to the separate migration workstream. :2192-2199 also reserves 'connected-account readiness' and 'recurring-executor replacement at scale' there, with Phase 16 supplying contracts. The C3 point is valid: Phase 30 cannot take on the freeze and reconciliation by shorthand. But the fix's split gives connected-account readiness to Phase 13, and :2195 places it in the workstream, not Phase 13. Discovery cannot pre-decide that. Phase 30 can only propose it as an owner requirement, and the owner edit happens at spec publication. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Add an O-item: Phase 30 proposes to own the Switch record, boundary, freeze-attestation evidence and reconciliation report as run evidence. Stop proof, re-authorization, vault transfer, connected-account readiness and executor replacement stay with the Phase 13/16 workstream until those owners name an owner by editing Phase 13 :743-746 and :2192-2199. Make that edit a precondition for tail closure, not for recording Q1.

#### v5-D3: Linking tail money to imported gifts depends on an unverified export column (gateway transaction ID)

- **What could go wrong:** Dashboard refunds, chargebacks, ACH returns and PayPal tail gifts exist only at the processor. If SiteStacker exports do not carry the processor charge or transaction ID, Phase 13 forbids linking by email, name or fingerprint. Every such fact then goes to quarantine, and the catch-up produces a growing exception backlog instead of reconciled history.
- **Why it matters:** It decides whether the tail can be reconciled at all, and how much work lands on finance staff for months.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** Phase 13 story 171 (:539-542): quarantine 'rather than linked by email, name, phone, or payment fingerprint' (repo-ratified). r1 R10 lists processor IDs in exports as unverified. r1 D13 (q01-r1:236): 'Linkage to the original is undocumented'; chargebacks and ACH returns are processor-only. Phase 29 AC29-D22-01: 'an imported CSV cannot certify financial coverage' (PR head, unmerged).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Make R10 a readiness gate, measured on the first real export. If the ID is present, link by exact scoped external reference. If it is absent, take processor-sourced tail facts only as unlinked finance exceptions, and show the ministry the projected exception volume before go/no-go.
- **Spec language:** Readiness records, per processor account, whether SiteStacker Transaction View rows carry the processor's charge or transaction identifier. Where they do not, every processor-sourced late-tail fact is imported only as an unlinked finance exception under Phase 13 story 171. The go/no-go report states the projected exception count from the most recent 120 days of processor activity in the final practice run. Phase 30 never links tail money by amount, date, name, email or payment fingerprint.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Verified. q01-r1:411 lists R10 (processor IDs in exports) as unverified. q01-r1:236 says refund linkage is undocumented. Phase 13 story 171 (:539-542) requires quarantine rather than fuzzy linkage. Because that ratified fallback already exists, a missing ID causes exception volume, not wrong money. The only new items are measuring R10 on a real export and projecting exception volume before go/no-go. That warrants medium, not high. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Record R10 as a readiness measurement on the first real export, with a projected count of quarantined tail facts shown at go/no-go. Linking follows Phase 13's exact scoped external reference or quarantine. No new mechanism is needed.

#### v5-D2: SiteStacker/WMTEK is an uncontracted integration whose export shape drifts across versions for the whole tail

- **What could go wrong:** The catch-ups need SiteStacker licensed, readable and stable in shape for months. Its release notes are login-gated, the help centre is inactive, no export documents its columns, WMTEK builds some BI datasets, and copying a vault held on a SiteStacker Connect platform needs the platform owner. A mid-tail upgrade invalidates the catch-up mapping, the contract can end, and WMTEK can refuse.
- **Why it matters:** Asym controls none of these levers, and the vendor has every incentive not to cooperate ('Migration friction is SiteStacker's moat', owner-constraints.md kit bullet).
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** decision-log.md:139: help centre returns 402; releases reach at least 2.56 (snippet-only). decision-log.md:160: no read API, relational export or exit package. decision-log.md:140: no change feed. Stripe Connect copy needs the platform owner, and partial copy is unavailable for Connect accounts (r4/r5 verifier, verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01). r1 R19/X13 and r5 E-24 track versions only between practice and final runs.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Make SiteStacker continuity until the recorded tail closure date a readiness attestation. Treat every catch-up as a new manifest checked against the version-pinned header set. Use processor reports as the primary source for tail money. Declare a fallback for every family that depends on WMTEK.
- **Spec language:** Readiness requires the ministry to attest that its SiteStacker licence, an unrestricted export user and read access remain in place until the Switch's recorded tail closure date. Every catch-up is a new source manifest. A header set or SiteStacker version that differs from the pinned catch-up profile rejects the manifest with a cause-owned 're-map required' action, and no catch-up row is parsed under a stale mapping. Each family whose only source is a WMTEK-built dataset is marked 'vendor-dependent' at readiness with a declared fallback disposition.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The cited facts hold: help centre returns 402, release notes are login-gated, there is no change feed and no exit package (decision-log.md:139-140, :160; section 9). They are snippet-only for version drift. The parts about tail continuity, licence and closure date largely duplicate v5-M5 (tail closure) and v5-S4 (catch-up cost). The Stripe Connect copy point is tangential to SiteStacker drift. 'Use processor reports as the primary source for tail money' is a reasonable direction, but processor report formats are themselves unverified (q01-r2:254 'general knowledge; unverified'). (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Merge the SiteStacker licence and export-access continuity attestation into v5-M5's tail closure record. Pin each catch-up manifest to a recorded header set and treat drift as a blocking plan conflict. Name processor reports as the candidate tail source per processor, subject to a real sample.

#### v5-D4: Outward effects from processors or SiteStacker are outside Asym's control and outside C23's measurable scope

- **What could go wrong:** Several senders sit outside Asym's suppression: Stripe ACH mandate emails, Stripe refund emails, gateway receipts (PaymentSpring send_receipt), PayPal notices, SiteStacker receipt and declined messages, and missionary-owned Mailchimp lists (a one-way manual export, so 'sync off' does not stop them). The claim 'zero unintended send' therefore cannot be tested as written.
- **Why it matters:** C23 is the release gate, and a gate that cannot be tested gets signed off on assumption.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** roadmap.md:4399 and :4403 (repo-published). Stripe payment-method-imports ACH emails and the refunds email setting (r5 verifier, verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01). PaymentSpring send_receipt (r2, verified-vendor-doc). SiteStacker article 151000122392 on the missionary Mailchimp export (r1 verifier, verified-vendor-doc).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Split acceptance in two. Asym-originated effects are measured from run evidence. External effect sources are listed in a census and each is attested as off, intentionally on (with a named owner) or not used.
- **Spec language:** Switch acceptance proves zero unintended Asym-originated send, enrollment, payment or public effect from run evidence. External effect sources (SiteStacker messages and automations, processor or gateway emails, PayPal notices, Stripe import emails, missionary-owned Mailchimp accounts, text-to-give replies) are listed in the outward-effect census. Each is recorded as 'confirmed off', 'intentionally on' (with a named owner and reason) or 'not used', with actor and time. The Switch never claims these are silent.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Valid: roadmap.md:4399 'zero unintended send, enrollment, payment or public effect' can only be measured for Asym-originated effects. Processor-originated emails (Stripe payment-method import notices, PaymentSpring send_receipt) are outside run evidence. But founder refinement (3) already has the same attestation shape for Spark, giving forms and Mailchimp. A separate census mechanism is more than needed. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Extend refinement (3)'s off-attestation list to processor-side donor notifications (receipt, refund and payment-method-import emails) for each processor in use, each marked off, intentionally on with a named owner, or not used. State that C23-style zero-effect acceptance covers Asym-originated effects only.

#### v5-D5: The current Stripe prototype would turn a migrated Stripe ministry's legacy account into live control

- **What could go wrong:** Current code reads a plaintext tenants.stripe_secret_key with a platform-key fallback, opens the whole-account Billing Portal for donors.stripe_customer_id, and routes by metadata. For a ministry already on Stripe, the easiest path is to paste its existing key, which is the same-account reuse the ratified design forbids. Stripe self-serve copy keeps cus_ IDs identical across accounts, so an imported legacy ID could resolve on two accounts, or let a donor change the payment method SiteStacker's cron charges.
- **Why it matters:** Legacy executors would gain live control, contrary to Phase 16 external read-only and formal-cutover rules.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** tenant-client.ts:44, :65-66; billing.ts:39-40; event-store.ts:154; recurring.ts:95 (repo-integrated). Phase 13 :659-665 requires Account Links, 'Do not use OAuth' and 'never a stored tenant key' (repo-ratified). P16 :1314 (repo-ratified). #692 is open. Stripe copy keeps the same customer ID (r4 verifier, verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Block go-live while the plaintext-key or fallback path can serve the tenant. Keep source provider IDs as inert scoped lineage only.
- **Spec language:** Go/no-go is blocked while the tenant can resolve a Stripe client from tenants.stripe_secret_key or the platform-key fallback. Provider identifiers from source exports are stored only as scoped, inert source lineage (tenant, provider, account, mode, object). No import writes them to any column read by checkout, the donor portal, refunds or webhook routing. Acceptance includes a fixture where the same cus_ ID exists on two accounts and no live path resolves it.
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified in code: packages/api/src/stripe/tenant-client.ts:44 reads plaintext tenants.stripe_secret_key, and :65-66 fall back to serverEnv.STRIPE_SECRET_KEY (repo-integrated). Phase 13 :659-665 ratifies Account Links and 'never a stored tenant key'. P16 :1314 verified. The platform-key fallback makes this worse than the review says: an unconfigured migrated tenant would resolve to the platform account. Likelihood is tempered because money targets cannot activate before Phase 13 owner commands exist (owner-constraints.md:389-391), and that work should retire this path. So this is a go-live gate, not a design change. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Accept as written: block go/no-go while the plaintext-key or platform-fallback path can serve the tenant, and keep source provider IDs as inert scoped lineage. The two-account cus_ fixture is reasonable as a Phase 13/16 acceptance test, not a Phase 30 one.

#### v5-D7: Reconciling through the Stripe API would hit read allocations sized for a young platform

- **What could go wrong:** If reconciliation or old-stop observation lists history through the API, two limits apply. The platform's read allocation on behalf of connected accounts is 500 reads per transaction, summed across connected accounts (minimum 10,000 a month). Live mode also rate-limits at 100 requests per second globally and 25 per endpoint, and migration-style bursts trigger 429s.
- **Why it matters:** Reconciliation would stall during switch or tail work. Limit increases need 6 weeks' notice.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** low / low
- **Evidence:** https://docs.stripe.com/rate-limits (verified-vendor-doc, accessed 2026-11-01): reporting products are excluded from allocations; 'Running a large volume of closely-spaced requests ... Often this is part of an analytical or migration operation'.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Take reconciliation evidence from processor report and export artifacts held in Phase 29 custody, not from paginated API listing.
- **Spec language:** Switch and catch-up reconciliation consume processor report/export artifacts held in Phase 29 custody; bulk API listing of historical objects is not a reconciliation source.
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Low and proportionate. The fix (reconcile from report and export artifacts rather than paginated API listing) costs nothing and fits Phase 30 owning reconciliation evidence. The Stripe quote was not re-fetched in this audit, so it rests on the reviewer's verified-vendor-doc label. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Keep as an implementation note only.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Migration/rollout: The 'freeze' cannot cover money. q01-r2:14 (verified-vendor-doc: SiteStacker CRON 151000097037 and article 151000115212) shows SiteStacker's own cron charges card and ACH recurring gifts from tokens in the ministry's processor vault. Refinement (2) therefore needs SiteStacker's recurring engine left running after the boundary while forms and Spark are off. If a ministry reads 'Asym becomes the only place' or refinement (3) as 'switch SiteStacker off', or lets the licence lapse, recurring revenue stops at once. The Q1 record should say which SiteStacker functions stay on until each Phase 16 cohort cutover. Severity high.
- Migration/rollout: Double charges at handover. If vault tokens are copied or adopted for Asym or Phase 16 collection while SiteStacker's cron still holds the same tokens, both executors can charge the same donor (P16 :1314 forbids active-active, but stop proof for SiteStacker's cron schedules is unverified). Nothing shows SiteStacker can bulk-disable or evidence-stop individual recurring schedules. That capability is a Phase 16 readiness unknown the review did not raise. Severity high.
- Dependency: Refinement (2)'s 'gifts-only' catch-up drops schedule state changes made in SiteStacker during the tail: donor cancellations, amount or date edits, and Move Payment Method (q01-r1 X2). Phase 16 adoption (#834) would then classify stale schedules, and a donor cancellation recorded only in SiteStacker could be lost (P16 :1293 treats cancellation as top priority). v5-S4 mentions 'full Recurring views' only as a diff input. The record should say that schedule-state deltas route to Phase 16 as evidence, while supporter details are still never overwritten. Severity high.
- Dependency/scalability: One ministry may run several processors and merchant accounts split by Site, currency and payment method (q01-r2:18, verified-vendor-doc 151000118930 plus vendor-served code). The review's tail, reconciliation and stop-proof concerns assume one processor. Reconciliation and tail closure need a per-processor, per-merchant dimension, and Phase 16 cutover multiplies accordingly. Severity medium.
- Dependency: Deferred History, final-snapshot custody and catch-up manifests (v5-D1, v5-D2, v5-S4) all assume Phase 29 private custody and F11 retention. Phase 29 is an unmerged spec (PR #2430), nothing is implemented (decision-log.md:150), and F2 asks whether Asym may hold a prospect's export before custody exists. The review should have made Phase 29 implementation readiness an explicit go/no-go dependency. Severity medium.
- Migration/rollout: Switch-date timing against the tax year. A mid-year boundary splits a donor's year between imported history and Asym gifts for Phase 19 year-end statements and Phase 7 receipts, and the Phase 7 import fact is undefined (D4). F13 lists cutover-year statements, but the review did not tie this to scheduling the switch, for example avoiding Q4 or year-end boundaries until Phase 7 and Phase 19 define combined-year behaviour. Severity medium.

### 5.20 Migration, rollout, and upgrade risks

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v5). **Synthesis verdict:** The switch-day activation order is undefined (an outage versus a concurrent writer, and suppression before sending): settle it before recording. Irreversibility after go-live needs an explicit abort window and a halt. Refinement 1 needs convergence. The tail needs a cap. The first switch's end of the fresh-build window is folded into the D13 census gate. Refinement 4 is reworded to 'does not preclude B'.

**What was checked:** I checked the r5 lifecycle (§3.1-§3.14), the roadmap and owner-constraints clauses on convergence, undo and backfill (roadmap.md:4349, :4363, :4395, :4403; owner-constraints 're-import updates instead of duplicating'), and P16 formal cutover (:1279, :1314). I traced the live donor-creation path (begin_donation_saga in supabase/migrations/20261024141000:13-17, :105-117). For first-tenant posture I read roadmap.md:432-434, ADR-0179 and docs/ops/environments.md:5 (D13). I also checked the PayPal srt cap and cron tail evidence from the r1/r2 verifiers, and refinement (4) against the per-family boundary pattern (ADR-0106 via r5).

#### v5-M2: Switch-day activation order is undefined: giving outage vs concurrent live writer vs suppression before sending

- **What could go wrong:** Read literally, the Q1 sequence (freeze, export, import, reconcile, then Asym) means online giving is down for the whole import, acceptance and sign-off window. If Asym giving opens earlier, live gifts create donor rows keyed by (tenant_id, profile_id) with no email, and the import cannot match them, so duplicates follow that only Phase 4 can merge. Refinement (3) also gates Asym sending only on SiteStacker being quiet, not on Asym having committed the final consent and suppression floors.
- **Why it matters:** Lost gifts during an outage, duplicate supporters from the first day, and sends to people who opted out.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** begin_donation_saga donor insert ON CONFLICT (tenant_id, profile_id); 'The saga still writes no donor email' (20261024141000:13-17, :105-117; repo-integrated, legacy donor model; Party model #570 unbuilt). roadmap.md:4349 'a changed target is a conflict'. C10. MS Learn 'During cutover, you can't use your old system or your new one' (via r10-gap-g4:194, verified-vendor-doc).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Fix the activation order: boundary; final snapshot; commit and reconcile Parties, contacts and suppression; a human publishes Asym giving; remaining families commit dark; owners activate sending only after coverage is declared. The founder chooses between a full import before go-live (longer outage) and a Party-first go-live (outage covers steps 2-3 only).
- **Spec language:** Switch activation order: (1) record the boundary; (2) accept the final snapshot; (3) commit and reconcile Parties, households, contact points and consent/suppression evidence including the final Mailchimp export; (4) only then may a human publish Asym giving and confirm SiteStacker giving entry points off or redirected; (5) remaining in-kit families commit outward-effect-dark; (6) no Asym automation, newsletter or first-support activation runs for the tenant until step (3) is reconciled and the owning phase has accepted its coverage declaration. The go/no-go report states the expected online-giving outage (step 2 start to step 4), computed from the measured profile.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified: 20261024141000:13-17 and :105-117 show begin_donation_saga inserting donors ON CONFLICT (tenant_id, profile_id) with no email, so any live Asym giving during import creates profile-keyed rows the import must converge with. roadmap.md:4349 treats a changed target as a conflict. The saga is the legacy donor model and Party (#570) is unbuilt, so that specific mechanism may change, but the general concurrent-writer and outage trade-off holds. The founder choice between a full import before go-live and Party-first go-live is genuine and sits alongside v5-D1. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Accept. Record the activation order and the founder choice (full import before go-live vs Party-first). Keep suppression and consent evidence committed before any Asym sending, which is already implied by C23 and F12.

#### v5-M1: Option A is irreversible after go-live, the Q1 text does not say so, and there is no halt control

- **What could go wrong:** A big-bang switch affects every supporter in the ministry. Once Asym-native facts exist, going back to SiteStacker needs an outbound migration that Phase 30 does not provide. Re-enabling SiteStacker forms creates a forbidden active-active writer. No halt or kill control exists, and run failures are invisible after handoff (O11).
- **Why it matters:** The founder needs to know when the decision becomes irreversible. Cutover practice requires a rollback plan and a go/no-go decision.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** r5 §3.14 X3 (proposed). roadmap.md:4395 'Do not promise universal time-window undo, permanent dual writers' and :4403 reversal procedures before tenant use (repo-published). P16 :1314 'no active-active replacement' (repo-ratified). decision-log.md:97 (O11), :150. MS Learn cutover plan includes 'A rollback plan' (via r10-gap-g4:194).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** State that abort is allowed only before go-live: discard plans, or quarantine committed rows and converge on retry. After go-live, only roll-forward is allowed. Specify a per-Switch and per-run Halt. Rehearse reversal procedures in the representative migration.
- **Spec language:** Q1 (amended): The switch is reversible only before go-live. Before go-live the ministry may abort; committed rows are quarantined from ordinary views and coverage, never mass-deleted, and a later attempt converges on the same source bindings. After go-live, correction is roll-forward only, through owner corrections, reviewed supplemental plans or visible exclusion. Re-enabling any SiteStacker giving entry point after go-live requires an explicit recorded founder exception. Every Switch and run has an authorized Halt that stops new chunk dispatch, leaves committed rows intact, and records who halted, when and why.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: roadmap.md:4395 ('Do not promise universal time-window undo, permanent dual writers') and :4403 ('Publish target-specific reversal and reconciliation procedures before tenant use') are verified. Irreversibility after go-live is inherent to Option A ('Asym then becomes the only place'), so stating it is a wording fix, not a defect. The real gaps are an explicit pre-go-live abort window and a run-level halt. A halt is partly covered by roadmap 'Recoverable processing' and by O11. Reversal procedures are already required by :4403. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Add one sentence to the decision: abort is possible before go-live (discard plans; owner-defined reversal of committed rows), and after go-live only roll-forward correction is possible. Add a per-run and per-Switch halt to the O11 run-state requirements.

#### v5-M3: Refinement (1) 'exact repeat = same snapshot replay' is too narrow for Option A's own retry and supplemental paths

- **What could go wrong:** A second attempt after an abort, a forgotten-file extension, Deferred History and every tail catch-up all import a different snapshot of the same source. If v1 guarantees only byte-identical replay, each of these paths duplicates supporters and gifts.
- **Why it matters:** The paths that make Option A safe would be the ones that create duplicates, contrary to the owner text.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** owner-constraints.md 're-import updates instead of duplicating' (repo-published); roadmap.md:4363 'Reimport converges on the same permitted target identity instead of creating another copy' (repo-published). r5 X4 and E-40 (proposed). The r5 and r6 verifiers note that refinement (1) is a new narrowing.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Require identity convergence across any snapshot of the same source instance in v1. Present field differences from later snapshots as source-changed conflicts and never apply them automatically.
- **Spec language:** Refinement (1) (amended): v1 guarantees (a) byte-identical replay of a sealed plan is a no-op, and (b) any later snapshot of the same source instance converges on existing source bindings, never creating a second target identity. Field differences from a later snapshot are presented as source-changed conflicts and are never applied automatically in v1. Missing rows are reported as 'absent from this snapshot', never as deletions.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified: roadmap.md:4363 'Reimport converges on the same permitted target identity instead of creating another copy' and owner-constraints.md:373-374 're-import updates instead of duplicating' (repo-published). Refinement (1) narrows a published requirement. Refinement (2)'s catch-up is itself a later snapshot, so it contradicts the founder's own design. The fix matches roadmap.md:4349 (changed target is a conflict) and leaves write modes to F8. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Accept: require identity convergence across all snapshots of the same source instance, with field differences from later snapshots shown as source-changed conflicts. Write modes stay with F8.

#### v5-M5: The 'bounded' tail can run for years and become the standing dual feed the roadmap forbids

- **What could go wrong:** SiteStacker-built PayPal subscriptions run up to their srt cap of 2-52 cycles (about 4.3 years monthly) unless cancelled. SiteStacker's cron keeps charging until a Phase 16 formal cutover that depends on open #809/#834 and an unowned workstream. Catch-ups would then repeat for years.
- **Why it matters:** It creates a lasting operational burden and contradicts 'Reviewed backfill is a separate bounded operation', the ban on permanent dual writers, and C18.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** Payment.js:1388-1422 srt clamp (verifier; inferred from vendor-served code). P16 :1279 and :1314 (repo-ratified). roadmap.md:4395 (repo-published). owner-constraints.md:424-430 (C18).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Record a tail closure date with a founder-set cap at go/no-go and run catch-ups on a fixed cadence. At closure, each remaining executor is either evidenced stopped or reclassified by Phase 16 as an ongoing external arrangement, with later gifts entering through a Phase 13/15 owner intake.
- **Spec language:** Each Switch records a tail closure date at go/no-go. Late-tail catch-ups run on a fixed cadence until that date and never after it. At closure, every legacy executor still active is either evidenced stopped or reclassified by Phase 16 as an ongoing external arrangement whose subsequent money facts enter only through a Phase 13/15 owner intake, not a Phase 30 catch-up. The tail cap is [founder value, proposed 12 months after go-live].
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified via q01-r2:16, :96 and :171. SiteStacker clamps PayPal srt to 2-52, so monthly PayPal subscriptions run about 4.3 years (inferred from vendor-served code). SiteStacker's own cron keeps card/ACH recurring alive only while it runs (q01-r2 section 0.2). P16 :1314 is verified. The P16 :1279 citation is wrong: that line is ICO guidance, not tail or executor text. owner-constraints.md:424-430 (operational source ingestion is owner-domain work) supports the fix's handoff of later gifts to a Phase 13/15 owner intake. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Accept. Correct the citation to P16 :62, :226 and :1314. A founder-set cap and cadence is reasonable. Make reclassification as an ongoing external arrangement a Phase 16 decision, not a Phase 30 one.

#### v5-M4: The first switch closes the fresh-build window on target schemas that do not exist yet

- **What could go wrong:** The first ministry switch is first-tenant onboarding, and the fresh-build posture closes then. The ADR-0179-style pre-production replacement is no longer available. Every later owner schema change becomes a live backfill over a whole ministry's imported history, written into unbuilt schemas (#570). Production status is also contradictory (D13).
- **Why it matters:** It creates permanent compatibility obligations and requires a census before any switch.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** roadmap.md:432-434 'a one-time window that closes at first tenant onboarding' (repo-published). ADR-0179 :14 'Core is not in production' (repo-ratified). docs/ops/environments.md:5 says production handles real donor data and live Stripe. decision-log.md:73 (D13).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Gate the first-tenant go/no-go on three things: a read-only hosted census that resolves D13, a declaration from each in-kit owner that its schema can be migrated forward over imported rows, and full lineage on every imported row.
- **Spec language:** Before the first tenant switch, a read-only census of the target hosted environment resolves D13. Each Minimum Switch Kit owner records that its schema is forward-migratable over imported rows. Every imported row carries Switch, snapshot, run and source-binding lineage. The go/no-go record notes that this switch ends the fresh-build posture (roadmap.md:434) for those owners.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Mostly a duplicate of decision-log D13, which already requires a read-only hosted census before any tenant migration. roadmap.md:432-434's 'one-time window' concerns Phase 3 classification retrofit of notes, files and pages, not target schemas in general, so the reviewer stretches it. Per-row lineage is already required (roadmap.md:4363). Asking each in-kit owner to declare forward-migratability is overengineered: owners already gate through typed import commands. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Fold into D13: the hosted census is a first-tenant go/no-go gate. Note that a first tenant ends the fresh-build posture for Phase 3 classification. Drop the owner forward-migratability declaration.

#### v5-M6: Refinement (4) overstates what run, snapshot and lineage records buy for a future Option B

- **What could go wrong:** Run and snapshot identity do not make Option B addable. B's hard parts are per-family authority and cross-system references, such as a SiteStacker-authoritative gift for a Party created in Asym. Claiming B is 'designed in' is misleading, and building B machinery now would be speculative.
- **Why it matters:** Without this correction, a later Option B decision could start from false confidence or carry speculative machinery built now.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** low / medium
- **Evidence:** Per-source-family half-open boundaries are an existing ratified pattern for Field Accounts (ADR-0106 via r5 A24/K6). Nothing in refinement (4) addresses reverse identity (inferred).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Store one boundary row per source family, all equal in v1, and build nothing else for B. Require a separate decision for any area-by-area mode.
- **Spec language:** Refinement (4) (amended): the Switch stores one boundary per source family, all equal in v1; no other Option B mechanism is built. Any later area-by-area mode requires its own decision on per-family authority and on identities created in Asym that SiteStacker must reference.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Fair and low. Run, snapshot and lineage identity do not by themselves make an area-by-area mode cheap, and reverse identity is unaddressed (inferred). The per-family boundary idea follows the ADR-0106 pattern cited via r5. It is cheap, but it should not become hidden B scaffolding. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Change refinement (4)'s wording to 'does not preclude B' rather than 'designed so B can be added'. Any area-by-area mode needs a separate decision. A per-family boundary column is optional, not required.

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Migration/rollout: The 'freeze' cannot cover money. q01-r2:14 (verified-vendor-doc: SiteStacker CRON 151000097037 and article 151000115212) shows SiteStacker's own cron charges card and ACH recurring gifts from tokens in the ministry's processor vault. Refinement (2) therefore needs SiteStacker's recurring engine left running after the boundary while forms and Spark are off. If a ministry reads 'Asym becomes the only place' or refinement (3) as 'switch SiteStacker off', or lets the licence lapse, recurring revenue stops at once. The Q1 record should say which SiteStacker functions stay on until each Phase 16 cohort cutover. Severity high.
- Migration/rollout: Double charges at handover. If vault tokens are copied or adopted for Asym or Phase 16 collection while SiteStacker's cron still holds the same tokens, both executors can charge the same donor (P16 :1314 forbids active-active, but stop proof for SiteStacker's cron schedules is unverified). Nothing shows SiteStacker can bulk-disable or evidence-stop individual recurring schedules. That capability is a Phase 16 readiness unknown the review did not raise. Severity high.
- Dependency: Refinement (2)'s 'gifts-only' catch-up drops schedule state changes made in SiteStacker during the tail: donor cancellations, amount or date edits, and Move Payment Method (q01-r1 X2). Phase 16 adoption (#834) would then classify stale schedules, and a donor cancellation recorded only in SiteStacker could be lost (P16 :1293 treats cancellation as top priority). v5-S4 mentions 'full Recurring views' only as a diff input. The record should say that schedule-state deltas route to Phase 16 as evidence, while supporter details are still never overwritten. Severity high.
- Dependency/scalability: One ministry may run several processors and merchant accounts split by Site, currency and payment method (q01-r2:18, verified-vendor-doc 151000118930 plus vendor-served code). The review's tail, reconciliation and stop-proof concerns assume one processor. Reconciliation and tail closure need a per-processor, per-merchant dimension, and Phase 16 cutover multiplies accordingly. Severity medium.
- Dependency: Deferred History, final-snapshot custody and catch-up manifests (v5-D1, v5-D2, v5-S4) all assume Phase 29 private custody and F11 retention. Phase 29 is an unmerged spec (PR #2430), nothing is implemented (decision-log.md:150), and F2 asks whether Asym may hold a prospect's export before custody exists. The review should have made Phase 29 implementation readiness an explicit go/no-go dependency. Severity medium.
- Migration/rollout: Switch-date timing against the tax year. A mid-year boundary splits a donor's year between imported history and Asym gifts for Phase 19 year-end statements and Phase 7 receipts, and the Phase 7 import fact is undefined (D4). F13 lists cutover-year statements, but the review did not tie this to scheduling the switch, for example avoiding Q4 or year-end boundaries until Phase 7 and Phase 19 define combined-year behaviour. Severity medium.

### 5.21 Testability, traceability, and proof

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v6). **Synthesis verdict:** The key verbs ('reconcile', 'only place', 'change nothing', 'exact repeat') are not falsifiable as worded. Restate them as testable statements. Scope the zero-effect proof to every run kind with automations live, plus senders outside Asym that are attested only. Label synthetic fixtures. Use file-qualified research IDs. Phase 16 governs over P13 story 173.

**What was checked:** I compared the founder's wording with Phase 30's published acceptance and retained owner obligations: roadmap.md:4363, :4369, :4387, :4395 and :4401-4403; owner-constraints.md:370-372, :378-388 and :431-437. I also checked the Phase 29 founder brief on PR #2430 head (:151 digest is not identity; :283), workflow-studio runtime :26, CONTEXT.md:5035-5043, and Phase 13 story 173 (:546) against P16 :1314. I grep-counted ID namespace collisions across the six q01 research files, reviewed the q01-r1 R1-R20 export propositions, and confirmed that no real SiteStacker artifact exists (decision-log section 7, g3).

#### v6-08: Reconciliation wording is narrower than published acceptance and would check Asym against itself

- **What could go wrong:** 'Reconcile counts and per-currency totals' leaves out two required partitions:
- Legal Entity: SiteStacker has none, so it must be asserted per merchant account or cohort.
- Source basis: undefined. Without it, the Historic Giving copy of native income can double-count on both sides of the reconciliation and still tie.

Reconciling Asym against the same files it parsed proves only the parser.

- **Why it matters:** Finance would sign off on a false proof that hides the most likely money defect, and the decision drifts from published acceptance.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** - roadmap.md:4369 (repo-published);
- owner-constraints.md:434-437 (repo-published);
- decision-log D16 (source basis undefined);
- q01-r1 K19 (verified-vendor-doc 151000122348);
- q01-r1 R3 and R18;
- q01-r2 (processor reports);
- r10-gap-g5;
- q01-r6 finding 3;
- C19.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Record the reconciliation clause in published-acceptance terms, with independent control totals, and define source basis before any money profile is enabled.
- **Spec language:** Switch reconciliation reports, for each fact family, source / accepted / rejected / quarantined / excluded / unchanged counts. For money it reports totals by Legal Entity x original currency x source basis, never summed across currencies. Each figure is compared with at least one control total not derived from the imported files: SiteStacker screen counts or the Statements report, and processor reports per merchant account x currency for processor-executed money. Every variance is explained by a named category or it blocks sign-off. 'Source basis' is defined in the Phase 30 spec before any money profile is enabled. The Legal Entity for each money cohort comes from a recorded, reviewer-accepted assertion, never from Site.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Verified. roadmap.md:4369 requires counts and totals 'by Legal Entity, original currency and source basis', and owner-constraints.md:434-437 requires Legal-Entity-partitioned per-currency totals. The founder's 'counts and per-currency totals' leaves out Legal Entity and source basis. But the claim that it 'would check Asym against itself' is overstated: reconciling against the source export is implicit (roadmap:4363). This is a wording fix. D16 (source basis undefined) is already open. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Record the clause as: reconcile record counts and native-currency totals by Legal Entity, original currency and source basis against control totals from the source export. A money profile stays disabled until D16 defines source basis.

#### v6-09: 'Asym becomes the only place for supporters and their history' cannot be falsified and is untrue during the recurring tail

- **What could go wrong:** Legacy recurring gifts keep running in SiteStacker, PayPal or gateway schedules as external read-only executors until a Phase 16 formal cutover. Ministries will be told they can switch SiteStacker off at go-live, and no test can pass or fail 'only place'.
- **Why it matters:** A false promise becomes a support incident, and an unfalsifiable claim cannot serve as an acceptance criterion.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** - P16 :2581-2593 and :1314 (repo-ratified);
- P13 :735-736 'An existing provider object may keep operating while it is observed' (repo-ratified);
- q01-r5 K1.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Restate as a testable authority statement with a closure condition.
- **Spec language:** From go-live, Asym is the system of record for in-scope supporter data, giving history and staff work. SiteStacker and the legacy processors may continue only as listed external read-only executors for named legacy recurring schedules, and for refund and dispute handling on legacy charges, until each schedule's Phase 16 formal cutover or recorded end. Acceptance at tail closure: zero unexplained post-boundary source facts, and every listed executor has stop proof or a recorded end.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Valid. P13:735-736 and P16:1314 and :2581-2583 say imported executors default to external read-only and may keep operating. Refinement 2 already accepts a recurring tail, so this is a wording correction rather than a change to the model. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Restate it as: after go-live, Asym is the only writer of supporter records and the system of record for history, except gifts charged by the declared external executors until each one has stop proof or the tail closes.

#### v6-10: 'Exact repeat import' is narrowed without saying so, and could be test-passed with a file-digest shortcut

- **What could go wrong:** Refinement 1 narrows published 'exact repeat import' and 'Reimport converges' / 're-import updates instead of duplicating' to replaying the same snapshot. If it is implemented as 'same file digest means skip':
- it violates Phase 29's rule that a matching digest is not business identity;
- it skips identical bytes re-uploaded after a reversal or under a superseding Switch.

Non-identical inputs still need row-level convergence even in v1: catch-ups, forgotten files, and second attempts.

- **Why it matters:** A misread acceptance criterion produces either duplicates or skipped re-plans.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** - roadmap.md:4363, :4387 and :4401 (repo-published);
- owner-constraints.md:370-372;
- PR #2430 head founder-brief :151 'A matching digest proves byte equality, not identical provenance ... or business significance' (ratified/published, unmerged);
- C11;
- q01-r5 E-40 and X4;
- q01-r6 verifier note.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Define the replay identity (manifest plus plan digest) separately from row convergence (source-record binding), record the narrowing as a founder narrowing, and test both.
- **Spec language:** v1 'exact repeat import' (a founder narrowing of roadmap.md:4387/:4401) means re-submitting the same snapshot manifest and plan digest. It returns the prior run, with zero target writes and zero new identities. Idempotency never rests on file digest alone. Identical bytes uploaded under a new Switch, or after a reversal, are re-planned. Any later snapshot (catch-up, manifest extension, superseding Switch) converges per row through source-record bindings: previously accepted and unchanged rows show 'no write', changed rows show a reviewed disposition, and nothing is duplicated.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified. PR #2430 head founder-brief.txt:151 says a matching digest proves byte equality, not provenance or business significance. roadmap:4363 requires reimport to converge on the same target identity, which is wider than replaying the same snapshot. The narrowing should be recorded explicitly. The test split is proportionate. (corrected severity: medium)

#### v6-11: 'Practice runs that change nothing' is not literally true and has no falsifiable definition

- **What could go wrong:** Practice runs do write: Phase 29 bytes, plans, staged derivatives, and possibly a staged shadow. A literal 'nothing changed' test either fails or gets weakened ad hoc, and ministries are misled about what PII Asym retains.
- **Why it matters:** This is a test gap and a privacy-expectation gap at the same time.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / high
- **Evidence:** - C11;
- PR #2430 head founder-brief :283 (uploading does not import);
- q01-r5 section 3.4 (R1-R3 modes).
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** List the writes a practice run is allowed to make and prove everything else is zero.
- **Spec language:** A practice run writes only: Phase 29 source custody, Phase 30 plan and staging records, its own audit and run records, and (where a target owner requires one) a discardable staged shadow invisible to ordinary views. A real-database test proves zero rows written to target-owner tables, source-record bindings, outward-effect outbox or dispatch rows, provider API calls and Giving Address issuance. The ministry is shown what was retained and its retention rule.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Correct but minor. Uploading writes Phase 29 bytes and Phase 30 staging records (founder-brief:283; D11). 'Changes nothing' should mean no target-owner writes and no outward effects. That is a wording and test point. (corrected severity: low) Corrected fix: Define a practice run as writing only Phase 29 custody and Phase 30 session, staging and run records, with zero target-owner writes or outward effects. Test that.

#### v6-12: The zero-unintended-effect proof is not scoped to catch-ups run with automations live, nor to senders outside Asym

- **What could go wrong:** Catch-ups run after go-live with Asym receipts, automations and recipes active, so tests that only cover the pre-go-live run prove nothing about the riskiest path. Some outward effects also happen outside Asym and cannot be proven by Asym:
- SiteStacker System Messages and Spark;
- gateway-sent receipts;
- Stripe's automatic ACH mandate emails;
- Stripe refund emails.
- **Why it matters:** Without explicit scope the C23 gate is either unprovable or quietly reinterpreted.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** - roadmap.md:4401 (repo-published); C23;
- docs.stripe.com/get-started/data-migrations/payment-method-imports (verified-vendor-doc via q01-r5 verifier, accessed 2026-11-01);
- q01-r1 R20;
- r10-gap-g5 C1: no suppression carrier integrated.
- **Effect on the answer:** changes
- **Best fix:** Split the gate into Asym-provable effects, tested for every run kind with automations live, and senders outside Asym, covered by census plus attestation.
- **Spec language:** The zero-unintended-effect gate is proven by production-shaped real-database tests for every run kind, including catch-up runs executed with all tenant automations, receipt issuance and workflow recipes active. Each test asserts no email, SMS, workflow trigger, acknowledgment release, receipt, provider call, enrollment, Giving Address issuance or public change. Effects outside Asym (SiteStacker messages, gateway and Stripe emails) are outside Asym's proof. They are listed in the outward-effect census and covered by attestation items, and the switch evidence states that limit.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: The C23 gate (roadmap:4401) is not scoped to catch-ups that run after automations are live. That point overlaps v6-19 (per-fact suppression). What v6-12 adds is splitting Asym-provable effects from senders outside Asym, which can only be covered by census and attestation. Keep that part. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Run the zero-effect test for every run kind, including catch-ups with automations live. List senders outside Asym as attested-only scope on the Switch record.

#### v6-13: Every acceptance criterion rests on SiteStacker export properties nobody has observed; risk of 'synthetic source success'

- **What could go wrong:** No real export or supplier exists. Each mechanism assumes an unverified property:
- the snapshot manifest assumes stable IDs (R1) and reproducible exports (R2);
- reconciliation assumes screen-count parity (R3) and that controls tie out (R18);
- catch-up joins assume explicit join columns (R4) and processor references (R10);
- boundary classification assumes known timestamp zones (R8).

Tests built on invented columns pass while proving nothing.

- **Why it matters:** The roadmap forbids synthetic source success and requires a representative SiteStacker migration (C23). This is the largest hidden-assumption risk.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** - decision-log section 7 (g3: no artifact or supplier);
- q01-r1 section 6, R1-R20 (unverified);
- roadmap.md:4395 and :4403 (repo-published).
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Trace every acceptance criterion to the R-propositions it depends on, label synthetic fixtures, and mark no profile supported until a real export proves its propositions.
- **Spec language:** Every SiteStacker-profile acceptance criterion names the q01-r1 R-propositions it depends on. Fixtures not derived from a real export are labelled synthetic and cannot satisfy C23. A profile (CRM, giving, recurring, catch-up from processor reports) is marked supported only after a representative real export has proven its propositions; unproven profiles stay visibly unsupported.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Valid. decision-log section 7 shows there is no real artifact, and roadmap:4395 and :4403 forbid synthetic source success and require a representative migration. These rules are already published (C23, and C11's 'unknown limits block a profile'). Tracing every criterion to research R-propositions is heavier than needed. (corrected severity: high) Corrected fix: Label synthetic fixtures as synthetic. No SiteStacker profile can be marked supported until a real export has been observed and recorded under Phase 29 custody.

#### v6-14: Traceability hygiene: colliding research IDs, overloaded terms, contradictory upstream text

- **What could go wrong:** Four sources of ambiguity:
- Colliding IDs: q01-r1..r6 reuse the K, M, X, R, A and P prefixes for different things (for example K1-K26 in r1 vs K1-K10 in r5; M in r2, r4 and r6). An amendment citing 'K4' or 'X3' is ambiguous.
- Overloaded terms: 'freeze' vs 'boundary' (the glossary avoids 'universal external freeze' and 'date-only go-live'); 'catch-up' vs 'reviewed backfill' vs C18 recurring intake; 'source basis' undefined; 'coverage' with two meanings.
- Contradictory upstream text: P13 story 173 still implies zero-gap is the primary path.
- Mis-pinned ticket: O7 points at #2295.
- **Why it matters:** The spec and tickets will inherit ambiguous references and contradictory authority.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** low / high
- **Evidence:** - grep count of ID prefixes across q01-r1..r6 (2026-11-01);
- CONTEXT.md:5042-5043; roadmap.md:4395;
- decision-log D16, D18 and C18;
- P13 PRD :546 vs P16 :1314 (repo-ratified).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Use file-qualified IDs in the decision log, add glossary candidates before the spec, and note which clause governs where texts conflict.
- **Spec language:** Decision-log entries cite research items with file-qualified IDs (for example q01-r5:K4, q01-r1:R10). Glossary candidates before spec: Switch, Switch Boundary (half-open, per source family), Freeze (ministry behaviour, attested), Snapshot Manifest, Catch-up Run (a bounded reviewed backfill of late-tail facts; not recurring intake), Source Basis, History Coverage vs Reversal Coverage. Where Phase 13 story 173 and Phase 16 differ, Phase 16 :1314 governs.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: This is hygiene only. D8 already shows overloaded 'D17'. I did not count the colliding IDs. (corrected severity: low)

#### v6-15: Boundary and cross-source classification have no falsifiable acceptance criteria or adversarial fixtures

- **What could go wrong:** Each of these can silently misclassify, duplicate or lose a gift:
- DST gaps and overlaps, and rows whose time zone is unknown;
- date-only rows on the switch day;
- authorized-not-captured cards and pending ACH at the boundary;
- one charge present under both a SiteStacker ID and a gateway ID;
- identical cus_ IDs on two Stripe accounts after a self-serve copy.

None of these is in published acceptance.

- **Why it matters:** The boundary is the most consequential computation in Option A, and errors in it are silent and financial.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** - roadmap.md:4401 (generic cases only);
- q01-r1 R8 (unverified);
- q01-r5 E-01 and E-02;
- Stripe copy keeps customer IDs (verified-vendor-doc via q01-r4 verifier, docs.stripe.com/get-started/data-migrations/pan-copy-self-serve, accessed 2026-11-01).
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Add named boundary and cross-source fixtures to Phase 30 acceptance.
- **Spec language:** Acceptance includes real-database fixtures for:
- a row exactly at the boundary instant (it classifies as late-tail);
- DST-gap and DST-overlap civil times;
- rows within one day of the boundary with an unknown zone (they go to review);
- date-only rows on the switch day;
- authorized-not-captured and pending-ACH rows (excluded from history; arrive in catch-up when settled);
- one charge present in both a SiteStacker export and a processor report (exactly one gift, or both rows quarantined);
- identical cus_ IDs on two accounts (never cross-resolved);
- a staff edit in Asym followed by a catch-up refund on the same gift (refund appended; the Asym edit is preserved).
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: It mostly overlaps v6-02 (per-family boundary) and v6-20 (cross-source duplicates). Named boundary fixtures are reasonable spec content, but there is no new risk beyond those two. (corrected severity: low)

**Skeptic: material items the reviewer group missed:**

- Footguns: the 'gifts-only' catch-up leaves out reversals of pre-boundary gifts that happen after the switch (refunds, chargebacks, disputes, ACH returns). P13 PRD:1767 makes contribution_postings append-only, and refund, ach_return and chargeback are posting kinds. P16 PRD:1295 says in-flight attempts can resolve after the boundary (repo-ratified). If only new gifts arrive, imported totals drift and reconciliation passes falsely. The catch-up needs a reviewed reversal lane routed to Phase 13 corrections. Severity high (inferred from the cited text).
