- Footguns / other hazards: if the ministry already used Stripe and connects the same account to Asym, Asym's webhook will receive events for SiteStacker-originated charges. packages/api/src/stripe/webhooks.ts:341-410 handles payment_intent, charge.refunded, subscription and invoice events purely by provider ID (repo-integrated). The result is two channels for one charge, alongside the catch-up export. Unmatched events disappear as 'not found' with no gap signal, and matched IDs mutate history. Whether connected-account events reach this endpoint for SiteStacker traffic depends on the Connect topology. That is inferred and I did not verify it against Stripe docs. Severity high.
- Footguns: a switch in the middle of a tax year. Phase 7 receipts and Phase 19 year-end statements must cover gifts SiteStacker already receipted in the same year without issuing new receipts and without leaving statements partial. P13:1031-1035 sets the receipt boundary, D4 leaves the Phase 7 import fact undefined, and C22 requires partial history to be shown as partial. The review does not raise the year-end statement for the switch year. Severity medium-high.
- Testability: v6-18 says 'no default consumed', but C15 (Phase 11, ratified) says 'defaults apply only at create'. The spec must reconcile the two explicitly: catalog defaults must not stand in for imported source facts. Otherwise one ratified rule appears to contradict the other. Severity low.
- Other development hazards: during the declared freeze, SiteStacker giving forms are off but Asym giving is not yet live. Nobody owns donor-facing continuity: where public giving URLs point and how mail-in checks received during the freeze are recorded (O10, Phase 24 giving addresses, Phase 15 offline batches). Gifts can be lost or keyed twice. Severity medium (inferred).

### 5.22 Other development hazards

**Material concern:** yes (reviewer v6). **Synthesis verdict:** The hazards are ownership and sequencing. The migration workstream is unowned, so record the split. Switching before money owner commands exist forces B through the back door, so use the eligibility kit. Environment truth (D13) must be pinned before real PII arrives. Bulk repair after go-live follows roadmap.md:4403 owner procedures plus per-rule spot checks. v6-27 is a duplicate of D12.

**What was checked:** Ownership text: Phase 13 PRD :159-165, :728-747 and :2190-2200; roadmap.md:4341, :4377, :4387 and :4403; decision-log C1, C3, D12 and D13. Live issue states (gh, 2026-11-01): #711, #809, #834 and #570 are OPEN; #2347 and #2087 are OPEN and labelled Ready. Environment truth: docs/ops/environments.md:5, :16, :41 and :206; scripts/seed-demo.sh:6-8 and :99-104. Repair duties: roadmap.md:4395 and :4403; P13 :1767 append-only postings.

#### v6-25: Option A's freeze and reconcile steps straddle a migration workstream nobody owns

- **What could go wrong:** Phase 13 assigns the following to an unnamed 'separate migration workstream': processor coordination, vault transfer, re-authorization campaigns, cutover freezes, bulk reconciliation, connected-account readiness and executor replacement at scale. The founder's 'ministry-declared freeze' and 'reconcile' steps sit on that seam. Either the Phase 30 spec takes this authority by shorthand (forbidden by C3), or nobody builds processor reconciliation or stop proof for non-Stripe executors (#809 is designed around a Stripe adapter).
- **Why it matters:** Unowned duties do not get built, and the money owners lose authority they still hold.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** - P13 PRD :743-746, :2192-2199 and :165 (repo-ratified);
- roadmap.md:4377 (repo-published);
- C3;
- q01-r4 section 6 and G20, plus its verifier;
- gh #809 OPEN.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Record the split now and add an owner requirement (O12) for the processor half.
- **Spec language:** Phase 30 owns the Switch record, boundary, attestation evidence, snapshot manifests, reconciliation-report mechanics and catch-up transport. The following belong to the separate migration workstream (P13 :743-746, :2192-2199), owner [TBD, new O12]: processor coordination, vault transfer, re-authorization campaigns, stop proof for non-Stripe executors, and the money meaning of bulk processor reconciliation. Phase 30 records their outputs as switch evidence and never decides them.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified. P13:743-746 and :2192-2199 assign processor coordination, cutover freezes and bulk reconciliation to a 'separate migration workstream'. roadmap:4377 gives Phase 30 only transport, staging, mappings, plans and run evidence. The decision log has no owner for that workstream (grep 2026-11-01). #809 is OPEN. The founder's freeze and reconcile steps sit partly in this unowned area. (corrected severity: high)

#### v6-26: Switching before the money owner commands exist forces Option B through the back door

- **What could go wrong:** None of the money targets exist yet: the P13 import command (#711), the P16 classifier (#834) and cutover (#809), the Party spine (#570), and the import-origin hold and suppression carrier. The roadmap starts with source-to-Party. A ministry that switches when only Parties are importable leaves giving history in SiteStacker family by family, which is the rejected Option B. Gifts would arrive months later against the old boundary, after Asym-native gifts exist, flipping first-gift and inactivity conclusions and late facts for statements.
- **Why it matters:** It contradicts the decision's own promise ('supporters and their history') and its rejection of Option B.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / high
- **Evidence:** - gh #711, #834, #809 and #570 all OPEN (2026-11-01);
- decision-log section 8;
- roadmap.md:4387;
- r10-gap-g5 C1;
- C22;
- q01-r5 E-40.
- **Effect on the answer:** narrows
- **Best fix:** Adopt a readiness rule for scheduling a switch, and make any late family import a manifest extension under the same Switch.
- **Spec language:** A ministry switch may be scheduled only when every family the ministry needs for 'supporters and their history' is either owner-ready (typed import command qualified) or recorded as a visible exclusion signed by the ministry, with a history-coverage declaration that blocks first-gift and inactivity conclusions for that scope. A family imported after go-live is a reviewed manifest extension under the same Switch and boundary. It is outward-effect-dark, and it revises coverage without triggering first-support work.
- **Bucket:** resolve_before_recording
- **Skeptic ruling:** upheld: Verified OPEN on 2026-11-01: #711, #834, #809 and #570. decision-log section 8 says no money owner commands exist. Scheduling a switch before they exist forces money families to come in late, which is Option B in practice. Treating a readiness rule plus a manifest extension under the same Switch as proportionate. (corrected severity: high)

#### v6-27: Phase 28 factory tickets can pre-empt the shared run, snapshot and plan machinery

- **What could go wrong:** #2347 and #2087 (both OPEN and Ready) put plan storage in Phase 28 tables with no per-profile bounds and add a parser package. Refinement 4's identities would then be retrofitted onto Phase 28-named machinery.
- **Why it matters:** It causes rework and creates a second importer, against roadmap.md:4377 and ADR-0173.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** medium / medium
- **Evidence:** - gh #2347 (OPEN, Ready, updated 2026-10-26);
- gh #2087 (OPEN, Ready);
- decision-log D12 and section 6.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Monitor the tickets until the Phase 30 spec names the shared identity model.
- **Spec language:** The Phase 30 spec names the shared plan, run and snapshot identity model and per-profile bounds. Phase 28 D17 consumes them, never the reverse.
- **Bucket:** monitor (signal: A PR linked to #2347 or #2087 opened or moved to review, or either issue closed.; threshold: Any such event before the Phase 30 spec settles plan and run ownership and per-profile bounds.; owner: Founder, with the Phase 30 grooming lead.; response: The founder decides whether to hold or amend the ticket. Tracker writes need founder authorization; discovery cannot make them.)
- **Skeptic ruling:** duplicate: D12 and section 6 already record it. #2347 and #2087 are confirmed OPEN and Ready. It adds nothing new for Q1. (corrected severity: low)

#### v6-28: Environment truth: census and rehearsal can hit the wrong database, or put real PII outside production

- **What could go wrong:** The docs conflict on whether production holds live donors and money. seed-demo.sh hosted mode expects the production ref by default. Preview must never hold production data, yet rehearsals need real ministry exports. Hosted databases drift from the migration files. The census could run against a schema CI never tested, or real PII could land in preview.
- **Why it matters:** It undermines C23 census evidence and is a privacy exposure.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / low
- **Evidence:** - docs/ops/environments.md:5, :16, :41 and :206 (repo-published);
- scripts/seed-demo.sh:6-8 (repo-integrated);
- decision-log D13.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Pin the environment and project for each switch with schema evidence, and accept real exports only into the tenant's production project under Phase 29 custody.
- **Spec language:** Every census, practice run and final run records the Supabase project ref and the applied-migration list, which must match the release under test. Real ministry exports are accepted only into the target tenant's production project under Phase 29 custody; preview and development reject them.
- **Bucket:** implementation_safeguard
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: D13 already covers the census and wrong-database risk. The new part is real PII being held outside a governed environment. But the fix 'only into the tenant's production project' assumes a production status that D13 records as contested (founder 'no users' ruling versus environments.md). (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Pin the environment and project for each Switch with schema evidence. Accept real exports only into an environment approved for real PII under Phase 29 custody, never a demo-seeded one. D13 settles which environment that is.

#### v6-29: No repair path for a systematic mapping error found after go-live

- **What could go wrong:** Option A commits all of history at once. One wrong mapping rule (for example a wrong designation mapping, or a fee treated as a gift) affects thousands of rows. After go-live there is no rollback, money corrections are append-only per fact, mass replay is forbidden, and no bulk reviewed correction exists. Repair falls to developers doing direct database fixes.
- **Why it matters:** It creates heavy operational burden and a temptation to bypass owner commands. The roadmap requires target-specific reversal and reconciliation procedures before tenant use.
- **Severity / likelihood (reviewer):** high / medium
- **Evidence:** - roadmap.md:4395 and :4403 (repo-published);
- P13 PRD :1767 append-only postings (repo-ratified);
- q01-r5 X3.
- **Effect on the answer:** no_change
- **Best fix:** Before tenant use, define a reviewed bulk-correction plan type that invokes owner correction commands. Add per-mapping-rule spot checks to go/no-go.
- **Spec language:** Before any tenant switch, the Phase 30 spec publishes, per target, a reviewed bulk-correction plan type. It selects affected rows by run and mapping rule, previews owner correction successors, commits through owner commands only, stays outward-effect-dark, and is recorded on the Switch. Go/no-go requires a signed per-mapping-rule spot-check sample from the final practice run.
- **Bucket:** spec_requirement
- **Skeptic ruling:** narrowed: Mostly covered already. roadmap:4403 requires target-specific reversal procedures before tenant use, and owner-constraints:380-385 routes covered facts to owner append-only corrections. A new 'bulk-correction plan type' is a design proposal and goes beyond discovery. Per-mapping-rule spot checks at go/no-go are cheap and useful. (corrected severity: medium) Corrected fix: Trace roadmap:4403 into the Phase 30 spec. Add per-mapping-rule sampled spot checks to go/no-go. Leave the shape of bulk repair to the target owners' correction commands.

- Footguns: the 'gifts-only' catch-up leaves out reversals of pre-boundary gifts that happen after the switch (refunds, chargebacks, disputes, ACH returns). P13 PRD:1767 makes contribution_postings append-only, and refund, ach_return and chargeback are posting kinds. P16 PRD:1295 says in-flight attempts can resolve after the boundary (repo-ratified). If only new gifts arrive, imported totals drift and reconciliation passes falsely. The catch-up needs a reviewed reversal lane routed to Phase 13 corrections. Severity high (inferred from the cited text).
- Footguns / other hazards: if the ministry already used Stripe and connects the same account to Asym, Asym's webhook will receive events for SiteStacker-originated charges. packages/api/src/stripe/webhooks.ts:341-410 handles payment_intent, charge.refunded, subscription and invoice events purely by provider ID (repo-integrated). The result is two channels for one charge, alongside the catch-up export. Unmatched events disappear as 'not found' with no gap signal, and matched IDs mutate history. Whether connected-account events reach this endpoint for SiteStacker traffic depends on the Connect topology. That is inferred and I did not verify it against Stripe docs. Severity high.

## 6. Payment processor handover

Who owns what. The ministry owns its merchant accounts and its money. SiteStacker keeps only processor tokens plus display details such as last four digits and expiry (SiteStacker 151000183512, verified-vendor-doc, accessed 2026-11-01). It runs its own scheduler for card and bank recurring gifts (151000097037 and 151000115212, verified; that the scheduler charges saved cards is inferred, and the server code cannot be checked). PayPal itself runs PayPal Standard subscriptions, which SiteStacker sets up for at most 52 payments (inferred from vendor-served code). Each Site picks its processor by currency and payment method, so one ministry can run several processors (151000118930, verified). Asym never handles card numbers, CVV or full bank details (P13 PRD :743-746). It never asks for or stores the old processor's credentials, and it never imports a saved card or bank account as something it can charge. At the switch Asym takes over supporters and history, and no recurring gift moves on switch day. Each legacy recurring gift stays external and read-only until Phase 16 moves it through a formal cutover: proof the old executor stopped, plus applicable fresh IDs and collection authorization (P16 :1314). Today's ratified text in practice means a donor step for every moved gift (:1230 'replace/bind method' is 'Complete with donor now'; :1236). CASE 1, the ministry used Stripe on its own account. The donors' saved cards are on the ministry's Stripe account. Asym's ratified onboarding uses Stripe Account Links and no OAuth (P13 :659-663). It most likely creates a new connected account and cannot attach the old one; this is a leaning, not verified. Even if it could attach the same account, SiteStacker is a different application running the schedule, so moving a gift is still a formal cutover. Stripe's self-serve copy can move cards, US ACH and SEPA payment methods to the new account and keeps customer IDs, so donors need not retype card numbers. It does not move subscriptions, Canadian pre-authorized debits or legacy SEPA sources (docs.stripe.com/get-started/data-migrations/pan-copy-self-serve, verified-vendor-doc, 2026-11-01). Under today's Phase 16 text a copy authorizes nothing, though it can make the donor step a single hosted 'confirm this card' step. Refunds and disputes on old charges stay on the old account, so the ministry keeps it open through the dispute and return windows. CASE 2, Stripe through a SiteStacker-controlled Connect platform. As case 1, except that only the platform owner (WMTEK) can share the customers for a copy. Partial copy is unavailable for Connect accounts. If WMTEK will not cooperate, donors re-enter their payment details (verified-vendor-doc via verifiers; WMTEK's willingness unverified). CASE 3, a gateway other than Stripe (iATS, Braintree, PaymentSpring/Nelnet, Authorize.net, Payeezy or 'STI'). The ministry asks its old processor to send the vault straight to Stripe. Asym receives only the mapping file and never the card file. Terms vary. Braintree exports cards only (no ACH), only to a PCI-attested receiver, and at most twice (verified). Authorize.net works through a support request (third-party sources only). iATS, STI, PaymentSpring and Payeezy have no verified procedure; Payeezy is reportedly deprecated (third-party). Any offer to 'unmask' full card numbers is refused. The same Phase 16 formal cutover follows. Where no export is possible, donors re-enter their details. CASE 4, PayPal Standard subscriptions. PayPal, not SiteStacker, keeps charging after SiteStacker is off, until the donor cancels or the payment cap is reached. These subscriptions cannot be moved. They stay external, their tail gifts come only from the ministry's own PayPal report files, and donors restart in Asym. Stripe's PayPal payment method is not an import path. Cancel each subscription only after the replacement is active and the old one is proved stopped, so there is no overlap. CASE 5, ACH and bank debit. Asym ACH waits for Phase 13 D.7. A Stripe import of US ACH requires the ministry to certify it holds Nacha mandates collected online. SiteStacker's ACH authorization text is opt-in per Site Channel, and Braintree mandates carry a hard-coded placeholder text, so cohorts with weak evidence should plan fresh mandates. Canadian pre-authorized debits cannot be copied and need re-authorization with notice. Changing the timing of a debit needs 7 days' notice (Stripe ACH doc, verified via verifier). CASE 6, several processors at once. One handover plan per merchant account, each tied to a Legal Entity by a reviewed assertion and never inferred from Site. Reconciliation ties out per merchant account × currency. CASE 7, offline recurring gifts and pledges. Phase 16 classifies them as manual or external arrangements; there is no processor handover. In every case: Asym may observe legacy money only through files the ministry supplies (no credentials, no OAuth in v1). Legacy provider IDs are inert evidence and never live control. Two systems never charge the same gift. WHAT THE FOUNDER MUST DECIDE OR RAISE. (a) The tail policy: how long SiteStacker stays licensed in tail mode, and what happens to gifts not yet moved at closure. (b) Whether to accept a donor-completed step for every moved gift (lightest form: one hosted confirmation of an already-copied card), or ask the Phase 16 owner to consider a narrow 'proved continuation' amendment, with counsel and processor sign-off. (c) PayPal handling in v1. (d) Whether any vault transfer is in the first release, or v1 is re-authorization only. (e) Who owns the processor half of the Phase 13 'separate migration workstream'. (f) Whether v1 accepts only ministries whose merchant accounts map one-to-one onto Legal Entities.

_Lead note:_ processor selection per currency or payment method is unverified; article 151000118930 says only that gateways are connected to a Site.

## 7. What a SiteStacker ministry brings over

Everything below is provisional until a representative real export confirms it (no export, supplier or consenting ministry exists: r10-gap-g3). Each family commits only through its owner's typed import command, so families without one are visibly excluded and not imported. BROUGHT OVER AS AUTHORITATIVE ASYM RECORDS (Asym becomes their only writer at the boundary): people, households and groups mapped to Parties; contact points (emails, phones, addresses), with placeholder and shared emails detected and excluded as contact data with a reason; custom-field values and data-group options through the Phase 11 catalog; relationships the ministry maps to Asym relationship types (Motion-created relationships flagged, and absence proves nothing); consent and suppression evidence, including the final Mailchimp export with archived and cleaned contacts, recorded as evidence that restricts contact and never as consent. BROUGHT OVER AS SETTLED HISTORY (outward-effect-dark, 'legacy' entry method, already receipted): succeeded transactions and designation lines, from exactly one source per gift population and period (Contributions or Historic Giving, with an overlap proof); refunds and reversals as append-only corrections; soft credits and tributes through Phase 14, with acknowledgments held; notes and activities only if F16 allows. BROUGHT OVER AS EVIDENCE ONLY: recurring schedules, offline recurring gifts and pledges, sent to the Phase 16 classifier as external/read-only (never chargeable, never authorization); payment-method display references (brand, last four, expiry) and processor IDs as inert scoped evidence; receipt and statement history; batch identity; source codes until O5 is settled; newsletter membership; Historic Giving ledger lines, offered to Phase 21 as opening evidence and never treated as gifts. CENSUS ONLY (counted, not migrated): SiteStacker logins and roles, SAML and API keys (no Asym account is created; any later Phase 4 claim is separate); Spark and Motion automations, System Messages, print templates, reports and dashboards; domains, giving URLs, QR codes and text-to-give targets (never turned into Issued Giving Addresses); processors, merchant accounts and executors per Site × currency; recurring schedules counted with and without a linked login. VISIBLY EXCLUDED WITH A REASON: child sponsorship, applied per designation and per schedule because it shares checkouts and schedules (whether historical sponsorship payments stay as giving history is a founder and owner question); failed transactions and draft batches; pre-login tokens, password hashes, API secrets, gateway credentials and missionaries' Mailchimp keys (rejected at intake); card and bank numbers (quarantined without preview); whole-database dumps in v1. DECIDED BY A REAL EXPORT: Record Types, Person Owner and Person Site, the Affiliated person, pledges, CRM files, form submissions, events, prayer requests and all content (content goes through Phase 23 portability). PAYMENT PROCESSORS: nothing chargeable crosses through Phase 30. Moving recurring donors is a Phase 16 formal cutover handled with the processor-side migration workstream.

Full 81-family matrix: `research/q01-r1-sitestacker-inventory.md` section 3; administrator export checklist section 5; propositions a real export must settle (R1-R20) section 6.

## 8. Top edge cases

| Scenario                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | Handling                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Owner                                                                                           |
| --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Contributions and Historic Giving are both exported and summed. Historic Giving copies native income automatically and contains accounting ledger lines, some negative and some positive.                                                     | Declare one source basis per gift population and period, with an aggregate overlap proof. Pair rows only on an exported shared ID. Filtering on record_type is mandatory, and ledger rows are never gifts and go to Phase 21 as evidence only. A fixture with both datasets must produce a blocking variance, not a doubled total.                                          | Phase 13 (basis precedence); Phase 30 (detection and report); Phase 21 (ledger evidence)        |
| The ministry reads 'Asym is the place' as 'switch SiteStacker off', or lets the licence lapse, while SiteStacker's scheduler still charges card and ACH recurring gifts.                                                                      | Authority wording (A1). The licence-continuity attestation runs until tail closure. The Kept-on list covers the recurring engine and its messages. The decommission checklist is tied to tail closure. A monitor flags any contract end dated before closure.                                                                                                               | Ministry; Phase 30 runbook and Switch record; Phase 16                                          |
| PayPal Standard subscriptions keep charging after SiteStacker is off, and those gifts never reach a SiteStacker re-export.                                                                                                                    | Treat PayPal as an external executor that has been declared. Tail gifts come only from the ministry's PayPal report files as the single authoritative source. Record each subscription's remaining cycles (srt cap). Cancel only after the Asym replacement is active and the subscription is proved stopped.                                                               | Phase 16; Phase 13 (external money command); Phase 30 (backfill transport); ministry            |
| Gateway-native schedules (Authorize.net ARB, Braintree or PaymentSpring plans, Stripe Subscriptions) run alongside or instead of SiteStacker's scheduler.                                                                                     | Count them per gateway at readiness and again at the final export. Each schedule needs its own stop evidence. If unlisted executors are charging, the overlap-and-gap check blocks the backfill.                                                                                                                                                                            | Ministry; Phase 16 (stop-evidence classes, O14)                                                 |
| A donor whose gift failed before the switch uses SiteStacker's rerun link weeks later, after Phase 16 has activated the Asym replacement. Staff edits on a run date also charge on save.                                                      | The census and each cohort cutover list failed gifts that can still be rerun. Phase 16 stop evidence for SiteStacker cohorts covers the rerun and charge-on-save paths. Backfills record the original due date and the payment date, and a charge after a recorded stop becomes an urgent refund exception.                                                                 | Phase 16; Phase 30 census and backfill; ministry finance                                        |
| After the switch, a refund, chargeback, dispute or ACH return lands on an imported gift, including refunds issued in the gateway dashboard that SiteStacker never recorded.                                                                   | The ministry acts in the old processor. The backfill appends a correction linked by exact scoped reference. If the gift already has an Asym-recorded correction, the backfill reversal is held for finance review. The old merchant account stays open through dispute and return windows.                                                                                  | Phase 13 (corrections and reversal idempotency); Phase 7 (receipt correction); ministry finance |
| The same charge appears under a SiteStacker transaction ID and under a processor or PayPal ID, or a cheque is keyed in both systems during the pause.                                                                                         | One authoritative gift source per executor × period, with other sources as reconciliation evidence only. Rows link only through a proven shared ID, never by name, email or fingerprint (story 171). Post-boundary manual or offline tender rows go to review as possible double entry.                                                                                     | Phase 30 (plan); Phase 13                                                                       |
| Giving forms go off at the boundary but Asym giving opens only after import and sign-off, or Asym checkout opens early and creates donor rows the import cannot match.                                                                        | The founder chooses a giving-continuity option (A8). Asym checkout opens only after a verified Settlement Account Binding exists and Parties are live. Pre-import givers go to match review with no automatic merge. The expected pause is measured in rehearsal and stated at go/no-go.                                                                                    | Founder; Phase 30; Phase 13 (#692 binding); Phase 4                                             |
| The 'automations off' step switches off per-billing receipts, the cancellation path, the declined-payment update link and the Spark recurring reminder while SiteStacker still charges.                                                       | Use the four-state surface list with the Kept-on items until each cohort is handed over or ended. Each duty has one responsible system per cohort, and SiteStacker alone receipts what it charges. The obligation follows SiteStacker's documentation for the ministry's gateway and is confirmed in the census.                                                            | Ministry; Phase 16/17 (re-homing duties); Phase 7                                               |
| Missionaries copied ministry donors into their own Mailchimp accounts (a manual one-way export), and those lists keep sending.                                                                                                                | Record these lists as 'Outside the ministry's control', with a per-missionary item to disconnect, request a suppression export, or record as out of scope. The privacy forwarding list covers opt-outs received in Asym.                                                                                                                                                    | Ministry; Phase 17/28 (consent); Phase 30 (Switch record)                                       |
| A SiteStacker-era cus_ ID is written into donors.stripe_customer_id. Checkout reuses it forever and the whole-account Billing Portal lets the donor change methods SiteStacker still charges. Stripe copy keeps the same ID on both accounts. | Provider IDs are inert scoped evidence only and are never written to live columns. A real-database test proves the next checkout creates a new customer. Only Phase 16 adoption commands may promote an ID to live control.                                                                                                                                                 | Phase 30 (safeguard); Phase 13/16                                                               |
| The ministry runs child sponsorship, which is out of scope. Sponsorship and missionary gifts share a checkout or schedule, and SiteStacker keeps editing and charging shared supporters after the switch.                                     | Exclusion applies per designation and per schedule, never per person. The census asks about residual live writers. The founder chooses between 'ineligible' and a declared residual system with no sync and written acceptance (A12).                                                                                                                                       | Founder; Phase 13/7 (sponsorship history); ministry                                             |
| An export run by a restricted User Group is silently partial, a long export is split into partitions, or SiteStacker is upgraded between rehearsal and the final export.                                                                      | The manifest records the exporter's role, filters, version and on-screen control counts. The partitions must add up to the screen count with no overlap. Header or version drift rejects the manifest with a 're-map required' action. A timed rehearsal of the same manifest shape is required before go/no-go.                                                            | Phase 30; ministry                                                                              |
| Records present in a rehearsal or the final export are missing from a later snapshot or backfill (hard delete, fraud cleanup, merge, transaction deleted after refund).                                                                       | Report as 'absent from snapshot' for review. Never delete or reverse automatically. Absent money rows go to finance review.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Phase 30; Phase 4 (merges); Phase 13 (money)                                                    |
| A donor marked do-not-contact, or whose address bounced, was reported as spam or is manually suppressed, needs to re-authorize a recurring gift. consent.ts blocks even transactional email for them.                                         | List these donors before the deadline for a path the owner approves. There is never a Phase 30 bypass. If no path exists, the gift ends with no catch-up charge or back-charge.                                                                                                                                                                                             | Phase 6/16/17                                                                                   |
| The switch falls mid-year. SiteStacker issued household statements and receipts for gifts before the boundary and keeps receipting tail gifts, while Asym issues per legal donor.                                                             | Warn when the boundary falls inside a window the ministry configures before its statement period ends. Tail gifts carry evidence that they were already receipted, so Asym does not include them in its cumulative statements again. Before decommission, statements for the pre-boundary part of the year come from SiteStacker. Phase 7/19 rule on the switch year (O18). | Phase 7; Phase 19; ministry                                                                     |
| Imported partners show up as 'joined today', Active or USD, or appear under Lapsed or At Risk labels, and backfill lag makes a month look missed.                                                                                             | No reliance on column defaults; unknown values are stored as unknown. No migrated tenant goes live while lapse labels or 'unknown means Active' can render. Tail arrangements display 'given through previous system, last updated {date}' (O17).                                                                                                                           | Phase 30 (import writers); Phase 28/16 (display)                                                |

Whole-database dumps are refused in v1, and mapping files are restricted evidence.                                                                     | Phase 30 (detection); Phase 29 (custody and disposal); Phase 13 (PCI intent)                    |

Rows within one civil day whose zone is unproven go to review. Only settled money is imported, and pending or uncaptured items arrive in the backfill once settled.                                                                                                           | Phase 30; Phase 13; Phase 16 (:1295)                                                            |
