## 9. Ruthless synthesis

### 9.1 Resolve before recording

- Obtain the founder confirmations listed for A1-A5, A8-A12 and A16. Until then, record Q1 in decision-log §1 as 'Option A chosen; amended text pending confirmation of the listed items', not as fully decided.
- Correct decision-log D9 and O7. gh #2295 (OPEN, body read 2026-11-01) drops and recreates the authenticated policies on crm_record_links in pure-tenant form and removes is_super_admin(). It does not revoke the authenticated INSERT/UPDATE grant at 20260508000413_crm_identity_mapping.sql:204. Record the revoke as a separate, unticketed owner requirement (Phase 12/4/9) and keep 'typed audited command plus a test that direct DML is denied' as the condition for trusting bindings. Also note that the inference that 'signup sets app_metadata.tenant_id' is wrong: 20260226113000:223-226 reads server-set raw_app_meta_data. **[Lead correction: do not apply the #2295 part. Spec r2 on #2295 (2026-10-24) does REVOKE ALL on crm_record_links and crm_merge_candidates; D9 and O7 are clarified to cite spec r2 (unintegrated, pending Ready). The condition 'typed audited command plus a test that direct DML is denied' stays.]**
- Add discrepancy D20: owner-constraints.md:373-374 and P14 :1864-1866 say 're-import updates instead of duplicating', but the v1 founder narrowing shows Party-field differences as conflicts and never applies them source-wins. Edit at Phase 30 publication.
- Add discrepancy D21: Phase 13 story 173 (:546) treats donor re-authorization as a fallback 'when zero-gap isn't possible'. Phase 16 :1236 and :1314 govern, so nobody may cite story 173 as approval for migration with no donor action.
- Add discrepancy D22: 'catch-up' is a Phase 16 Avoid term (CONTEXT.md:3101, :3111, :3121, :3136). Use 'Legacy Tail Backfill'.
- Add discrepancy D23: Phase 13 legacy external references (:722-724; story 169 :534-536) are scoped to 'connected account, livemode', which is Stripe-shaped, and the P14 partial UNIQUE(tenant_id, external_ref) is not scoped to a source system. Non-Stripe SiteStacker gateways and PayPal need a source-qualified reference: tenant, source system, installation, record family, source object ID, with processor provenance as optional evidence.
- Add owner requirements O12-O18. O12: name owners for the processor half of the migration workstream (P13/P16). O13: an owner command for externally collected money facts after the boundary, since D.6 covers only pre-Asym gifts (P13/P16). O14: stop-evidence classes for executors that are not Stripe (SiteStacker schedule plus its rerun and charge-on-save paths, PayPal profile, gateway-native schedule); #809 is Stripe-adapter only (P16). O15: a Phase 7 receipt fact that distinguishes 'receipted by the previous system' for tail gifts from pre-Asym history (D4). O16: source-qualified import identity (D23) (P13/P14). O17: a Phase 16/28 projection of the planned external tail, distinct from incident quarantine ('given through previous system, last updated {date}'). O18: Phase 19/7 rules for statements in the switch year.
- Add frontier items. F18: who operates a switch in v1 (the ministry admin self-serve, an Asym migration specialist under an Operator Tenant Grant, or a partner); until it is decided, v1 UI is limited to per-run screens plus a read-only switch summary. F19: tail cap and PayPal handling, if not settled in the confirmations. Under F8: whether a corrected mapping may re-plan the same sealed manifest after go-live, which requires storing last-imported values per field (three-way memory).
- Mark the q01-r1 to q01-r4 inventory and processor analysis as provisional in the record. Set F2 as the next question.

### 9.2 Spec requirements

- Switch record that stores facts, not workflow state: tenant; source system and installation; one boundary per source family (all equal in v1); manifests; runs; attestation items; reconciliation report versions and sign-offs; the declared list of external executors (executor type, gateway, merchant account, source schedule ID); tail closure date and evidence; abort or supersede reason. The displayed stage is derived from these facts and cannot be set directly. At most one non-terminal Switch per tenant and source instance, and one committing run per target family at a time.
- Switch Boundary: a UTC instant plus the tenant giving IANA zone and civil time, defaulting to the first moment of a civil date, displayed as 'starts at the first moment of {date}, {zone} (UTC{offset})'. A reviewed timestamp-zone evidence fact per export profile. Rows within one civil day of the boundary with an unproven zone go to review. The boundary never sets or implies a Phase 16, 20 or 21 boundary.
- Source Snapshot Manifest per Switch, recording for each file: the Phase 29 byte identity, filter or partition, exporter User Group, SiteStacker version, start and finish times, and the on-screen control count captured at export. The final manifest must be taken after the boundary. The union of partitions must equal the on-screen count with no overlap. A restricted exporter or a shortfall against the on-screen count rejects the manifest. Header or version drift rejects it with a 're-map required' action.
- Identity and replay. A Source Record Binding is (tenant, environment, source system, installation or provider account, mode, record family, source ID). Re-submitting the same sealed manifest and plan digest returns the prior run with zero writes. Every other snapshot converges per row on bindings; Phase 4 survivors are followed and erasures block re-creation. Source-changed conflicts are reviewed and never applied source-wins. Absence from a snapshot or backfill is reported for review and never treated as a deletion or reversal.
- Practice runs write only Phase 29 custody and Phase 30 session, plan, staging and run records (plus a discardable shadow where an owner requires one). A real-database test proves zero target-owner writes, bindings, outbox or dispatch rows, provider calls and Giving Address issuance. Mappings, value maps and exclusion rules carry forward. Rehearsal match choices return only as labelled suggestions on unchanged records.
- Readiness census with two parts. The processor-and-automation census is listed in A15. Alongside it: the retained-state census (D13); the census-derived list of legacy outward surfaces; the installation-to-tenant and merchant-account-to-tenant mapping; residual live writers; existing Phase 28 D17 restricted preparation; and a Legal Entity assertion for each merchant account and Site.
- Eligibility gate: each Minimum Switch Kit family is committable through its owner command; every other family is committable or carries a ministry-accepted visible exclusion with 'history not yet covered' status; every recurring cohort has a Phase 16 external/read-only target. A Switch whose census lists a cohort with no target cannot leave readiness.
- Reconciliation report at level 1 (each final-manifest row has exactly one disposition; accepted totals tie to export totals) and level 2 (export totals tie to independent controls), partitioned by Legal Entity × original currency × source basis, with processor controls partitioned by merchant account × currency. One basis per gift population and period, with an overlap proof; Historic Giving rows with record_type=ledger are never gifts. Named variance categories; unexplained variance blocks sign-off. Sign-off is bound to a report version. When money families are in scope, a separate finance approver signs off, or the single operator records an explicit acknowledgment.
- Legacy Tail Backfill: a bounded series of separately reviewed plans on a fixed cadence up to the closure date, with no backfill committable after closure. Exactly one authoritative gift source per executor × period. Rows are classified into three lanes by source-effective time, recording source-effective, source-recorded and discovery times. Money facts are appended through Phase 13/16 owner commands. Schedule state and stop evidence go to Phase 16. Adverse contact facts are applied restrict-only (if confirmed). Parties are resolved only through the stored binding plus the Phase 4 survivor read; a missing, ambiguous or erased match goes to finance exception review. A backfill reversal on a gift that already has a post-import Asym correction is held for finance review. Each fact carries the import-origin outward-effect class and the SiteStacker receipt evidence.
- Legacy surface dispositions: each surface found by the census, per item and scope, is in exactly one state (Off at boundary, Kept on through tail with owner and end condition, Not used, Outside the ministry's control). Each state records actor, time, and an optional Phase 29 evidence reference or 'none supplied'. Display shows 'ministry attested'. Asym cannot activate a duty for a cohort while a matching legacy surface is kept on. States are re-confirmed at every backfill.
- Overlap-and-gap check every backfill cycle against the declared list of external executors. Use the proof wording 'No known overlap in the inspected scope' and record the inspected scope at go-live.
- Giving continuity and activation order according to the founder's choice. Asym checkout for the tenant opens only after a verified Phase 13 Settlement Account Binding exists and the Parties are live. Pre-import Asym givers go to match review with no automatic merge. No Asym automation, newsletter or first-support work runs until consent and suppression evidence (including the final Mailchimp export) is committed and the owning phase has accepted its coverage declaration.
- Declared maximum editing-pause length. Default deadline action: abort before commit and resume SiteStacker. An intake log for requests received during the pause, entered in Asym after go-live. Go/no-go requires a timed rehearsal of the same manifest shape within N days.
- Abort and halt. Abort is possible only before go-live: committed rows stay invisible and no Party is deleted. A per-run and per-Switch Halt records who, when and why. Go-live is blocked until every in-scope run is terminal and verified or is a ministry-accepted visible exclusion (extends O11 with run-level states and notification of named roles).
- Invisible until live. For a new tenant with no live users, missionary and donor surfaces, checkout and claim matching, reports, automations and coverage stay off until one atomic, audited go-live. If the census finds live users, the target owners must provide a cohort-level not-yet-live state before a switch is scheduled.
- Authorization. Separate capabilities for planning and running an import, reading raw rows and error files, committing (money families also need the money owner's capability), and signing off. Boundary declaration, attestation items, commit, sign-off, go-live, abort and tail closure each record the human principal and the capability used. Background chunks carry the plan digest and the committing principal and re-check authority before each chunk; on failure they pause visibly as authority_lost. Non-member operators act only under an Operator Tenant Grant whose tenant is fixed by the server.
- Visibility and sensitive data. Imports never write donors.missionary_id, donors.notes, donors.tags, the anonymity keys in giving_preferences, or any successor field that grants visibility; visibility comes from the Phase 25/28 projection. Anonymity is mapped per gift, and unknown stays anonymous. Sensitive note families default to restricted. No worker Party commits without a Phase 10 security level, and an alias where one is required. An excluded record's disposition keeps only key, family, reason and counts.
- Intake hygiene. Only allowlisted families and columns are accepted. A bounded content scan for card numbers, bank numbers and secrets runs before staging; a match quarantines the file without preview, the only remedy is a corrected re-export, and disposal follows Phase 29. Whole-database dumps are refused in v1. Every file records its exporter's role.
- Real exports are accepted only into Phase 29 custody, in an environment approved for real PII (resolved under D13), never preview, development or a demo-seeded environment, and only on the terms F2 settles. Practice snapshots and their derivatives are disposed N days after reconciliation acceptance or abort, and the disposal is recorded.
- The privacy list of items 'pending in legacy systems' and the ministry-attested decommission checklist (A16). Tail closure and decommission sign-off are blocked while any item is open.
- Zero-effect proof uses production-shaped real-database tests for every run kind, including backfills run with all tenant automations, receipt issuance and workflow recipes active. Effects that happen outside Asym (SiteStacker messages, gateway emails, Stripe import and refund emails, PayPal notices) are listed on the Switch as covered only by ministry attestation.
- Fixtures not derived from a real export are labelled synthetic and cannot satisfy C23. No SiteStacker profile is marked supported until a representative real export has been observed under Phase 29 custody.
- Go/no-go includes a signed, sampled spot check for each mapping rule from the final practice run. The target-specific reversal and reconciliation procedures required by roadmap.md:4403 are published before tenant use.
- The representative migration records committed rows per minute per family at a declared size profile, plus the time the manual export takes. Each go/no-go estimates the commit duration and giving pause from the tenant's census. Scheduling a tenant above the measured profile is blocked by default, and the founder may override.
- Statement timing. Warn when the boundary falls inside a ministry-configured window before the end of its statement or receipting period. Before decommission, statements or receipts for the pre-boundary part of the switch year must be issued from SiteStacker or covered by a Phase 7/19 rule.
- Donor contact. Imports never set email verification or claim state, and the switch sends no account invitations. Placeholder, shared or role emails make a Party ineligible for invitations until staff record verification. Donors whose consent or suppression state blocks transactional email (do_not_contact, bounce, spam, manual) are listed for an owner-approved path and are never contacted through import tooling. Each donor gets at most one handover request covering all their legacy arrangements, under a Phase 16/17 message contract.
- The only per-profile SiteStacker parser bounds are measured and published before any switch is scheduled; the Phase 28 D17 bounds are not the SiteStacker bounds. One SiteStacker source profile plus one canonical external-money-facts target. Processor CSVs supplied by the ministry are kept as exact source bytes and mapped through presets, never re-keyed by hand. There are no per-gateway parsers and no OAuth in v1.
- Edge cases are specified as invariants, each with representative production-shaped fixtures, and operational scenarios go in a published ministry switch runbook. Minimum invariants: absence is never deletion; each fact is classified once against the boundary; imports and backfills cause no outward effects; legacy executors stay external and read-only until a Phase 16 formal cutover; unmatched money waits in match review; a later snapshot converges and never applies source-wins; money totals never cross Legal Entity or currency; authority never returns to SiteStacker after go-live without a founder decision; the tail is bounded by a closure date; provider IDs are inert evidence.

### 9.3 Implementation safeguards

- Legacy processor and SiteStacker identifiers are stored only as inert source evidence, scoped by tenant, environment, provider, provider account, mode and object type. No import, backfill or mapping preset writes them to columns read by live checkout, the donor portal, refunds, recurring or webhook code: today that means donors.stripe_customer_id, donations.stripe_payment_intent_id/stripe_charge_id and donor_pledges.stripe_*. Only Phase 16 adoption commands (#834) may promote a reference to live control. Real-database test: a donor imported with a cus_ keeps a null live customer column, and their next Asym checkout creates a new customer on the bound account. The reasons are in saga.ts:209-210, which reuses any existing ID; the completion RPC's COALESCE (20260223170000:1255); billing.ts:38-41, which opens a portal for the whole account; and Stripe self-serve copy keeping cus_ IDs (verified-vendor-doc via the q01-r4 verifier).
- No import or backfill relies on a column default for a business fact (joined_date, gift_date, currency, status, source, preferred_language). Every value is explicit or recorded as unknown, and a real-database test proves no default fired (init_schema.sql:76, :82; foundation_1_schema.sql:334-338). Imported Parties with unknown status never render as Active, New, Lapsed or At Risk (donors.ts:174-192; P16 :1068). Phase 11 'defaults apply only at create' never stands in for an imported source fact.
- A switch cannot go live, and no rehearsal can run, while the tenant can resolve a Stripe client from tenants.stripe_secret_key or the platform-key fallback (tenant-client.ts:64-66). Removing the fallback is a Phase 13 deliverable (#692; P13 :664-667), not a Phase 30 one.
- Source bindings and lineage are append-only and written only by the Phase 30 commit path or an audited owner command, with actor and run recorded. Corrections append a superseding row. A separate owner ticket, not #2295, revokes authenticated INSERT/UPDATE on crm_record_links and crm_merge_candidates before any binding counts as trusted provenance. **[Lead correction: #2295 spec r2 includes the revoke; the requirement is that it lands, plus the typed command and the denial test.]**
- Backfill profiles can invoke only owner commands that append money facts or executor state. There is structurally no path to create or update a Party, contact, consent or relationship. Acceptance test: a backfill file that changes an address produces a reviewed discrepancy and zero Party writes.
- Runs execute only inside withTenant(run.tenant_id), with a tenant set by the server when the run is created. Every resolved target ID (missionary, fund, designation, Party) is checked against the same tenant before the command is invoked, because missionary_id and fund_id have no composite tenant foreign key (only donor_id does: 20261025100000:10-13).
- Run plan, cursor and per-row outcomes live in Postgres. Inngest executes bounded chunks keyed by (run, chunk index), and each chunk commit is idempotent on that key. The cursor never advances past an uncommitted lower chunk. No per-row events are emitted. Chunk size keeps measured p99 wall time below half the host timeout.
- Reconciliation and backfills read processor report and export artifacts held in Phase 29 custody, never paginated provider API listings (Stripe rate and read allocations; verified-vendor-doc via v5). Asym never requests, stores or uses credentials for a legacy processor, SiteStacker, Mailchimp or another legacy integration.
- Gateway vault tokens and Stripe or processor mapping files are restricted provider evidence. They are excluded from issue grids, error downloads and exports, and are disposed of with the snapshot unless Phase 16 names them as cutover evidence it keeps.
- Error files and issue-grid downloads use csvSafeCell formula-injection neutralization (packages/lib/csv.ts, ADR-0212) and require the raw-row capability. Care and security-classified values are redacted.
- Every census, practice run and final run records the Supabase project ref and the list of applied migrations, which must match the release under test (D13; scripts/seed-demo.sh:6 defaults to the production ref).
- Manual live send actions (receipt resend at contribution-operations resend-receipt.ts:17-55, acknowledgment release) refuse import-origin and backfill facts unless the governing owner rule allows them. Suppression is keyed to the origin of each fact, never to a tenant-level 'migration mode'.
- Test fixtures include the same cus_ on two Stripe accounts, the same source ID in two installations or record families, one charge appearing in both a SiteStacker export and a processor report, and a staff-recorded refund followed by a backfill refund on the same gift.

### 9.4 Monitor (signal, threshold, owner, response)

| Risk                                                                                                                                                     | Signal                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | Threshold                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Owner                                                                                                                                | Response                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Legacy Recurring Tail does not close and becomes a standing dual feed, or a legacy executor charges after its recorded stop                              | For each Switch: the count and amount of active legacy external recurring arrangements by executor × merchant account × currency; days since the boundary; backfill run count; any charge on a schedule after its recorded stop proof; and the age of each 'Cancellation requested — provider confirmation pending' item | Urgent: any charge after recorded stop proof, or from no listed executor. Escalate: any arrangement still active 30 days before the recorded closure date; the closure date passing with any executor not proved stopped, handed over or ended; or a pending cancellation older than one billing cycle | Phase 16 owner (stop proof and cohort decisions) with the ministry's named switch owner; the Asym switch operator watches the signal | Hold activation of any related Asym schedule, open a refund exception in the old processor, and notify the donor through the Phase 16/17 path. For each cohort, escalate to a Phase 16 handover, a deliberate end with donor notice, or a recorded extension with a reason. Escalate to the founder if closure slips twice |
| Residual SiteStacker writes after go-live (a forgotten form, text-to-give, QR code, staff habit, or a merchant account shared with another organization) | For each backfill cycle: post-boundary source rows that are not occurrences of a declared legacy recurring arrangement (one-time gifts, gifts from forms recorded Off, new source persons from inbound capture, rows from merchant accounts not in the census)                                                           | Any row that contradicts a recorded surface state, or any row from a merchant account not in the census                                                                                                                                                                                                | Asym switch operator together with the ministry admin who signed the surface dispositions                                            | Hold commit of the affected rows, require re-attestation of the surface states, and list affected donors for the receipt owner. Send unmatched rows to match review or finance exception. For a merchant account not in the census, pause further backfills and re-run the merchant-account census                         |
| Missionary recurring support lost during the donor handover (attrition has no measured benchmark)                                                        | Share of each handover cohort's legacy arrangements confirmed in Asym, and unconfirmed monthly recurring amount per missionary, by days since the handover request                                                                                                                                                       | Set after the first switch. Starting values are assumptions to recalibrate: under 60% of the cohort confirmed at day 21, or any missionary with more than 25% of monthly recurring support unconfirmed at day 21                                                                                       | Phase 16 owner with the ministry's named switch owner                                                                                | Personal follow-up by staff or missionaries, as far as Phase 28 visibility allows. Extend that cohort's external tail before the closure date. Report measured rates to the founder to inform Design 1 versus a proved-continuation request                                                                                |
| Backfills stop, tail evidence decays, or the exception backlog grows unseen                                                                              | Days since the last successful backfill per executor; quarantined share per backfill; open migration exceptions by class and age; unexplained level-2 variance per merchant account × currency; a SiteStacker contract end or processor account closure dated before the planned tail closure                            | Cadence plus 7 days; over 5% of rows quarantined (proposed) or any exception older than 30 days; any non-zero unexplained variance; any shutdown date before closure                                                                                                                                   | Phase 30 run owner for the Switch, with the ministry finance approver                                                                | Run the backfill or record why not. Pause the next backfill and escalate money exceptions to Phase 13 finance exception review. Block tail closure. Before shutdown, take final exports and processor reports, or extend the tail                                                                                          |
| Privacy requests honoured in Asym are not carried out in SiteStacker, processors or missionary-owned lists while those systems still hold data or send   | Age of the oldest open 'pending in legacy systems' privacy item, and the open count, for each open Switch                                                                                                                                                                                                                | Any item open more than 10 business days. This is a conservative operational limit borrowed from the FTC opt-out window, not a legal conclusion for nonprofits                                                                                                                                         | The ministry's named switch owner; Phase 30 provides visibility on the Switch record                                                 | Escalate to the ministry admin and the Asym switch operator. Block tail closure and decommission sign-off. Hold any backfill row that touches an affected Party                                                                                                                                                            |

### 9.5 Ordered path

1. This round: the founder confirms or adjusts the material amendments (A1-A5, A8-A12, A16) and the processor and PayPal choices. Only then is Q1 recorded in decision-log §1, in the same edit that corrects D9 and O7, adds D20-D23, O12-O18 and F18-F19, and marks the bring-over research provisional. Why: C3 and the materiality rule. Founder shorthand cannot narrow published text, and changes to cutover, reversal, consent or money policy need founder confirmation. Depends on: nothing.

2. Next question: F2, the source of a real SiteStacker export and the processor reports, and the custody and agreement terms. Why: the bring-over inventory, reconciliation, boundary classification and backfill joins all rest on unobserved export properties (q01-r1 R1-R20: stable IDs, processor IDs, Historic Giving shared ID, timestamp zones). It has the longest lead time, and C23 forbids synthetic success. Depends on step 1.

3. In parallel with step 2, once a candidate ministry exists: send that ministry the processor-and-automation census as a questionnaire with no personal data (gateways per Site × currency, executors, PayPal, gateway-native schedules, ACH authorization field, Stripe own-account versus platform, licence terms, WMTEK cooperation). Why: it sizes the PayPal, ACH, platform-Stripe and weak-mandate cohorts and the tail before any donor data moves. Depends on: a candidate ministry from F2.

4. Founder-authorized owner routing, with no tracker writes during discovery. Phase 13/16 owners name owners for the processor half of the migration workstream and draft edits to P13 :743-746 and :2192-2199 (O12). Phase 16 rules on non-Stripe stop evidence (O14) and on whether a hosted confirmation of an already-moved method counts as fresh authorization, or considers a proved-continuation amendment. Phase 13 defines the post-boundary external money command (O13) and source-qualified references (O16). Phase 7 defines the receipt fact (O15). Phase 19 rules on switch-year statements (O18). A separate ticket revokes client DML on crm_record_links. Why: Phase 30 cannot decide money, authorization or receipt rules (C1, C3), and tail closure depends on them.

5. Continue the frontier in dependency order. F13 first: money in the first release and Legal Entity evidence, which now defines the Minimum Switch Kit. Then F5 (whether tail gifts may earn Asym acknowledgments), F12 (consent and suppression prerequisites, including the final Mailchimp and missionary-list exports), F11 (retention, how long SiteStacker stays readable, tail cap), F18 (operator model), F4 (rehearsal mode), F8 (write modes and post-go-live corrective re-plan), then F9, F14 and F16. Handle F17 (#2347/#2087 governance) in parallel before those PRs merge, because it is time-sensitive (D12).

6. After confirmations and before any spec: draft a feature-local ADR, as proposed, for 'one planned switch per ministry with an external legacy recurring tail'. Why: it is hard to reverse, surprising and a real trade-off. Depends on step 1.

7. Only on the founder's explicit /to-spec authorization: the Phase 30 spec, covering the Switch record, about ten invariants with fixtures, the runbook, the reconciliation report, Legacy Tail Backfill profiles and acceptance criteria traced to roadmap.md:4401-4403. Depends on steps 2-6.

8. Preconditions before any tenant switch: Phase 29 custody implemented (PR #2430 merged and built); the Party spine (#570) and kit-family owner commands (#711, #834, #512 and others); the Phase 13 Settlement Account Binding replacing the plaintext key and platform fallback (#692); the lineage DML revoke; the D13 hosted census; a measured representative migration (throughput, export timing, bounds). Why: owner-constraints.md:389-392 and C23.

9. First ministry switch, following the runbook: census, practice runs, go/no-go, boundary and editing pause, final manifest, import, reconcile, go-live, then backfills with overlap checks and monitors, Phase 16 cohort handovers, tail closure and decommission checklist. Report measured handover and backfill data back to the founder.

## 10. Proposed glossary terms (settle when Q1 is recorded)

| Term                             | Definition                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Avoid                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| SiteStacker Switch (Phase 30)    | Proposed; settles when Q1 is recorded. The one planned transfer of supporter and history authority for one Tenant from one SiteStacker installation to Asym, recorded as a durable Switch with its boundary, manifests, runs, attestations, reconciliation sign-offs, declared external executors and tail closure. It never moves a recurring executor; that is a Phase 16 formal cutover. | cutover (collides with Posting Ownership Cutover, Field Account Operational Cutover and Phase 16 formal cutover), migration complete, big bang, go-live as the only state, tenant migration flag |
| Switch Boundary                  | The recorded half-open UTC instant, shown in the tenant giving IANA zone and starting by default at the first moment of a civil date, that classifies source facts as pre-switch history or post-switch legacy facts for Phase 30 import only. It never sets or implies a Phase 16, 20 or 21 boundary.                                                                                      | freeze, cutoff date, midnight, date-only go-live, universal external freeze, posting cutover                                                                                                     |
| Editing pause (ministry action)  | The ministry's attested action of stopping staff edits in SiteStacker and setting each legacy surface to its recorded state around the Switch Boundary. Asym records it but cannot enforce it.                                                                                                                                                                                              | freeze, lock, read-only SiteStacker                                                                                                                                                              |
| Source Snapshot Manifest         | The sealed set of export files for one Switch. Each file records its Phase 29 byte identity, filter or partition, exporter role, SiteStacker version, export times and on-screen control counts. The final manifest is taken after the boundary and may contain several files.                                                                                                              | the final export (as one file), snapshot (unqualified; collides with Receipt Identity Snapshot and other Snapshot terms), file hash as identity                                                  |
| Source Record Binding (Phase 30) | The append-only link from (Tenant, environment, source system, installation or provider account, mode, record family, source ID) to an Asym target. It follows Phase 4 survivors and blocks re-creation after erasure. Replay and convergence rest on it, never on a file digest or a manifest.                                                                                             | source binding (Phase 21 usage), external ID as identity, provider ID as identity, Execution binding (Phase 16)                                                                                  |
| Exact repeat import              | Re-submitting the same sealed Source Snapshot Manifest and plan digest. It returns the prior run with zero writes. It is a v1 founder narrowing of roadmap.md:4387; other snapshots still converge on Source Record Bindings.                                                                                                                                                               | re-import update, refresh import, upsert, same file means skip                                                                                                                                   |
| Legacy Recurring Tail            | The bounded period after a Switch goes live during which named legacy recurring arrangements keep being charged by SiteStacker, PayPal or a gateway as Externally controlled/read-only (Phase 16). It ends at the recorded tail closure date.                                                                                                                                               | grace period, side-by-side, dual run, migration period, parallel running                                                                                                                         |
| Legacy Tail Backfill             | One separately reviewed, bounded plan within the Legacy Recurring Tail. It brings in append-only money facts, schedule-state and stop evidence, and restrict-only adverse contact facts recorded by legacy executors after the boundary, and never writes supporter details (roadmap.md:4395 reviewed backfill).                                                                            | catch-up (Phase 16 Avoid term for collecting missed periods: CONTEXT.md:3101, :3111, :3121, :3136), sync, delta import, refresh import, feed                                                     |
| Legacy Surface Disposition       | The ministry-attested state of one SiteStacker, processor or missionary-held outward surface for one scope: Off at boundary, Kept on through tail (owner and end condition), Not used, or Outside the ministry's control.                                                                                                                                                                   | automations-off attestation, kill switch, SiteStacker switched off                                                                                                                               |
| Switch live / Switch closed      | Live: supporter and history authority has moved to Asym at the boundary. Closed: every legacy executor is proved stopped, formally cut over or ended, the tail is closed and decommission is recorded.                                                                                                                                                                                      | migration complete, done, fully migrated                                                                                                                                                         |

**ADR:** recommended: Feature-local ADR (Phase 30, proposed): One planned switch per ministry with an external legacy recurring tail. All three tests are met. Hard to reverse: after go-live, correction is roll-forward only, authority never returns to SiteStacker without a new founder decision, and the first switch ends the fresh-build posture for affected owners (roadmap.md:432-434). Surprising: Asym becomes the system of record while SiteStacker must keep running in tail mode, and every moved recurring gift is a Phase 16 formal cutover even for ministries that already used Stripe. Both contradict common vendor practice of moving tokens with no donor action. Real trade-off: A versus B versus C, a declared giving pause versus a concurrent writer, and donor attrition from re-authorization versus asking Phase 16 for a proved-continuation amendment. Write it after the founder confirms the material amendments and before any spec. Record it as proposed until ratified, and cite P13 :735-747 and :2192-2199, P16 :1236 and :1314, roadmap.md:4363, :4369 and :4395, and ADR-0106/0058 as precedent.

## 11. Adversarial check

### What could go wrong with this answer?

Missionary recurring support could silently end if SiteStacker is switched off or its licence lapses while it is still the executor. Donors could be charged twice at handover through reruns, gateway-native schedules or PayPal. Reconciliation could tie the import to itself and pass a doubled or truncated history. Online giving could go dark for days, or Asym checkout could open early and create duplicate supporters, or route money to the platform account through the key fallback. Missionaries could see false 'lapsed' or 'joined today' signals. The processor half of the work could stay unowned so the tail never closes. Real donor data could land in ungoverned environments before Phase 29 custody exists. Opt-outs could keep being violated from SiteStacker or missionary-owned Mailchimp lists.

### What hidden assumptions are we making?

Several assumptions sit underneath the decision. That SiteStacker's own scheduler is the executor for card and ACH gifts (inferred; server code unverifiable). That exports carry stable internal IDs, processor IDs, explicit join columns and known time zones (q01-r1 R1-R20, all unverified). That a willing ministry and a usable export exist (none found). That ministries can supply processor and PayPal report files covering the tail. That SiteStacker can be kept licensed in a tail mode and can stop individual schedules with evidence (unverified). That each merchant account maps to one Legal Entity. That Account Links cannot attach an existing Stripe account (leaning, unverified). That owner import commands will exist before the first switch. That Mastercard-style pre-billing duties apply beyond the PaymentSpring case.

### How does this affect the whole product?

A switch touches Phase 4 identity and merge, Phase 7 receipts, Phases 9-11 CRM and custom data, Phase 13 ledger and bindings, Phase 14 credits and holds, Phase 15 offline batches, Phase 16 recurring control, Phase 17 messages, Phase 19 statements, Phases 20-21 accounting and Field Accounts, Phase 24 giving addresses, Phases 25 and 28 portal displays, and Phase 29 custody. Phase 30 delivers only transport, staging, plans, run evidence, the Switch record and reconciliation reports. Owners decide money, authorization, receipts and display. The tail state must reach donor, missionary and staff surfaces honestly.

### How does this affect the end-user experience?

Donors should notice nothing from the data move itself: no welcomes, receipts, invitations or account creation. Recurring donors will notice the handover. Under today's Phase 16 text that means one hosted confirmation per donor at best, with stragglers followed up personally and uncontactable donors routed to an owner-approved path. During the tail, donors keep using SiteStacker to change or cancel legacy gifts while Asym shows them as 'managed by previous system'. Missionaries need an honest tail display and no false lapse labels. Staff face a short editing pause with an intake log. The ministry admin works through a clear, census-derived checklist, and at most a measured, declared giving pause.

### Does this follow modern best practices?

Current practice is: repeated mock cutovers, a business go/no-go and a rollback plan (MS Learn go-live checklist, verified-vendor-doc via research); processor-to-processor vault transfer with no card numbers in the platform's hands (Stripe data migrations, verified); staff-led nonprofit CRM conversions with no published freeze runbook (vendor docs and marketing); re-enrollment campaigns whose attrition is not measured anywhere; Stripe's toolkit advice to cancel old schedules before they charge. Two conflicts with the repo are resolved in the repo's favour. (1) Token migration with no donor action conflicts with P16 :1236 and :1314, so the repo governs; the founder may ask the Phase 16 owner to consider a narrow proved-continuation amendment. (2) Vendor 'freeze' language conflicts with ADR-0106 and ADR-0058, so the repo governs, using an attested editing pause, 'No known overlap in the inspected scope' and post-activation overlap monitoring.

### Does this fit Asym's existing repo and product direction?

The decision aligns with roadmap.md:4363 (convergence), :4369 (reconciliation partition), :4395 (reviewed bounded backfill, no permanent dual writers), :4401-4403 (acceptance), P13 D.6 and :735-747, P16 :1314 and :2581-2593, C1-C23, and the ADR-0106/0058 cutover precedent. Collisions to fix: 'catch-up' is a Phase 16 Avoid term; 'freeze' is a Phase 21 Avoid term; D9 and O7 wrongly say #2295 revokes DML; P13's unowned migration workstream must be edited by its owners (C3); the 'connected account, livemode' reference scope is shaped around Stripe; P13 story 173 versus Phase 16. No implementation exists on develop 629cb71dc, so nothing here describes current behaviour.

### Should we adjust the recommendation?

Record Option A with the amended text, after the founder confirms A1-A5, A8-A12, A16 and the processor and PayPal choices. Correct D9 and O7, and add D20-D23, O12-O18 and F18-F19. Mark the research provisional. Ask F2 next. Route owner requirements only with founder authorization. Draft the feature-local ADR as proposed. Do not create a spec, tickets or tracker writes during discovery.

## 12. Founder confirmations, next question, unknowns

### Founder confirmations needed

- A1/clause 2: accept the wording 'Asym is the system of record and only writer for supporters, history and staff work; legacy recurring arrangements stay external and read-only until each Phase 16 formal cutover or recorded end', and accept that the ministry keeps SiteStacker licensed, with its recurring engine and recurring donor messages running, until the Legacy Recurring Tail closes.
- A2/clause 1a: confirm the Minimum Switch Kit. Recommended: Parties and households; contact points with consent and suppression evidence; one-time giving history including reversals; recurring evidence for the Phase 16 classifier. Also confirm there is no exception path that lets a switch go ahead without a kit family.
- A3: confirm the tail policy shape (closure date recorded at go/no-go, fixed backfill cadence, final backfill after the dispute and return windows), set the cap (q01-v5 proposed 12 months after go-live, an assumption), and say which closure outcomes are acceptable: Phase 16 handover; the ministry ends the gift with donor notice and a restart invitation; Phase 16 reclassifies it as an ongoing external arrangement; or a recorded extension.
- A4/clause 6: confirm the Legacy Tail Backfill scope: money facts plus schedule-state and stop evidence; sourced per executor, including the ministry's own PayPal and processor report files; never creates or updates supporters; no outward effects. Separately, confirm that adverse contact facts (unsubscribe, do-not-contact, deceased, erasure) are applied, restrict-only.
- A5/clause 7: confirm the four-state legacy surface list, including the Kept-on list (recurring engine, legacy receipts, recurring confirmations and reminders, declined-payment message and update link, donor cancel and update path), and that SiteStacker alone receipts what it charges during the tail.
- A8/clause 4: choose giving continuity. Recommended: (a) a short, declared online-giving pause whose length is measured in rehearsal, using a Party-first commit order. Alternative: (b) SiteStacker one-time giving stays on until Asym giving opens, with gap gifts arriving through the first backfill.
- A9/clause 10: confirm that abort is possible only before go-live, and that after go-live authority never returns to SiteStacker without a new founder decision. Batch reversal stays as published.
- A10: confirm the proposed ownership split and authorize routing to the Phase 13/16 owners the request to name owners for the processor half of the 'separate migration workstream' (P13 :743-746, :2192-2199). Discovery makes no tracker writes.
- A11: confirm that v1 is one tenant fed by one SiteStacker installation, that shared or multi-organization installations and merchant accounts are out of scope, and say whether v1 also requires each legacy merchant account to map to exactly one Legal Entity.
- A12: for ministries where SiteStacker modules will keep writing to migrated people (for example child sponsorship), choose whether they are ineligible for v1 or proceed under a declared residual system (no sync, an overlap count, and written ministry acceptance).
- A16: confirm the 'pending in legacy systems' privacy list and the ministry-attested decommission checklist.
- Processor question for the Phase 16 owner (founder decides whether to raise it): under today's ratified text, every moved recurring gift needs proof the old executor stopped plus applicable fresh authorization, which in practice means a donor step. Option one: accept that, with the lightest form being one hosted confirmation of an already-moved card ('Design 1'). Option two: ask the Phase 16 owner to consider a narrow 'proved continuation' amendment (same merchant evidence, a processor-to-processor vault transfer, network transaction IDs, retained mandate evidence, per-executor stop proof, planned notice), with counsel and processor sign-off.
- PayPal in v1: confirm that PayPal Standard subscriptions stay external until each donor restarts in Asym, that their tail gifts come only from the ministry's PayPal report files, and that cancellation happens only after the replacement is active and the old subscription is proved stopped (no overlap).
- Whether any vault transfer (Stripe self-serve copy, or a processor-to-Stripe PAN import) is in the first release, or v1 is re-authorization only.

### Next question (after confirmation)

Where will the first real SiteStacker exports, and the ministry's matching processor and PayPal reports, come from, and on what terms may Asym hold them? In particular: may Asym hold a ministry's real donor export before that ministry has signed a customer agreement with data-processing terms, and before Phase 29 private custody is built?

_Why next:_ Every amended clause rests on export properties nobody has observed: stable IDs, processor IDs for linking tail money, a shared ID between Historic Giving and Contributions, timestamp zones, and status vocabulary (q01-r1 R1-R20). C23 forbids synthetic source success. The processor-and-automation census, which sizes the PayPal, ACH and platform-Stripe cohorts and the tail, needs a candidate ministry. F2 has the longest lead time and blocks the kit, the rehearsal mode (F4), retention (F11) and money scope (F13). Practice runs put real donor data into Asym, so the custody and agreement terms carry privacy risk (v3-S4).

- (1) A named, willing ministry under a signed customer agreement with data-processing terms. Real exports are accepted only into Phase 29 custody once it is implemented. Until then, use synthetic fixtures labelled as synthetic, which cannot satisfy C23, plus a census questionnaire with no personal data sent now.
- (2) A willing prospect ministry provides real exports before a contract, under a limited evaluation agreement, held in a designated environment with production-equivalent controls agreed with the Phase 29 owner.
- (3) Ask WMTEK for a qualified export or exit package for a willing ministry. Its availability and cost are unverified.
- (4) No real ministry data yet. Build only against redacted samples or screenshots of column headers supplied by a ministry, and defer every 'supported' claim.

_Recommendation:_ Option (1). Start the census questionnaire with no personal data immediately with any candidate ministry, since it needs no donor data. Accept real exports only under signed terms and into Phase 29 custody in an environment approved for real data (D13). Treat a WMTEK package as optional until qualified. This keeps privacy and custody intact (C11, v3-S4) and still lets readiness and processor research advance.

### Unresolved unknowns

- Real SiteStacker export columns, and whether exports carry stable internal IDs, processor charge or transaction IDs, explicit join columns, timestamp time zones and the Contributions transaction ID on Historic Giving rows (q01-r1 R1-R20; no artifact exists).
- Whether SiteStacker's scheduler charges saved Stripe cards for recurring gifts that start today (decided on the server and not visible in client code), and whether SiteStacker can stop individual recurring schedules with evidence, or disable them in bulk.
- What happens when SiteStacker's recurring cron is paused and restarted: whether missed dates are back-charged.
- SiteStacker's full gateway list, what 'STI' is, and per-ministry gateway configuration. The two WMTEK installations checked already run different Stripe code.
- Whether ministries created gateway-native schedules (ARB, Braintree or PaymentSpring plans, Stripe Subscriptions) outside SiteStacker.
- Whether Stripe Account Links can onboard or attach a ministry's existing Stripe account without OAuth (evidence leans no; no official confirmation found). Whether a read_only OAuth connection to a platform-controlled account is possible.
- The identity of SiteStacker/WMTEK's Stripe Connect platform, WMTEK's willingness to share customers for a copy, and whether a WMTEK exit package exists.
- SiteStacker licence terms for a tail mode, and how long a ministry can keep SiteStacker readable.
- Whether SiteStacker's Contribution Receipt fires for every recurring charge under gateways other than PaymentSpring, and whether card-network pre-billing duties apply beyond the PaymentSpring and Mastercard article (the primary Mastercard text was not read).
- Whether self-service profile editing in the SiteStacker donor dashboard can be disabled while cancellation stays available (based only on a marketing claim).
- Authorize.net, iATS, PaymentSpring/Nelnet and Payeezy vault export procedures. Authorize.net limits and fees come only from third-party sources.
- Which object type SiteStacker's SEPA path creates. Legacy Sources cannot be copied by Stripe.
- Whether a willing SiteStacker ministry exists, and the size distribution of target ministries (every volume figure is an assumption).
- How common child sponsorship and multi-organization or fiscal-sponsor merchant accounts are among target ministries.
- Production data status (D13: environments.md versus the founder 'no users' ruling and ADR-0179) and which environment may hold real PII.
- Measured donor attrition for re-authorization campaigns; no reliable benchmark exists.
- Whether CAN-SPAM-style opt-out deadlines bind nonprofit solicitation messages in this context.
- Implementation status of Operator Tenant Grants, the Asym Inngest plan limits and the host function timeout.
- SiteStacker releases 2.53-2.56 (help centre inactive or TLS failure; release notes behind a login).
